---
title: The wizard's bestiary
sidebar: Handbook
showTitle: true
---

The wizard runs on a harness we own, so we get feedback from every run on how our skills and SDKs behave in real repositories. We turn that feedback into the edge cases below, and feed the fixes back into the SDKs, evals, and docs so general agents benefit too.

Below are 756 edge cases the wizard has caught. Each one helped us improve the integration skills.

Think of these as the Wizard's battle trophies!

## Init placement and server rendering

<details>
<summary>Next.js init written from memory in lib file</summary>

**Problem.** Fast or low-effort models write Next.js boilerplate from training data instead of reading the installed reference. They pick an older file convention, guess the env var name and leave out current SDK options, and the build still passes, so nothing flags it.

**Impact.** Silent: init lands in a file nothing imports at app start, reads an env var nobody set, and has no defaults (no history_change pageviews), so capture can be dead while the diff looks complete.

```ts file=Generic agent
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY!, {
  api_host: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST,
})   // no defaults; nothing imports this at startup
export default posthog
```

```ts file=Wizard
// instrumentation-client.ts (Next 15.3+, app and pages router)
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  api_host: '/ingest',
  ui_host: 'https://us.posthog.com',
  defaults: '2026-01-30',
  capture_exceptions: true,
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Browser init inside a component effect</summary>

**Problem.** Older posthog-js docs and most blog posts show a 'use client' PostHogProvider or _app.tsx that calls posthog.init in useEffect, often with a dynamic import to dodge SSR. Agents reproduce it from training data even when instrumentation-client.ts is available.

**Impact.** Init waits for hydration, so the first pageview and captures or flag reads made before the effect runs are dropped or throw. Every caller needs init guards and flags flicker. Same for React SPAs, where init belongs at module scope in main.jsx.

```tsx file=Generic agent
export function PostHogInitializer() {
  useEffect(() => {
    void import('posthog-js').then(({ default: posthog }) => {
      posthog.init(token, { api_host: host })
      window.posthogInitialized = true
      window.dispatchEvent(new Event('posthog_initialized'))
    })
  }, [])
}   // pages call posthog.capture() with no init guard
```

```ts file=Wizard
// instrumentation-client.ts runs before any component renders
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  api_host: '/ingest',
  defaults: '2026-01-30',
})
// components: useFeatureFlagEnabled('flag') works without PostHogProvider
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Nuxt plugin also runs on the server</summary>

**Problem.** plugins/posthog.ts looks client-side, but Nuxt runs unsuffixed plugins in Nitro SSR too. Only the .client filename makes it browser-only.

**Impact.** SSR throws 'window is not defined' or initializes a browser SDK on the server.

```ts file=Generic agent
// plugins/posthog.ts   (runs on server and client)
export default defineNuxtPlugin(() => {
  posthog.init(runtimeConfig.public.posthog.publicKey, {
    tracing_headers: [window.location.hostname],
  })
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
// plugins/posthog.client.ts   (client-only by filename)
export default defineNuxtPlugin((nuxtApp) => {
  const posthogClient = posthog.init(runtimeConfig.public.posthog.publicKey, {
    api_host: runtimeConfig.public.posthog.host,
    tracing_headers: [window.location.hostname],
  })
  return { provide: { posthog: posthogClient as PostHog } }
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Unguarded window access in SSR provider options</summary>

**Problem.** Options like tracing_headers look like plain config, but the root shell component also renders on the server where window does not exist.

**Impact.** SSR throws 'window is not defined' on every server render.

```tsx file=Generic agent
<PostHogProvider apiKey={key} options={{
  api_host: '/ingest',
  tracing_headers: [window.location.hostname],
}}>
```

```tsx file=Wizard
// Guarded for SSR, where `window` is undefined.
tracing_headers:
  typeof window !== 'undefined' ? [window.location.hostname] : [],
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>iOS SDK set up inside a SwiftUI view</summary>

**Problem.** SwiftUI code puts side effects in onAppear, which looks like an app-start hook, but views appear many times and after early events.

**Impact.** Setup runs repeatedly or after the first capture, so early and lifecycle events drop silently and repeated setup can reinitialize the SDK with a different identity state.

```swift file=Generic agent
struct HomeView: View {
    var body: some View {
        Text("Home").onAppear {
            PostHogSDK.shared.setup(PostHogConfig(apiKey: key, host: host))
        }
    }
}
```

```swift file=Wizard
@main
struct BurritoStandApp: App {
    init() {
        let config = PostHogConfig(apiKey: posthogProjectToken, host: posthogHost)
        PostHogSDK.shared.setup(config)   // once, early
    }
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flutter native auto-init left on beside Dart setup</summary>

**Problem.** The Flutter SDK auto-initializes natively from the token in AndroidManifest.xml and Info.plist, and the agent follows that path or adds Dart setup without disabling it. Replay and surveys options are not available through auto-init, and nothing errors.

**Impact.** Double initialization with config split across Dart and native files. The native SDK starts before Dart config applies, so session replay and surveys silently never start.

```xml file=Generic agent
<!-- android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml: native auto-init still on -->
<meta-data android:name="com.posthog.posthog.PROJECT_TOKEN" android:value="phc_xxx"/>
// main.dart: second init path
config.sessionReplay = true;   // ignored by native auto-init
await Posthog().setup(config);
```

```dart file=Wizard
<meta-data android:name="com.posthog.posthog.AUTO_INIT" android:value="false"/>
<!-- ios/Runner/Info.plist and macos/Runner/Info.plist: AUTO_INIT = <false/> -->
// main.dart: one place for all config
WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();
final config = PostHogConfig(const String.fromEnvironment('POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN'));
config.sessionReplay = true;
await Posthog().setup(config); // before runApp()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flutter setup not awaited before runApp</summary>

**Problem.** Fire-and-forget setup after runApp looks harmless in Dart, and the SDK does not complain when early events arrive before it is ready.

**Impact.** Events from the first frames are lost and setup options may not take effect. Silent.

```dart file=Generic agent
void main() {
  runApp(const MyApp());
  Posthog().setup(config); // not awaited, after runApp
}
```

```dart file=Wizard
Future<void> main() async {
  WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();
  final config = PostHogConfig('<token>');
  await Posthog().setup(config);
  runApp(const MyApp());
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>instrumentation-client.ts on Next.js older than 15.3</summary>

**Problem.** Current docs and the skill's own Next.js guidance point at instrumentation-client.ts without a version gate, and the agent does not check the installed next version. Older Next silently ignores the file, so it compiles and builds cleanly.

**Impact.** Silent: the file is never executed, client-side PostHog never initializes and no event ever fires.

```ts file=Generic agent
// package.json: "next": "14.2.5"
// instrumentation-client.ts  (never loaded on Next < 15.3)
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY!, {
  api_host: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST,
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
// wizard harness: src/frameworks/nextjs/nextjs-wizard-agent.ts
detection: { packageName: 'next', minimumVersion: '15.3.0',
  getInstalledVersion: (o) => Promise.resolve(getInstalledPackageVersion('next', o.installDir)) }
// below it the intro screen says "Version 14.2.5 is not supported by the wizard.
// Please upgrade to 15.3.0 or later." with a manual-setup docs link
// skill (posthog-best-practices#Next.js): "Check the installed version before choosing"
// >= 15.3: instrumentation-client.ts
// <  15.3: 'use client' provider in the root layout (app router) or _app (pages router)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>instrumentation-client file gets the wrong extension</summary>

**Problem.** Agents carry component habits (.tsx/.jsx) to a Next.js file convention, and the wizard's own prompt listed the extensions in conflicting order. Next gives no error for a file it does not recognize.

**Impact.** Inconsistent runs: sometimes the init file is not picked up as the instrumentation-client convention and PostHog never initializes, silently.

```ts file=Generic agent
// instrumentation-client.tsx   (or .js in a TS project)
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, { api_host: '/ingest' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
// That prompt (src/nextjs/docs.ts) was later removed; today the shape comes
// from the example apps, which ship instrumentation-client.ts at the project root.
// instrumentation-client.ts
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  api_host: '/ingest', defaults: '2026-01-30',
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Second PostHog init beside the existing one</summary>

**Problem.** Agents add a PostHogProvider or init by reflex without searching for an existing provider or instrumentation-client.ts init, because most React examples pair init with a provider, including for flag hooks. Double init throws no error.

**Impact.** Two browser inits race to stamp $set_once properties like $initial_pathname, corrupting attribution and double-capturing pageviews. One multi-SDK customer saw about 165k blocked merges per day.

**Variants.**

- A second browser provider or init next to instrumentation-client.ts.
- A new client built just for a feature like metrics or AI.
- A migration that swaps an old vendor provider for a second PostHog one.

```tsx file=Generic agent
// instrumentation-client.ts
posthog.init(KEY, { api_host: '/ingest' })
// app/providers.tsx (always mounted)
useEffect(() => { posthog.init(KEY, { api_host: '/ingest' }) }, [])
// app/layout.tsx: <PHProvider>{children}</PHProvider>
// and <PostHogProvider> added again in the providers list
```

```tsx file=Wizard
// instrumentation-client.ts: the one browser init
//IMPORTANT: Never combine this approach with other client-side PostHog
// initialization approaches, especially components like a PostHogProvider.
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, { api_host: '/ingest' })

// components: hooks use the singleton, no provider needed
const on = useFeatureFlagEnabled('promo')
// audit init-not-duplicated: warning on 2+ browser inits, error if confirmed on one page
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PostHog script tags cause hydration errors</summary>

**Problem.** Agents copy a minimal posthog.init without the defaults date, unaware that older defaults append lazy-loaded scripts into body, where they interleave with the router's server-rendered DOM.

**Impact.** Hydration mismatch errors in SSR React apps. The app still renders, so it looks like an unrelated framework bug.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, { api_host: host })   // no defaults: scripts appended to <body>
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(import.meta.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, {
  api_host: import.meta.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST,
  defaults: '2026-01-30',   // implies external_scripts_inject_target: 'head'
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>SvelteKit init runs during server render</summary>

**Problem.** +layout.ts and the module script of +layout.svelte look like client app-root code, but SvelteKit executes them on the server too, and posthog-js is browser-only.

**Impact.** Unguarded init or identify runs during SSR (no-op or error), a universal load can re-run on navigation, and events from the server pass are lost. Silent.

```ts file=Generic agent
// src/routes/+layout.ts  (also runs on the server)
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY, { api_host: PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST })
// same problem in +layout.svelte <script>: module scope runs during SSR
```

```ts file=Wizard
// src/hooks.client.ts: runs once, in the browser, at app start
export async function init() {
  posthog.init(PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, {
    api_host: '/ingest', ui_host: 'https://us.posthog.com',
    defaults: '2026-01-30', capture_exceptions: true,
  })
}
// src/lib/auth.svelte.ts
if (browser) { posthog.identify(username, { username }) }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Angular init runs on the server render</summary>

**Problem.** Constructors, ngOnInit and app config also execute during Angular SSR, and agents call posthog-js there directly or import it in components instead of one DI service with a platform check.

**Impact.** Server render throws on missing window/document or breaks hydration, server-side calls hit an uninitialized client, and init is scattered across components.

```ts file=Generic agent
export class AppComponent implements OnInit {
  ngOnInit() {
    // also executes during the server render
    posthog.init(environment.posthogKey, { api_host: '/ingest' })
  }
}
// other components import posthog-js and call capture directly
```

```ts file=Wizard
@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })
export class PostHogService {
  init(apiKey: string, options: Partial<PostHogConfig>) {
    if (isPlatformBrowser(this.platformId) && !this.initialized) {
      posthog.init(apiKey, options); this.initialized = true
    }
  }
  get posthog() { /* real client in browser, no-op Proxy on the server */ }
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Nuxt components assume a PostHog client exists</summary>

**Problem.** Agents import posthog-js directly in a component or call usePostHog().capture() without a guard, not realising there is no client during server render or when the token is unset.

**Impact.** Unguarded calls throw during SSR or in event handlers, breaking the page for the sake of analytics.

```vue file=Generic agent
<script setup>
import posthog from 'posthog-js'   // browser-only, also runs during SSR
posthog.capture('burrito_considered')
</script>
```

```ts file=Wizard
const posthog = usePostHog()
posthog?.capture('burrito_considered', {
  total_considerations: response.user.burritoConsiderations,
})
posthog?.captureException(err)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>One init pattern applied to every rendering mode</summary>

**Problem.** Agents treat a framework as one install shape, but Astro static/SSR/hybrid/view-transitions, Nuxt 3.0-3.6 vs 3.7+/4, and TanStack file- vs code-based routing each need a different init. A plain Astro script tag is bundled and type-checked, so the snippet throws TypeScript errors.

**Impact.** Init breaks at build time or never runs for the project's actual mode or version.

```astro file=Generic agent
<!-- Layout.astro: Astro processes this script -->
<script>
  import posthog from 'posthog-js'
  posthog.init(import.meta.env.PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, { api_host })
</script>
```

```astro file=Wizard
<!-- src/components/posthog.astro, imported in the Layout -->
<script is:inline define:vars={{ apiKey: import.meta.env.PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, apiHost: import.meta.env.PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST }}>
  /* browser snippet */
  posthog.init(apiKey || '', { api_host: apiHost || 'https://us.i.posthog.com', defaults: '2026-01-30' })
</script>
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Next.js App Router and Pages Router mixed up</summary>

**Problem.** Most current examples assume the App Router, so an agent applies app/providers.tsx and layout wiring to a Pages Router project.

**Impact.** The provider is created but nothing mounts it, so PostHog never initializes and nothing errors.

```tsx file=Generic agent
// Pages Router project (pages/_app.tsx exists), App Router recipe applied
// app/providers.tsx
'use client'
export function PHProvider({ children }) {
  return <PostHogProvider client={posthog}>{children}</PostHogProvider>
} // nothing mounts it
```

```ts file=Wizard
// getNextJsRouter: pages/_app.* XOR app/**/layout.* decides;
// both or neither -> null -> ask the user
setup.questions = [{ key: 'router', message: 'Which Next.js router are you using?',
  options: [App Router, Pages Router], detect: getNextJsRouter }]
// getAdditionalContextLines -> ['Router: pages']
// context-mill ships variants nextjs-app-router / nextjs-pages-router
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Server SDK client built in every request</summary>

**Problem.** Constructing the client next to the capture call is the shortest diff and works in a quick test. The leak and dropped events only show under real traffic or on exit.

**Impact.** A client with its own queue and timers leaks per request, and buffered events are lost when the request ends. Nothing errors.

```ts file=Generic agent
// app/api/checkout/route.ts  (or src/pages/api/signup.ts in Astro SSR)
export async function POST(req: Request) {
  const posthog = new PostHog(process.env.POSTHOG_KEY!)   // new client every request
  posthog.capture({ distinctId, event: 'checkout_completed' })
  return Response.json({ ok: true })
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
// utils/posthog-server.ts: module singleton reused by every handler
let client: PostHog | null = null
export function getPostHogClient() {
  if (!client) client = new PostHog(process.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
    host: process.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST, flushAt: 1, flushInterval: 0,
  })
  return client
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Django client created outside AppConfig.ready</summary>

**Problem.** Configuring PostHog at the top of settings.py or views.py, or creating a client inside a view, is the obvious Python move. Django's autoreloader and lack of a shutdown hook hide the cost, and agents forget the signals module only registers if something imports it.

**Impact.** Duplicate clients and consumer threads per request, import or autoreload, import-order problems at startup, no single shutdown to flush, and the user_logged_in receiver is never registered, so login events are lost or personless.

```python file=Generic agent
# settings.py, import time
import posthog
posthog.api_key = os.environ['POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN']
# views.py
def create_order(request):
    client = Posthog(settings.POSTHOG_API_KEY, host=settings.POSTHOG_HOST)
    client.capture("order_created", distinct_id=str(request.user.pk))
# core/signals.py is never imported, so user_logged_in has no receiver
```

```python file=Wizard
class CoreConfig(AppConfig):
    def ready(self):
        import posthog   # inside to avoid startup import issues
        posthog.api_key = settings.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN
        posthog.host = settings.POSTHOG_HOST
        if settings.POSTHOG_DISABLED:
            posthog.disabled = True
        from . import signals  # noqa: F401  registers the login receiver
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Full-stack app gets only the browser SDK</summary>

**Problem.** The agent stops after one working SDK, usually the browser one, without noticing the app has a server runtime with its own flows and errors (or the reverse on server-first stacks like Rails).

**Impact.** Server-side events, exceptions and server-only flows are never captured. No error shown.

```text file=Generic agent
// Outline (React + Koa, TS)
// client: posthog.init(...) in the app entry
// server: no posthog-node anywhere, no reverse proxy
```

```text file=Wizard
INITIALIZE it at the framework's entry point for every runtime the integration targets
(typically both client and server) - BEFORE adding any event capture.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Logs setup replaces the AI tracing provider</summary>

**Problem.** Each product's setup is written in isolation and creates its own OTel SDK or provider in the shared init file, unaware another step already registered a span processor there.

**Impact.** AI Observability's span processor is dropped or the init files conflict, and traces silently stop arriving.

```ts file=Generic agent
// instrumentation.ts: AIO step registered PostHogSpanProcessor on the provider
// logs step then adds, in the same file:
const sdk = new NodeSDK({ logRecordProcessors: [/* PostHog logs */] })
sdk.start()   // replaces the provider AIO configured
```

```text file=Wizard
queue: capture -> ai-observability -> logs -> review (sequential, same entry/init files)
logs.md: "Preserve any tracing provider AIO configured in the previous step."
logs.md: name packages in the handoff; "leave installation, manifest and lockfile updates, builds, and lint to review"
```

</details>

## Environment config and tokens

<details>
<summary>Key in .env.example treated as configured</summary>

**Problem.** A grep for the key finds it in the committed template, so the agent concludes it is set, or writes the real secret into that template because it looks like an env file. Adding a tracked file to .gitignore afterwards changes nothing.

**Impact.** Credential setup is skipped and the app has no key, or the real secret is committed and published with the repository.

```js file=Generic agent
# .env.example (committed)
OPENAI_API_KEY=
# agent: 'key already present' -> skips collecting it
# or writes the real secret into the template:
set_env_values({ filePath: '.env.example', values: { OPENAI_API_KEY: 'sk-live-...' } })
```

```js file=Wizard
check_env_keys -> { "OPENAI_API_KEY": { "status": "missing", "foundIn": [".env.example"] } }
// "present" only if a non-template env file sets it
set_env_values({ filePath: '.env.example', ... })
-> Error: "...a credential written there would be published with the repository.
   Write to .env or .env.local"
// refuses .env.example / .sample / .template / .dist
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Env file written to a duplicated subdirectory</summary>

**Problem.** In a monorepo the agent passes a repo-root-relative path while the wizard already runs inside the subproject, and a mkdir -p style writer happily creates the missing directories.

**Impact.** A stray apps/web/apps/web/.env appears and the real app never sees the token. The write itself reports success.

```js file=Generic agent
// wizard cwd is <repo>/apps/web
set_env_values({ filePath: 'apps/web/.env', values: { NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN: '...' } })
// -> creates <repo>/apps/web/apps/web/.env
```

```text file=Wizard
Error: parent directory does not exist: "apps/web". filePath is resolved against
the wizard working directory: pass ".env" for a file there, or
"<subproject>/.env" for an existing nested project.
// resolveEnvPath also rejects paths outside the working directory
// then ensureGitignoreCoverage(workingDirectory, '.env')
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Too much guard code around a simple bootstrap</summary>

**Problem.** Once the fail-loudly config rule existed, agents applied it everywhere. They wrapped a few-line docs bootstrap in an init function, a module global and a custom raise, even where a direct os.environ lookup already fails loudly.

**Impact.** Needless restructuring, extra failure surface and diff noise in the user's LLM client setup that drifts from the documented pattern.

```python file=Generic agent
_posthog = None
def init_posthog():
    global _posthog
    key = os.environ.get('POSTHOG_API_KEY')
    if not key:
        raise RuntimeError('POSTHOG_API_KEY is not set')
    _posthog = Posthog(key, host=os.environ['POSTHOG_HOST'])
```

```python file=Wizard
# module level, next to the existing vendor client
posthog = Posthog(os.environ['POSTHOG_API_KEY'], host=os.environ['POSTHOG_HOST'])
client = OpenAI(posthog_client=posthog)
# unset key -> KeyError, already the loud failure. No guard branch.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>A .env directory crashes the key lookup</summary>

**Problem.** existsSync returns true for directories, and Python virtualenvs are often named .env, so 'exists then read' throws.

**Impact.** Uncaught EISDIR aborts the API-key and env-key lookup before any integration work.

```ts file=Generic agent
const envPath = path.join(process.cwd(), envFile);
if (fs.existsSync(envPath)) {                      // true for a directory too
  const content = fs.readFileSync(envPath, 'utf8'); // EISDIR
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
// null when missing, oversized or a directory (a Python virtualenv)
const content = readProjectFile(path.join(process.cwd(), envFile));
const match = content?.match(/^POSTHOG_PERSONAL_API_KEY=(.+)$/m);
if (match) return match[1].trim();
// check_env_keys on a directory reports 'missing' instead of throwing
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>OAuth silently picks the wrong project</summary>

**Problem.** Taking the first granted team from the token is the simple path, and drops the --project-id the caller asked for.

**Impact.** The wrong project's token is written and events are silently captured into it.

```ts file=Generic agent
const projectId = tokenResponse.scoped_teams?.[0]; // whatever was ticked on consent
```

```ts file=Wizard
const resolution = resolveGrantedProject(options.projectId, tokenResponse.scoped_teams);
if (!resolution.ok) abort(`You authorized project ${resolution.granted}, but setup is targeting project ${resolution.requested} (from --project-id).`);
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>First project's token used without asking</summary>

**Problem.** The MCP projects-get call returns a list and agents take element zero. Nothing shows the org has Production and Staging.

**Impact.** Events land in the wrong PostHog project.

```text file=Generic agent
projects-get -> [{ name: 'Production', api_token: 'phc_a...' },
                 { name: 'Staging', api_token: 'phc_b...' }]
// agent writes projects[0].api_token to .env without asking
```

```markdown file=Wizard
If multiple projects are returned, ask the user which project to use.
If the MCP server is not connected or not authenticated,
ask the user for their PostHog project token instead.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>.env.example added to .gitignore</summary>

**Problem.** An over-cautious agent protecting secrets ignores every .env\* file, including the example file that holds no secrets.

**Impact.** The env-var documentation the step just wrote is hidden from version control, so the next developer never sees it.

```diff file=Generic agent
# .gitignore
+.env
+.env.example
```

```diff file=Wizard
# .gitignore
+.env
# .env.example stays committed: it is documentation
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Env key check looks at one file only</summary>

**Problem.** Checking ./.env is the obvious lookup, but real projects keep keys in .env.local or in nested apps/\*/.env.local. The detector's evidence string also always said ".env", so the agent trusted the wrong file and then took 'missing' as a reason to stop instead of asking the user.

**Impact.** A key that is set reads as missing, so the agent gives up and hands the user a browser link. A source it could have connected never gets created. Of 697 completed warehouse tasks, only 247 created a source.

```js file=Generic agent
check_env_keys({ filePath: '.env', keys: ['OPENAI_API_KEY'] })  -> missing
// key really in apps/api/.env.local
matchedSignal: "found `OPENAI_API_KEY` in .env"   // always .env, even when no root .env exists
// agent: 'key missing, giving the user a browser link'
```

```js file=Wizard
check_env_keys({ keys: ['OPENAI_API_KEY'] })  // no filePath: scans every .env* up to 3 levels, same walk as the detector
-> { OPENAI_API_KEY: { status: 'present', foundIn: ['apps/api/.env.local'] } }
// reads `export KEY=` lines too; names and paths only, never values
// a key only in .env.example is 'missing' (foundIn lists it); templates are never a write target
matchedSignal: "found `OPENAI_API_KEY` in apps/api/.env.local"
// 'missing' is not a reason to stop: go on and wizard_ask the user
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Missing PostHog config crashes the app</summary>

**Problem.** Reading the token as a required setting (os.environ\[...\], a required pydantic field, expect(), a non-null assertion, a required Spring property) is the idiomatic way to load real config, and the docs never said analytics must degrade to a no-op. With the same docs on the same day, half the runs crashed and half guarded.

**Impact.** Fresh clones, CI, review apps, e2e runs and any deploy without PostHog env crash at import, boot or build, or 500 on every request.

```python file=Generic agent
POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN = os.environ["POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"]   # KeyError when unset

class Settings(BaseSettings):
    posthog_project_token: str   # required: app will not boot

// Spring: @Value("${posthog.project-token}") String token   -> context fails to start
// Rust: std::env::var("POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN").expect("must be set")   -> panics at boot
```

```python file=Wizard
POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN = os.getenv("POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN")
POSTHOG_HOST = os.getenv("POSTHOG_HOST")

def ready(self) -> None:
    if not settings.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN or not settings.POSTHOG_HOST:
        if settings.DEBUG:
            raise RuntimeError("POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN variable required by PostHog is missing ...")
        return  # production: no-op
# Spring: @Value("${posthog.project-token:}") + log.warn when blank
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Missing token skipped silently with no dev error</summary>

**Problem.** Once told not to crash, the agent over-corrects to `if (key) init()` or an empty-string init. That is quiet everywhere, so a developer never learns why no events arrive.

**Impact.** Silent. No events and no error, and the gap only shows when dashboards stay empty.

```ts file=Generic agent
const key = import.meta.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY
if (key) {
  posthog.init(key, { api_host: import.meta.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST })
}
// key missing: no log, no error, no events

# Rails
PostHog.init { |c| c.api_key = api_key } if api_key.present?  # silently skipped
```

```ts file=Wizard
if (posthogKey && posthogHost) {
  posthog.init(posthogKey, { api_host: posthogHost, defaults: '2026-01-30' })
} else if (import.meta.env.DEV) {
  throw new Error(
    `${missing} variable required by PostHog is missing or un-configured, this causes events to be silently missed. This error stops appearing once ${missing} is configured`
  )
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Empty or placeholder token used as fallback</summary>

**Problem.** Defaulting to '' or 'phc_placeholder' is the usual way to keep code compiling, satisfy a non-optional type and stop init from throwing, and the example apps did it too. Nothing fails: the SDK accepts the dead key and logs nothing.

**Impact.** Silent. Init runs with an empty or fake key, the build passes and no events arrive.

```kotlin file=Generic agent
val token = System.getenv("POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN") ?: ""
PostHogAndroid.setup(this, PostHogAndroidConfig(token))

// Astro example app
posthog.init(apiKey || '', { api_host: apiHost || 'https://us.i.posthog.com' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
if (!apiKey || !apiHost) {
  if (mode === 'development') {
    throw new Error(`${missingVariable} variable required by PostHog is missing or un-configured, this causes events to be silently missed. This error stops appearing once ${missingVariable} is configured`);
  }
} else {
  posthog.init(apiKey, { api_host: apiHost, defaults: '2026-01-30' });
}
// where a build has no env (mobile release), embed the real public token instead
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Mobile token read from Xcode scheme env</summary>

**Problem.** The generic rule says 'use env vars, never hardcode', and the skill used to show a fatalError pattern. Xcode scheme env vars only exist when you run from Xcode, and release builds have no environment, so every local run passes and the shipped build breaks.

**Impact.** Archive, TestFlight and App Store builds crash on launch at the fatalError, or ship a nil or empty token that silently sends nothing.

```swift file=Generic agent
enum PostHogEnv {
  static let token = ProcessInfo.processInfo.environment["POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"]
    ?? { fatalError("Set POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN in the Xcode scheme") }()
}
PostHogSDK.shared.setup(PostHogConfig(apiKey: PostHogEnv.token, host: PostHogEnv.host))
// or: environment["POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"] ?? ""   -> archive build ships ""
// Android: PostHogAndroidConfig(System.getenv("POSTHOG_TOKEN") ?: "")
```

```swift file=Wizard
// release/archive builds have no environment: ship the real public token
private let posthogProjectToken = "phc_<real project token>"   // public client key, safe to ship
private let posthogHost = "https://us.i.posthog.com"
let config = PostHogConfig(apiKey: posthogProjectToken, host: posthogHost)
PostHogSDK.shared.setup(config)
// scheme env var only as an optional override; never fatalError or force-unwrap
// Flutter: String.fromEnvironment('POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN') (--dart-define), disable if empty
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flutter web snippet ships a placeholder token</summary>

**Problem.** The agent copies the docs snippet's phc_your_project_token_here, or obeys 'never hardcode' by reading a window global that nothing defines. Static HTML cannot read env, and the public token is not a secret.

**Impact.** Silent. The web build passes, PostHog starts with a fake or undefined token, and nothing is captured.

```html file=Generic agent
<!-- web/index.html -->
posthog.init('phc_your_project_token_here', {
  api_host: 'https://us.i.posthog.com',
})
<!-- or: posthog.init(window.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, ...)  a 'later build step' never defines it -->
```

```html file=Wizard
<!-- web/index.html: HTML cannot read env, and the token is public -->
posthog.init('phc_<the configured project token>', {
  api_host: 'https://us.i.posthog.com',
  defaults: '2025-05-24',
})
<!-- phc_your_project_token_here is a placeholder for readers, not code to copy -->
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Project token pasted into init code</summary>

**Problem.** The prompt hands the agent a token and host, and quickstart snippets show literals, so pasting them inline is the shortest path that works. The mobile and static-web carve-outs for the public token make the rule look optional.

**Impact.** The token is committed to source, cannot change per environment, and dev and staging traffic mix with production. A host literal is wrong for other regions or proxies, and a guessed key sends to no project. The wizard scanner blocks the edit and the agent burns time retrying. Same for Angular environment files.

```ts file=Generic agent
// lib/posthog.ts
posthog.init('phc_Zk3Qreal0token', {
  api_host: 'https://us.i.posthog.com',
})

# Python server
posthog = Posthog('phc_xxxxxxxxxxxx', host='https://us.i.posthog.com')
```

```ts file=Wizard
check_env_keys({ keys: ['NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN', 'NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST'] })
set_env_values({ filePath: '.env.local', values: { NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN: '<token>', NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST: '<host>' } })
// instrumentation-client.ts
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  api_host: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST,
})
// runtime note: a literal PostHog URL or token in source is blocked (YARA posthog_hardcoded_key)
// exception: mobile release builds and static web snippets embed the public token
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Agent edits .env files with its own tools</summary>

**Problem.** Read then Write, sed and echo &gt;&gt; are how a generic agent edits any file, so it re-types every line of .env.local to add one key. LLMs are poor at copying long random strings exactly, and the damage is one character in an unrelated secret.

**Impact.** Silent. Existing secrets are clobbered or changed and found hours later in another service. Plain reads also pull real secrets into model context and the gateway.

```text file=Generic agent
Read  .env.local   # DATABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY, AWS_* now in model context
Write { file_path: ".env.local", content: <whole file re-typed> }
# SUPABASE_KEY comes back 1 character different
$ sed -i 's/KEY=.*/KEY=phc_.../' .env.local   # same risk from the shell
```

```js file=Wizard
check_env_keys({ keys: ['NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN'] })   // names only, never values
set_env_values({ filePath: '.env.local', values: {
  NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN: '<token>',
  NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST: 'https://us.i.posthog.com' } })
// mergeEnvValues touches only matching lines
// harness denies Read/Write/Edit (and Grep targeting) any .env* basename; .env.example/.sample/.template/.dist stay writable
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Existing env key assumed to be correct</summary>

**Problem.** Seeing the key name already in .env, or check_env_keys returning present, looks like the job is done, and skipping the write feels like respecting existing config.

**Impact.** Silent. A stale or placeholder token or host stays in place, so events go to the wrong project or region, or an invalid token disables analytics.

```bash file=Generic agent
grep -q POSTHOG .env.local && echo "already configured, skipping"
# or: check_env_keys -> NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN: present -> skip write
# file still holds phc_old_or_placeholder
```

```js file=Wizard
// Don't assume the presence of keys means the value is up to date.
// Write the correct value each time.
set_env_values({ filePath: '.env.local', values: {
  NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN: '<current project token>',
  NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST: '<host>' } })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Browser code reads an unexposed env var</summary>

**Problem.** The agent names variables from memory (POSTHOG_KEY, REACT_APP_\*) instead of following the bundler's public-prefix rule. Server-side reads work, and the wizard's own react-router config still returns REACT_APP_ names for a Vite-based router, so nothing looks wrong until the browser runs.

**Impact.** Silent. The bundler strips the variable or there is no process.env in the browser, the token is undefined, and init is skipped, sends nothing or throws. Same for Astro, which exposes only PUBLIC_ variables and needs define:vars in inline scripts.

```ts file=Generic agent
// lib/posthog.ts (Next.js), name from model memory
posthog.init(process.env.POSTHOG_KEY!, {
  api_host: process.env.POSTHOG_HOST,
})
// Vite app with CRA habits: process.env.REACT_APP_POSTHOG_KEY  (no process.env in the browser)
// Vue/Vite: import.meta.env.POSTHOG_KEY   (Vite only exposes VITE_*)
```

```ts file=Wizard
environment: {
  getEnvVars: (apiKey, host) => ({
    NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN: apiKey,
    NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST: host,
  }),
}
// detectEnvVarPrefix(): next -> NEXT_PUBLIC_, react-scripts -> REACT_APP_, vite/TanStack -> VITE_PUBLIC_, SvelteKit -> PUBLIC_
// Nuxt: NUXT_PUBLIC_*   Vue: VITE_*   Angular (ngx-env): NG_APP_*
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Angular reads env vars the CLI never exposes</summary>

**Problem.** The agent writes import.meta.env\['NG_APP_...'\] or process.env as it would in Vite. The stock Angular builder loads no .env at all, NG_APP_ only works with @ngx-env/builder installed, and only the example app's angular.json carried that fix.

**Impact.** Silent or a build error. The token is always undefined at runtime: in production the guard returns quietly and PostHog never sends events. In dev the guard throws and the page is blank.

```ts file=Generic agent
// stock @angular/build:application builder loads no .env
export const environment = {
  posthogKey: import.meta.env['NG_APP_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN'],  // undefined
  posthogHost: import.meta.env['NG_APP_POSTHOG_HOST'],
};
// or: posthog.init(process.env['POSTHOG_KEY']!, { api_host: '/ingest' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
// Open the thing you read from and see the key defined, or don't read from it.
// Option A: angular.json "builder": "@ngx-env/builder:application" (+ :dev-server, :karma), src/env.d.ts
// Option B: src/environments/environment.ts (the public token ships in the bundle anyway)
export const environment = {
  posthogKey: 'phc_REAL_PROJECT_TOKEN',
  posthogHost: 'https://us.i.posthog.com',
};
// service: posthog.init(environment.posthogKey, { api_host: environment.posthogHost })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>React Native reads token from process.env</summary>

**Problem.** process.env works in Node and web bundlers, so the agent assumes Metro injects .env too. Bare React Native embeds env at build time through react-native-config plus native Gradle wiring, and Expo uses expo-constants extras, which agents mix up.

**Impact.** Silent. The token is undefined on device, PostHog never starts, and nothing crashes. Edits to .env need a native rebuild.

```ts file=Generic agent
const token = process.env.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN   // undefined in a bare RN/Expo bundle
export const posthog = new PostHog(token ?? '', {
  host: process.env.POSTHOG_HOST,
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
// bare RN: embedded at build time
import Config from 'react-native-config'
const projectToken = Config.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN
// android/app/build.gradle
apply from: project(':react-native-config').projectDir.getPath() + "/dotenv.gradle"
// Expo (NOT react-native-config): app.config.js extra.posthogProjectToken
import Constants from 'expo-constants'
const token = Constants.expoConfig?.extra?.posthogProjectToken
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Token stored under legacy API key name</summary>

**Problem.** Older docs, training data, the SDK parameter apiKey and some docs pages call the public phc_token an 'API key' (POSTHOG_KEY, POSTHOG_API_KEY, POSTHOG_PROJECT_API_KEY), so the agent copies those names. Banning the name outright over-corrects, because renaming a project that already reads it breaks deploys.

**Impact.** Silent. Code reads a name the wizard, hosting upload or CI never sets, so the app starts with an empty token. Renaming a working project leaves every deploy step and CI secret that passes the old name starting the app with an empty token. People also confuse the token with the phx_ personal API key.

```js file=Generic agent
# fresh Next.js project
set_env_values({ filePath: '.env.local', values: { POSTHOG_KEY: 'phc_...' } })
posthog.init(process.env.POSTHOG_KEY)   // nothing else reads this name

POSTHOG_API_KEY=phc_your_api_key_here
$apiKey = getenv('POSTHOG_API_KEY');
# other drift: NUXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY, VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY, POSTHOG_PROJECT_API_KEY
```

```text file=Wizard
set_env_values({ values: { POSTHOG_KEY: '...' } })
-> Error: "POSTHOG_KEY" is not a valid PostHog env var name. Use the key name from
   your framework's integration guide (e.g. NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN).
   POSTHOG_KEY is accepted only when the project already reads it.

POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN=phc_your_project_token_here
NUXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN=phc_...
# scripts/lint-env-naming.sh flags POSTHOG_API_KEY and _POSTHOG_KEY in examples
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>New env keys missing from .env.example</summary>

**Problem.** The agent writes only the gitignored local file, because that is enough to make its own run work. Out of caution it sometimes adds .env.example or .env\* to .gitignore, hiding the one committed file that documents the keys.

**Impact.** Teammates, CI, fresh worktrees and deploy environments do not know which PostHog variables to set, so the integration runs silently unconfigured or the SDK warns.

```bash file=Generic agent
# .env.local (gitignored) gets:
NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN=phc_...
NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST=https://us.i.posthog.com
# .env.example and bootstrap scripts: unchanged

# .gitignore (agent edit)
.env.example   # or .env*  -> hides the documentation
```

```bash file=Wizard
# .env.example (committed): real names, placeholder or empty values
NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN=
NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST=
# .gitignore: ignore only .env, never .env.example
.env
# report checklist (only if env vars were added): the exact names are in
# .env.example, monorepo/bootstrap scripts and deploy envs
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Node start script exits when .env is missing</summary>

**Problem.** Plain Node does not load .env, so the agent adds node --env-file=.env to the start script as a clean, dependency-free fix. It works locally, but .env is gitignored and absent where hosts inject variables.

**Impact.** Deployed apps exit with code 9 before any app code runs (node: .env: not found), even though the host injects every variable.

```json file=Generic agent
"scripts": {
  "start": "node --env-file=.env dist/index.js",
  "dev": "node --env-file=.env --watch index.js"
}
// $ npm start  ->  node: .env: not found (exit 9)
```

```text file=Wizard
"start": "node dist/index.js"   // start/dev left untouched, nothing installed
// report opens with:
// > ⚠️ **Needs your attention**
// > - Load `.env` in the app, or set `POSTHOG_API_KEY` in its environment.
// a loader already in the project (dotenv import, env-file flag) is left alone;
// dotenv's config() is a no-op when .env is missing
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Token written to .env that nothing loads</summary>

**Problem.** Next.js and Vite load .env automatically, so the agent assumes Express, Fastify, Hono, plain Ruby, go run, Spring or compiled binaries do too. The build is green, so nothing flags it.

**Impact.** The missing-variable guard throws at module load against an env file nothing reads, or the token is nil and the client silently sends nothing.

```ts file=Generic agent
// server.ts (Express), no dotenv import; "start": "node dist/server.js"
const key = process.env.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN
if (!key) throw new Error('POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN required by PostHog is missing')

# todo.rb (plain Ruby): .env is never loaded
posthog = PostHog::Client.new(api_key: ENV['POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN'])
```

```text file=Wizard
# Ruby: Gemfile gem 'dotenv', '~> 3.0' (Rails: 'dotenv-rails'); todo.rb: require 'dotenv/load'
# Go/Java/Rust/Elixir READMEs: copy .env.example to .env, then export the vars before run
# Node (error-tracking flow): never wire a loader or edit start/dev
#   (--env-file=.env exits 9 where .env is missing); name the gap in the handoff
#   with the variable names: load .env with dotenv, or set real environment variables
# Node app that already loads .env (dotenv import / env-file flag): leave it alone
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Gradle build crashes when .env is missing</summary>

**Problem.** The agent loads .env into Properties in build.gradle.kts to fill BuildConfig fields and does not check that the file exists. That file is always there on the author's machine.

**Impact.** Gradle sync and build throw FileNotFoundException on fresh clones and in CI, so missing PostHog config breaks the whole build.

```kotlin file=Generic agent
val env = Properties().apply {
    rootProject.file(".env").inputStream().use { load(it) }   // throws if .env is absent
}
buildConfigField("String", "POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN", "\"${env["POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"]}\"")
```

```kotlin file=Wizard
val posthogProperties = Properties().apply {
    val envFile = project.file(".env")
    if (envFile.exists()) { envFile.inputStream().use(::load) }
}
fun posthogConfig(key: String): String =
    providers.environmentVariable(key).orNull ?: posthogProperties.getProperty(key).orEmpty()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Dev and staging events land in production</summary>

**Problem.** The agent initializes PostHog with no condition and the production token, so localhost, preview and staging all send events. Nothing fails.

**Impact.** Event volume goes up, funnels and dashboards are polluted, and the bill rises. You only find it by breaking down $current_url.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init('phc_prod_xxx', { api_host: '/ingest' })  // runs on localhost, previews, staging
```

```ts file=Wizard
if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') {
  posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, { api_host: '/ingest' })
}
// or a separate project token for dev/staging (audit recommendation, not an integration rule)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PostHog starts during tests and CI</summary>

**Problem.** Test runners import the same app entry, and the agent does not think about them. The cost is quota, not a failure.

**Impact.** Every test run fires /flags requests and records synthetic replay sessions, which burns quota without anyone noticing. Same for Elixir, where config/test.exs sets test_mode: true.

```ts file=Generic agent
// app entry, also imported by vitest and Playwright
posthog.init(key, { api_host: '/ingest' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
if (process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'test' && !process.env.CI) {
  posthog.init(key, opts)
}
// or in test/CI: advanced_disable_feature_flags: true, disable_session_recording: true
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Elixir reads env in config.exs, not runtime.exs</summary>

**Problem.** config/config.exs is the familiar place for app config, and System.get_env there works in dev. It runs at compile time, so releases bake in the build machine's value, and Phoenix does not load .env on its own.

**Impact.** Silent. A release ships with nil or the wrong token, so the SDK is disabled or events go nowhere.

```elixir file=Generic agent
# config/config.exs  (evaluated at compile time)
config :posthog,
  api_key: System.get_env("POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"),
  api_host: System.get_env("POSTHOG_HOST")
```

```elixir file=Wizard
# config/runtime.exs  (runs at boot)
config :posthog,
  api_key: System.get_env("POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"),
  api_host: System.get_env("POSTHOG_HOST") || "https://us.i.posthog.com"
# config/config.exs keeps only: enable: true, in_app_otp_apps: [:my_app]
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Placeholder from .env.example accepted as real token</summary>

**Problem.** Copying .env.example to .env is the normal setup step, and a truthiness check passes for phc_your_project_token_here. The agent guards against unset, not against the template value.

**Impact.** Events go to an invalid token and ingestion rejects them while the app shows no error.

```php file=Generic agent
$projectToken = getenv('POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN'); // 'phc_your_project_token_here'
PostHog::init($projectToken, ['host' => $host]);
```

```php file=Wizard
$projectToken = getenv('POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN');
if (!$projectToken || str_starts_with($projectToken, 'phc_your_')) {
    echo "WARNING: PostHog not configured (POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN not set)\n";
    return false;
}
// React Native: disabled: !isPostHogConfigured, where
// isPostHogConfigured = projectToken && projectToken !== 'phc_your_project_token_here'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Env write duplicates an export KEY line</summary>

**Problem.** A naive updater matches ^KEY= and appends when no match is found, missing lines written as `export KEY=...`. Keys are also put into regexes unescaped.

**Impact.** Two declarations of one key. The winner depends on the dotenv loader, so the app can keep the stale or wrong token.

```bash file=Generic agent
# before
export POSTHOG_HOST=https://eu.i.posthog.com
# after an append-only write
export POSTHOG_HOST=https://eu.i.posthog.com
POSTHOG_HOST=https://us.i.posthog.com
```

```ts file=Wizard
const regex = new RegExp(
  `^(\\s*(?:export\\s+)?${escapeRegExp(key)}\\s*=).*$`, 'm');
result = result.replace(regex, `$1${value}`); // keeps `export `, one declaration
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Existing init reads a variable nobody defined</summary>

**Problem.** An init call plus the SDK in the manifest looks like a finished integration, so the agent leaves it alone without checking the variable exists.

**Impact.** The client is built from an empty string. The app starts, every capture returns, and no event ever arrives.

```js file=Generic agent
// init found, package listed -> "already integrated", skip
posthog.init(process.env.POSTHOG_KEY ?? '', { api_host })
// .env has no POSTHOG_KEY
```

```js file=Wizard
// check_env_keys for the EXACT name the init reads
// present -> leave alone and say so
// absent  -> set_env_values({ POSTHOG_KEY: ... }) under the name the code
//            already reads (never rename the code), and document it in .env.example
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Deploy step does not pass the token</summary>

**Problem.** The agent matches names between code and .env but never opens docker run, compose, systemd or PaaS settings. Those start steps predate PostHog and either pass an old name or none at all, even when the agent adds the new release ID there.

**Impact.** Silent. In production the init guard returns quietly. The process boots, serves traffic and reports nothing.

```bash file=Generic agent
# pre-existing deploy step, app source reads POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN
docker run -e POSTHOG_KEY=$KEY app

# or the start step passes the release ID but not the token and host
docker run -d \
  -e DATABASE_URL="$DATABASE_URL" \
  -e POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID="$ID" \
  app:latest
```

```bash file=Wizard
# pass the names the app's source reads, same mechanism as its other variables
docker run -d -e DATABASE_URL="$DATABASE_URL" \
  -e POSTHOG_API_KEY="$POSTHOG_API_KEY" -e POSTHOG_HOST="$POSTHOG_HOST" \
  -e POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID="$ID" app:latest
# change the deploy step, not the code; name any new secret in the handoff
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Env vars set locally, missing in production</summary>

**Problem.** Agents write .env.local and stop. Local dev works so nothing shows the production deploy has no key.

**Impact.** Vercel production builds have no token, so init is skipped and production events are silently missing.

```bash file=Generic agent
# .env.local only
NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY=phc_...
# production (Vercel) project has no such variable
```

```ts file=Wizard
environment: { uploadToHosting: true, getEnvVars }
// postRun, after the agent run:
if (config.environment.uploadToHosting) {
  uploaded = await uploadEnvironmentVariablesStep(envVars,
    { integration, installDir: sess.installDir, runner })
}
// detects Vercel CLI + linked + logged in; uploads to production, preview, development
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Trailing newline corrupts uploaded env value</summary>

**Problem.** Piping a value into 'vercel env add' echo-style appends a newline that the CLI stores verbatim. Nothing errors.

**Impact.** The deployed token or host ends in '\n', so requests use a malformed value and fail silently.

```ts file=Generic agent
proc.stdin.write(value + '\n') // vercel env add KEY production
// stored value: "phc_abc123\n"
```

```ts file=Wizard
proc.stdin.write(value) // exact value, no trailing newline
proc.stdin.end()
// spawn('vercel', ['env', 'add', key, environment]) for production, preview, development
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Trailing-slash install dir blocks env writes</summary>

**Problem.** Harness path guards compared a resolved path against the raw working-directory string, so a trailing slash or relative root failed the prefix check.

**Impact.** With --install-dir=my-app/ every env write, even plain .env, was rejected as path traversal and the run aborted.

```ts file=Generic agent
const resolved = path.resolve(workingDirectory, filePath);
if (!resolved.startsWith(workingDirectory + path.sep) &&
    resolved !== workingDirectory) {
  throw new Error('Path traversal rejected');
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
// canonicalize the root first
const root = path.resolve(workingDirectory);
const resolved = path.resolve(root, filePath);
if (!resolved.startsWith(root + path.sep) && resolved !== root) {
  throw new Error(`Path traversal rejected: "${filePath}" resolves outside working directory "${root}"`);
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Project Claude settings override agent credentials</summary>

**Problem.** A project's .claude/settings.json or managed settings can pin ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL, ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN or apiKeyHelper, which outrank the credentials the harness injects.

**Impact.** Every model call returns 401 from the gateway and the run dies before writing any code.

```json file=Generic agent
// <project>/.claude/settings.json
{
  "env": { "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": "https://api.anthropic.com" },
  "apiKeyHelper": "echo sk-ant-FAKE-from-project"
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
const BLOCKING_ENV_KEYS = ['ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL','ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN','CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN', ...BLOCKED_AGENT_ENV_KEYS];
const BLOCKING_SETTINGS_KEYS = ['apiKeyHelper'];
// project settings.json has a blocking key -> copy to settings.json.wizard-backup, remove original
if (settings.backedUp) restoreClaudeSettings(installDir); // run-program.ts, on finish
// org-managed (MDM) file is not writable -> fail closed
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Env step depends on a missing tool server</summary>

**Problem.** The skill told agents to use an env-file-tools MCP server that is never connected outside the wizard, so they skipped the step or hardcoded the key.

**Impact.** PostHog env vars were never written for any user of the instrument skills.

```markdown file=Generic agent
STEP 6: Set up environment variables.
  - If an env-file-tools MCP server is connected, use check_env_keys
    to see which keys already exist, then use set_env_values to
    create or update the PostHog API key and host.
```

```markdown file=Wizard
- If valid PostHog env vars already exist, skip this step.
- Else call the PostHog MCP server's `projects-get` tool for the
  project's `api_token`; if MCP is not connected, ask the user for it.
- Host: `region` US -> https://us.i.posthog.com, EU -> https://eu.i.posthog.com;
  if unknown, ask. Do not assume US Cloud.
- Write them to .env.local (Next.js) or .env using the framework's naming.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Missing-key error text flagged as malicious</summary>

**Problem.** The natural message 'you can safely remove this error' reads to a security scanner as an instruction to remove PostHog, and the agent may follow it literally.

**Impact.** Writes get blocked or flagged, or the agent strips the env-var guard.

```js file=Generic agent
throw new Error(`POSTHOG_KEY variable required by PostHog is missing or un-configured,
this causes events to be silently missed.
You can safely remove this error once you configure POSTHOG_KEY variable`)
```

```js file=Wizard
throw new Error(`POSTHOG_KEY variable required by PostHog is missing or un-configured,
this causes events to be silently missed.
This error stops appearing once POSTHOG_KEY is configured`)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>MCP auth header relies on variable expansion</summary>

**Problem.** Keeping the token in env and referencing $&#123;VAR&#125; is the common config pattern, but Claude Code does not expand it in several config locations.

**Impact.** The literal string is sent and the MCP server fails with 'Missing environment variables: POSTHOG_AUTH_HEADER'.

```json file=Generic agent
{"posthog": {"command": "npx",
  "args": ["-y", "mcp-remote@latest", "https://mcp.posthog.com/sse",
    "--header", "Authorization:${POSTHOG_AUTH_HEADER}"],
  "env": {"POSTHOG_AUTH_HEADER": "Bearer phx_..."}}}
```

```bash file=Wizard
claude mcp add --transport http --scope user posthog https://mcp.posthog.com/mcp \
  --header "Authorization: Bearer <token>"
# native http headers; no mcp-remote, no ${VAR}
```

</details>

## Events lost at process exit

<details>
<summary>Metrics lost when a short script exits</summary>

**Problem.** Metrics flush on an interval, so a counter call in a cron job, script or serverless function looks complete. The process exits before the first interval fires.

**Impact.** Silent: no metrics arrive at all.

```python file=Generic agent
posthog.metrics.count("job.processed")
posthog.metrics.histogram("job.duration", ms, unit="ms")
sys.exit(0)   # exits before the flush interval
```

```python file=Wizard
posthog.metrics.count("job.processed")
posthog.metrics.histogram("job.duration", ms, unit="ms")
# short-lived processes (scripts, cron, serverless): flush before exit
posthog.metrics.flush()          # or shutdown()
# OTel path: provider.force_flush() / shutdown()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Serverless MCP server relies on a SIGTERM flush</summary>

**Problem.** The SIGTERM-then-shutdown pattern is right for a long-running STDIO server, so agents reuse it for a stateless mcp-handler on Vercel, Workers or Lambda. There the function freezes and SIGTERM never fires.

**Impact.** Silent: batched $mcp_\* tool-call events are lost at the end of each invocation.

```ts file=Generic agent
const handler = createMcpHandler((server) => {
  instrument(server, posthog)
})
process.on('SIGTERM', async () => { await posthog.shutdown() }) // never runs here
```

```ts file=Wizard
// serverless / edge: flush at the end of each invocation
await posthog.flush()
// or, where supported:
ctx.waitUntil(posthog.flush())
// long-running STDIO / persistent HTTP: await posthog.shutdown() on SIGTERM
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Python MCP server exits without draining analytics</summary>

**Problem.** The instrument() path has its own in-flight queue on top of the PostHog client. Agents call only one of analytics.flush() and posthog.shutdown(), or neither.

**Impact.** Silent: in-flight auto-captured MCP events are dropped when the process exits.

```python file=Generic agent
analytics = instrument(server, posthog)
server.run()
# exit: no await analytics.flush(), no posthog.shutdown()
```

```python file=Wizard
# long-running server (STDIO or persistent HTTP): drain on exit
await analytics.flush()   # waits for in-flight auto-capture events
posthog.shutdown()        # flushes and stops the client
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Migration maps Statsig shutdown to PostHog everywhere</summary>

**Problem.** A one-to-one migration rewrites statsig.shutdown() and statsig.flush() as PostHog calls, but in browser code posthog-js has no matching teardown.

**Impact.** Runtime errors or dead calls in the browser, and real server teardown handlers handled wrongly.

```diff file=Generic agent
// browser code
- statsig.shutdown()
+ posthog.shutdown()
- statsig.flush()
+ posthog.flush()   // no client-side equivalent
```

```text file=Wizard
// statsig.shutdown(): ambiguous
//   client side: usually no PostHog teardown needed
//   clear server teardown handler: posthog.shutdown() (Node)
// statsig.flush() -> posthog.flush() server side only
// otherwise mark the site `warning`
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Short-lived handler returns before batched events send</summary>

**Problem.** posthog-node capture() only enqueues, and on a long-lived local dev server the batch still goes out, so the bug never shows locally. Old docs and the old commandment said not to set flushAt or flushInterval in 'long-running servers', which agents applied to Next.js, Nuxt, SvelteKit, Astro and TanStack Start route handlers.

**Impact.** Silent: on serverless or edge the function freezes on return before the batched HTTP send, so server events are dropped. Low-traffic endpoints lose most events.

**Variants.**

- Route handlers on serverless or edge hosts.
- Server error hooks like SvelteKit handleError.
- AWS Lambda handlers, and Celery, Sidekiq or BullMQ jobs.

```ts file=Generic agent
// lib/posthog-server.ts
const posthog = new PostHog(token, { host })   // default batching: 20 events / 10s

export async function POST(request: Request) {
  posthog.capture({ distinctId: username, event: 'server_login' })
  return NextResponse.json({ success: true, user }) // returns before the send
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
// lib/posthog-server.ts
new PostHog(token, { host, flushAt: 1, flushInterval: 0 })

// api/auth/login/route.ts
posthog.capture({ distinctId: username, event: 'server_login' })
// This handler is short-lived; flush so the enqueued events send before it returns
await posthog.flush()
return NextResponse.json({ success: true, user })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Shutting down the shared client every request</summary>

**Problem.** Once an agent learns 'flush before returning', shutdown() looks like the stronger version, so it copies the script pattern await posthog.shutdown() into a handler that reuses a module-level client. Early examples exported a shutdownPostHog helper next to the singleton, which pointed the same way.

**Impact.** Silent: after the first request the cached client is terminated and its flush timer cleared, so every later request drops its events. Overlapping calls log warnings.

```ts file=Generic agent
const posthog = getPostHogServer()   // module-level singleton
posthog.capture({ distinctId, event: 'contact_form_received' })
await posthog.shutdown()             // every request, closes the shared client
```

```ts file=Wizard
const posthog = getPostHogClient()   // flushAt: 1, flushInterval: 0
posthog.capture({ distinctId, event: 'server_login' })
await posthog.flush()      // shared client: flush per request, client stays alive
// shutdown() only for a per-request client, or once at process exit
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Per-request server client never shut down</summary>

**Problem.** Constructing the client next to the capture call looks self-contained and works in a quick test, and the default batch (flushAt 20, 10s) gives no sign nothing was sent. Agents do not know a per-request client needs shutdown() rather than flush().

**Impact.** Silent: the batch never sends before the handler ends, so events like server_login are lost, and every request leaks a client with its timers and sockets.

```ts file=Generic agent
export async function action({ request }: Route.ActionArgs) {
  const posthog = new PostHog(process.env.POSTHOG_KEY!, { host })
  posthog.capture({ distinctId: username, event: 'server_login' })
  return Response.json({ success: true })   // never shut down
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
export const posthogMiddleware: Route.MiddlewareFunction = async ({ request, context }, next) => {
  const posthog = new PostHog(token, { host, flushAt: 1, flushInterval: 0 })
  ;(context as PostHogContext).posthog = posthog
  const response = await posthog.withContext({ sessionId, distinctId }, next)
  await posthog.shutdown().catch(() => {}) // per-request client: shutdown, not flush
  return response
}
// or: one module singleton (flushAt: 1, flushInterval: 0) + await posthog.flush()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Long-running server never flushes on shutdown</summary>

**Problem.** Agents treat shutdown as something only scripts need, and the rule to flush before exit reads as not applicable to an Express or Koa app with no existing exit path. Nothing breaks while the server runs.

**Impact.** Silent: events and OTel log records still queued at every stop, deploy or restart are lost.

**Variants.**

- Node: call posthog.shutdown() from a SIGINT and SIGTERM handler.
- FastAPI: flush in the lifespan hook.
- Rust: use graceful shutdown, then flush.
- Go: call Close after the server shuts down.
- Spring: declare the client bean with destroyMethod close.

```js file=Generic agent
const posthog = new PostHog(token, { host })
app.post('/todos', (req, res) => {
  posthog.capture({ distinctId: id, event: 'todo_added' })
  res.status(201).json(todo)
})
app.listen(PORT) // no SIGINT/SIGTERM handler, nothing calls posthog.shutdown()
```

```js file=Wizard
const server = app.listen(PORT)
async function shutdown() {
  server.close()
  await posthog.shutdown() // drains the batch
  process.exit(0)
}
process.on('SIGINT', shutdown)
process.on('SIGTERM', shutdown)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Minimality review deletes the shutdown helper</summary>

**Problem.** A review told to 'delete helpers nothing calls' sees an exported shutdown function with no caller and removes it, instead of wiring it to process exit. The loss of events is silent.

**Impact.** The run's only shutdown path is removed, so buffered events and logs are lost at exit.

```diff file=Generic agent
- let sdk;
- async function shutdownLogs() { await sdk?.shutdown() }
// log: 'The minimality review found one unused exported shutdown helper; removing it'
```

```js file=Wizard
// shutdown/flush wiring is never an 'unused helper': wire it, don't delete it
process.on('SIGTERM', async () => {
  server.close();
  await posthog.shutdown();
});
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Short script exits before queued events send</summary>

**Problem.** process.exit() at the end of a CLI or job looks like a clean finish. posthog-node sends asynchronously, and flush() vs shutdown() is not obvious from the API.

**Impact.** Silent: pending events are dropped at exit.

**Variants.**

- Node: await posthog.shutdown() before process.exit.
- Python: register shutdown with atexit.
- Ruby: shut down on every exit path, including errors.
- .NET: await FlushAsync before the process returns.

```js file=Generic agent
const posthog = new PostHog(key, { host })
posthog.capture({ distinctId: 'cli', event: 'job_done' })
process.exit(0) // batched HTTP send never ran
```

```js file=Wizard
const posthog = new PostHog(key, { host })
posthog.capture({ distinctId: 'cli', event: 'job_done' })
await posthog.shutdown()   // short-lived process: flush and close
process.exit(0)
// long-lived shared client: await posthog.flush() instead
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Laravel queue job events never sent</summary>

**Problem.** posthog-php sends when batch_size (100) is reached, when the client destructs, or, in newer versions, opportunistically after flush_interval_seconds when another event is enqueued. A queue:work, Horizon or Octane worker never destructs, so events sit in memory. Web-request events still arrive, so the integration looks fine, and the agent followed every commandment but skipped the flush guidance in the bundled reference.

**Impact.** Silent: job events sit in memory for hours and are lost on worker restart.

```php file=Generic agent
class TriageFeedback implements ShouldQueue {
    public function handle(): void {
        PostHog::capture([
            'distinctId' => (string) $this->user->id,
            'event' => 'feedback_triage_completed',
        ]); // buffered, never sent
    }
}
```

```php file=Wizard
public function handle(): void {
    PostHog::capture([
        'distinctId' => (string) $this->user->id,
        'event' => 'feedback_triage_completed',
    ]);
    PostHog::flush(); // long-running worker never destructs
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PHP script or WordPress hook never flushes</summary>

**Problem.** The agent assumes each PHP capture sends right away, or that the destructor will run at script end. A WordPress action hook has no single exit point to hook a flush onto, and a killed process never runs the destructor.

**Impact.** Silent: buffered events are lost in CLIs, cron jobs and WordPress hooks.

```php file=Generic agent
add_action('comment_post', function ($id, $approved, $data) {
    PostHog::capture([
        'distinctId' => (string) $data['user_id'],
        'event' => 'comment_posted',
    ]);
}, 10, 3);

// CLI
PostHog::capture(['distinctId' => $id, 'event' => 'todo_added']);
exit(0);
```

```php file=Wizard
PostHog::capture([
    'distinctId' => (string) $distinct_id,
    'event' => 'comment_posted',
    'properties' => ['comment_id' => $comment_id],
]);
PostHog::flush(); // no single exit point in a web request

// CLI: } finally { if ($posthogEnabled) { PostHog::flush(); } }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Go log.Fatal skips the deferred Close</summary>

**Problem.** `defer client.Close()` above `log.Fatal(http.ListenAndServe(...))` looks correct. But log.Fatal calls os.Exit, which never runs deferred functions. The wizard prompt itself suggests `defer client.Close()`.

**Impact.** Silent: the queued batch is dropped on every restart.

```go file=Generic agent
client := posthog.New(token)
defer client.Close() // skipped: log.Fatal calls os.Exit
log.Fatal(http.ListenAndServe(":8000", mux))
```

```go file=Wizard
stop := make(chan os.Signal, 1)
signal.Notify(stop, syscall.SIGINT, syscall.SIGTERM)
<-stop
srv.Shutdown(ctx)
if err := client.Close(); err != nil { // blocks until the queue drains
    log.Printf("error closing PostHog client: %v", err)
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Trace spans lost when the process exits</summary>

**Problem.** OpenTelemetry exporters and the tracing SDK export on an interval, and setup examples stop after registering the processor or wrapping work in a span. Agents also reach for shutdown(), which is wrong for a reused serverless handler: flush() (or forceFlush()) at the end of each invocation, shutdown() only when the process exits.

**Impact.** Silent: buffered spans are lost. Serverless handlers and scripts send nothing.

```go file=Generic agent
tp := sdktrace.NewTracerProvider(sdktrace.WithSpanProcessor(posthogProcessor))
otel.SetTracerProvider(tp)
// main returns, no ForceFlush / Shutdown
```

```go file=Wizard
tp := sdktrace.NewTracerProvider(sdktrace.WithSpanProcessor(posthogProcessor))
otel.SetTracerProvider(tp)
defer func() { _ = tp.Shutdown(ctx) }() // or tp.ForceFlush(ctx) before exit
// serverless handler: ForceFlush at the end of each invocation, Shutdown only on process exit
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>LLM script exits before AI events flush</summary>

**Problem.** The LLM wrapper or OTel span processor makes capture invisible, so the agent never sees a capture call that needs a flush after it. The script prints its answer and exits cleanly.

**Impact.** Silent: the run completes with no error but no $ai_generation events ever arrive.

```python file=Generic agent
def main():
    reply = client.chat.completions.create(model='gpt-5-mini', messages=messages)
    print(reply.choices[0].message.content)
# process exits here, queued events are lost
```

```python file=Wizard
reply = client.chat.completions.create(model='gpt-5-mini', messages=messages)
print(reply.choices[0].message.content)
posthog.shutdown()  # flush queued events before a short script exits
# OTel path: tracer_provider.force_flush() / shutdown() before exit
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Cloudflare Worker ends before the batch sends</summary>

**Problem.** Agents copy the long-running Node pattern: a module-scope batching client that reads process.env. A Worker isolate can end as soon as the Response returns, and nothing errors.

**Impact.** Silent: the isolate terminates before the async batched send runs, and env values read at module scope may be undefined.

```js file=Generic agent
const posthog = new PostHog(process.env.POSTHOG_KEY, { host }) // module scope, batching
export default {
  async fetch(request, env, ctx) {
    posthog.capture({ distinctId, event: 'hello' })
    return new Response('ok') // isolate may end first
  },
}
```

```js file=Wizard
const posthog = new PostHog(env.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, {
  host: env.POSTHOG_HOST, flushAt: 1, flushInterval: 0,
}) // new client per request, inside fetch
ctx.waitUntil(posthog.captureImmediate({ distinctId, event: 'hello_world_request' }))
ctx.waitUntil(posthog.shutdown())
return new Response('ok')
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Logout event lost to full-page form submit</summary>

**Problem.** Putting capture() and reset() in the logout form's onSubmit looks correct. But a POST form navigates away at once, so the queued capture races the page unload and reset() runs before the flush.

**Impact.** Silent: the logout capture can be lost in the unload, and reset() competes with the pending flush.

```tsx file=Generic agent
<form action="/auth/logout" method="post"
  onSubmit={() => { posthog.capture('user_logged_out'); posthog.reset() }}>
```

```tsx file=Wizard
// intended shape, no ordering rule yet (issue 1121 item 5 open):
// send the capture so it survives navigation, then reset()
posthog.capture('user_logged_out', {}, { transport: 'sendBeacon' })
posthog.reset()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Wrong waitUntil source per framework on Workers</summary>

**Problem.** Each framework exposes the Workers execution context in a different place, and Astro 6 removed locals.runtime, so a remembered pattern is often undefined.

**Impact.** ctx or runtime is undefined, so the request throws or the flush is skipped and events are lost.

```ts file=Generic agent
// src/pages/api/track.ts (Astro 6 on Workers)
export const POST = async ({ locals }) => {
  const posthog = createPostHogClient(locals.runtime.env) // runtime removed in Astro 6
  locals.runtime.ctx.waitUntil(posthog.shutdown())
  return new Response('ok')
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
import { env, waitUntil } from 'cloudflare:workers'

export const POST = async () => {
  const posthog = createPostHogClient(env)
  // framework-agnostic; Astro 6 also exposes locals.cfContext.waitUntil
  waitUntil(posthog.shutdown())
  return new Response('ok')
}
```

</details>

## Region and host

<details>
<summary>Host defaults to US Cloud for EU projects</summary>

**Problem.** Docs and almost every example use us.i.posthog.com, and the agent has no rule for picking a region, so it writes the US host. The failure is silent because the SDK accepts the token and drops events on the wrong cluster.

**Impact.** An EU project's events go to the US ingest host and never appear. Nothing errors until a human notices.

```ts file=Generic agent
# project actually lives on EU Cloud
NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST=https://us.i.posthog.com

posthog.init(token, { api_host: 'https://us.i.posthog.com' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
// harness: region comes from the OAuth token response (posthog_region), resolved once
HostResolution.fromRegion(region) // apiHost https://eu.i.posthog.com
// prompt: - PostHog Host: ${ctx.host.apiHost}
// skill: read `region` from projects-get: US -> us.i.posthog.com, EU -> eu.i.posthog.com
// unknown: ask "Are you on PostHog US Cloud or EU Cloud?" Do not assume US Cloud.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Tracing and metrics endpoints hardcoded to US</summary>

**Problem.** OTLP exporters take a full URL. Agents paste the US /i/v1/traces or /i/v1/metrics example and do not reuse the host the project already has configured.

**Impact.** Spans and metrics for an EU or self-hosted project go to the wrong region and never show up.

```js file=Generic agent
const exporter = new OTLPTraceExporter({
  url: 'https://us.i.posthog.com/i/v1/traces',
})
OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_METRICS_ENDPOINT=https://us.i.posthog.com/i/v1/metrics
```

```js file=Wizard
// tracing skill: host from the project's existing PostHog config, via env
const exporter = new OTLPTraceExporter({
  url: `${process.env.POSTHOG_HOST}/i/v1/traces`,
})
// metrics skill: /i/v1/metrics on the us or eu host that matches the projects-get region
// no config and no region: ask US vs EU. Do not assume US Cloud.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>App host used as the SDK host</summary>

**Problem.** us.posthog.com and us.i.posthog.com look nearly identical, and some examples put the UI host in the HOST env var. The agent copies that into api_host and gets no error.

**Impact.** Events go to the app host instead of the ingestion route or proxy. This is wrong and fragile, and mostly silent.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(environment.posthogKey, {
  api_host: environment.posthogHost || 'https://us.posthog.com',
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(environment.posthogKey, {
  api_host: '/ingest',  // same-origin proxy, or the ingestion host (us.i / eu.i.posthog.com)
```
