```ts file=Wizard
  ui_host: environment.posthogHost || 'https://us.posthog.com', // app host belongs only in ui_host
})
// harness: HostResolution keeps apiHost (ingestion) and appHost (web app) as separate fields
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Host hardcoded as a literal in init</summary>

**Problem.** Pasting the host string is the shortest working code, and the agent cannot tell a region literal from a proxy. A bare literal freezes the region, and an unknown domain quietly receives every event, identify call and replay.

**Impact.** The region or proxy is baked into source. EU or self-hosted projects send to the wrong endpoint, and an unknown host collects all user data.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init('phc_AbCdEf123456', {
  api_host: 'https://us.i.posthog.com', // project is EU
})
posthog.init(key, { api_host: 'https://t.metrics-edge.example' }) // unknown host
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  api_host: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST || 'https://us.i.posthog.com', // env wins
})
// pi runtime note NO_LITERAL_URL: never write a PostHog URL or token as a literal; read env vars
// warlock posthog_hardcoded_unknown_api_host (warn): api_host outside *.posthog.com/localhost/127.0.0.1
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Region detected by substring match</summary>

**Problem.** host.includes('eu.posthog.com') is the obvious one-liner, and it passes every normal test. Lookalike hosts get through anyway.

**Impact.** A host like eu.posthog.com.attacker.com is routed as EU, and CodeQL flags it as incomplete URL substring sanitization.

```ts file=Generic agent
const isEu = host.includes('eu.posthog.com')
return isEu ? EU_URL : US_URL
```

```ts file=Wizard
const hostname = new URL(host).hostname.toLowerCase()
const isEu = hostname === 'eu.posthog.com' || hostname.endsWith('.eu.posthog.com')
// current main, gateway-session.ts: parse URL, then compare exact origins
if (url.hostname.endsWith('.posthog.com')) {
  return url.origin === 'https://ai-gateway.us.posthog.com' ||
         url.origin === 'https://ai-gateway.eu.posthog.com'
}
```

</details>

## Content Security Policy

<details>
<summary>Review trusts 'CSP handled' without checking directives</summary>

**Problem.** The init step says 'CSP handled' and review takes the handoff at its word instead of opening the policy. A connect-src-only fix looks complete because events still send.

**Impact.** A partial CSP passes review, so replay, surveys and the toolbar silently never load (events still send, so the integration looks alive).

```text file=Generic agent
init handoff: "Content-Security-Policy handled."
review: reads handoff, marks CSP dimension done
        (never opens the policy to check script-src / connect-src / worker-src)
```

```text file=Wizard
# init handoff names directives touched and left alone:
#   touched script-src, connect-src, worker-src; left X alone
# (an unnamed directive reads as unhandled, not as fine)
# review verifies directive-by-directive, not on the handoff's word:
script-src  allows PostHog hosts (needed even when posthog-js is bundled;
            replay/surveys/toolbar lazy-load from the assets CDN)
connect-src allows the API host (bare default-src 'self' blocks sending)
worker-src  blob: if session replay is on
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Rails CSP initializer left blocking PostHog</summary>

**Problem.** The agent gives the layout snippet a CSP nonce and believes the CSP is handled. The host allowlist lives in config/initializers/content_security_policy.rb, which it never opens, or it only tells the user to set env vars.

**Impact.** Silent: array.js and the replay and survey bundles are blocked. The diff looks complete while zero events send.

```ruby file=Generic agent
# config/initializers/content_security_policy.rb left as shipped
policy.script_src  :self, *sources.(:script_src)
policy.connect_src :self, *sources.(:connect_src)
# layout adds <script nonce=...> PostHog snippet; the CDN bundle is blocked
```

```ruby file=Wizard
policy.script_src  :self, "https://us-assets.i.posthog.com", *sources.(:script_src)
policy.connect_src :self, "https://us.i.posthog.com", "https://us-assets.i.posthog.com", *sources.(:connect_src)
policy.worker_src  :self, :blob   # session replay worker (from the generic rule, not in 2729)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Replay assets lack CORS and crossorigin</summary>

**Problem.** Turning on replay looks like one config flag. Nothing tells the agent that replay re-loads the app's fonts, stylesheets and images from origin, and that those responses need Access-Control-Allow-Origin for the PostHog app origin. Stylesheet links also need crossorigin to be readable for capture.

**Impact.** Silent: capture looks healthy, but replays render unstyled or without fonts and images.

```html file=Generic agent
<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://cdn.example.com/app.css">
<!-- asset responses carry no Access-Control-Allow-Origin -->
```

```html file=Wizard
<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://cdn.example.com/app.css" crossorigin="anonymous">
<!-- asset responses: Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://us.posthog.com -->
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Bundled SDK still needs script-src for lazy bundles</summary>

**Problem.** Bundling posthog-js looks like it satisfies script-src, and the old skill even said so. But replay, surveys and the toolbar lazy-load from the PostHog assets CDN, not from the API host.

**Impact.** Silent: events send, so the integration looks alive, but session replay, surveys and the toolbar never load.

```ts file=Generic agent
// entry.server.tsx (react-router saas-template)
"connect-src": [MODE === 'development' ? 'ws:' : undefined, "'self'", process.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST],
"script-src": ["'strict-dynamic'", "'self'", `'nonce-${nonce}'`],  // untouched
```

```text file=Wizard
Whenever you extend a CSP, extend every directive the SDK needs in one pass:
script-src:  https://*.posthog.com   (lazy-loaded bundles, even when self-hosting the entry)
connect-src: <the api host>          (event ingestion + feature flags)
worker-src:  blob:                   (session replay's worker)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>CSP edit leaves the replay worker blocked</summary>

**Problem.** The agent extends the directives that make events send, then stops. Nothing tells it that session replay runs in a blob: worker that worker-src must allow.

**Impact.** Silent: events send, so the integration looks alive, but session replay never starts.

```text file=Generic agent
connect-src 'self' blob: ${domain} https://*.posthog.com
worker-src 'self'
```

```text file=Wizard
connect-src 'self' blob: ${domain} https://*.posthog.com
worker-src 'self' blob:   # rule: worker-src: blob: (session replay's worker)
```

</details>

## Reverse proxy

<details>
<summary>Proxy sends /array config to the API origin</summary>

**Problem.** SvelteKit has no rewrites, so the agent hand-writes a handle() proxy and picks the host with one startsWith check. Docs and old examples only checked /static.

**Impact.** Silent: /ingest/array/ remote config is served from the API origin without cache-control and goes stale in browsers. If assets aren't split at all, the SDK script fails. Same for Next.js rewrites, Vite proxy rules and SvelteKit proxy hooks.

```ts file=Generic agent
if (pathname.startsWith('/ingest')) {
  const hostname = pathname.startsWith('/ingest/static/')
    ? 'us-assets.i.posthog.com'
    : 'us.i.posthog.com' // /ingest/array/* lands here
```

```ts file=Wizard
const useAssetHost = pathname.startsWith('/ingest/static/')
  || pathname.startsWith('/ingest/array/')
const hostname = useAssetHost
  ? 'us-assets.i.posthog.com'
  : 'us.i.posthog.com'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Hand-written proxy forwards the original headers unchanged</summary>

**Problem.** Passing the request through with fetch looks like a faithful proxy. But the Host header, the client IP and Node's streaming-body rules all need explicit handling, and no error points at them.

**Impact.** Requests fail or lose geolocation. Host still names the app, every event gets the server's IP, and Node fetch throws on a streamed body without duplex.

```ts file=Generic agent
return fetch('https://us.i.posthog.com' + pathname.replace('/ingest', ''), {
  method: event.request.method,
  headers: event.request.headers, // Host is the app's; client IP lost
  body: event.request.body,       // Node fetch needs duplex: 'half'
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
const headers = new Headers(event.request.headers)
headers.set('host', hostname)
headers.set('accept-encoding', '')
const clientIp = event.request.headers.get('x-forwarded-for') || event.getClientAddress()
if (clientIp) headers.set('x-forwarded-for', clientIp)
return fetch(url.toString(), { method: event.request.method, headers,
  body: event.request.body, duplex: 'half' })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Browser SDK sends straight to PostHog, no proxy</summary>

**Problem.** The quickstart snippet uses the public ingest host and works on the agent's machine. Ad-blocker loss only shows up as under-counting in production.

**Impact.** Silent: ad and tracking blockers drop a meaningful share of browser events, flags and replay.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(import.meta.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, {
  api_host: 'https://us.i.posthog.com', // blocked by ad blockers
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(import.meta.env.VITE_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, {
  api_host: '/ingest',
  ui_host: 'https://us.posthog.com',
  defaults: '2026-01-30',
})
// plus framework rewrites: /ingest/static, /ingest/array -> assets origin; /ingest -> us.i.posthog.com
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Proxy configured only for the dev server</summary>

**Problem.** The examples proxy through Vite server.proxy or Angular proxy.conf.json, which work during local testing. Neither exists in a production build, and nothing warns about it.

**Impact.** Silent in production: the static build has no /ingest route, so ingestion and SDK asset requests 404 and no events arrive.

```ts file=Generic agent
// vite.config.ts (vite dev / preview only)
server: { proxy: { '/ingest': { target: 'https://us.i.posthog.com', changeOrigin: true } } }
// app
posthog.init(token, { api_host: '/ingest' }) // no /ingest route in prod
```

```text file=Wizard
# vercel.json / netlify / nginx / CDN, same three routes as dev
/ingest/static/*  -> https://us-assets.i.posthog.com/static/*
/ingest/array/*   -> https://us-assets.i.posthog.com/array/*
/ingest/*         -> https://us.i.posthog.com/*
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Proxied api_host without ui_host</summary>

**Problem.** ui_host looks redundant once api_host is set, so the agent leaves it out or drops it while tidying init. Capture keeps working, so nothing shows the break.

**Impact.** Toolbar, replay and 'view in PostHog' links resolve against the proxy path instead of the PostHog app.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  api_host: '/ingest',
  // ui_host dropped
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  api_host: '/ingest',
  ui_host: 'https://us.posthog.com', // eu.posthog.com for EU
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>App middleware intercepts the proxy path</summary>

**Problem.** The agent adds rewrites without reading the existing i18n or auth middleware, whose matcher runs before the rewrites. Static assets still load, so PostHog looks initialized.

**Impact.** Silent: POST /ingest/s gets a 307 to /en/ingest/s, then a 404, so events, flags and replay all drop.

```ts file=Generic agent
// middleware.ts (next-intl)
export const config = {
  matcher: ['/((?!api|_next|_vercel|.*\\..*).*)'],
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
// exclude the proxy prefix from the middleware matcher
export const config = {
  matcher: ['/((?!api|ingest|_next|_vercel|.*\\..*).*)'],
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Proxy set up on one host, not the others</summary>

**Problem.** The agent proxies the app it is editing and misses a second site or subdomain in the same project. Per-host gaps don't show up without grouping events by host.

**Impact.** Silent: ad blockers drop events only from the direct host, so one domain under-reports.

```ts file=Generic agent
// app.example.com
posthog.init(key, { api_host: '/ingest', ui_host: 'https://us.posthog.com' })
// go.example.com (same project)
posthog.init(key, { api_host: 'https://us.i.posthog.com' })
```

```sql file=Wizard
countIf(coalesce(properties.$lib_custom_api_host,'') = ''
  OR coalesce(properties.$lib_custom_api_host,'') LIKE '%posthog.com%') AS direct,
countIf(coalesce(properties.$lib_custom_api_host,'') != ''
  AND coalesce(properties.$lib_custom_api_host,'') NOT LIKE '%posthog.com%') AS proxied
-- GROUP BY $host HAVING total >= 100
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Reverse proxy targets left on US for EU projects</summary>

**Problem.** The agent switches the main /ingest target to EU but copies the example's us-assets.i.posthog.com rules unchanged. Examples hardcode the US assets origin, and the split is easy to miss.

**Impact.** An EU project proxies /static and /array requests to the US assets origin while ingestion goes to EU, so SDK config or scripts may fail to load.

```ts file=Generic agent
'/ingest/array': { target: 'https://us-assets.i.posthog.com', changeOrigin: true }, // project is EU
'/ingest':       { target: 'https://eu.i.posthog.com', changeOrigin: true },
```

```ts file=Wizard
// route /static/* AND /array/* to the region's assets origin
const strip = (p: string) => p.replace(/^\/ingest/, '')
'/ingest/static': { target: 'https://eu-assets.i.posthog.com', changeOrigin: true, rewrite: strip },
'/ingest/array':  { target: 'https://eu-assets.i.posthog.com', changeOrigin: true, rewrite: strip },
'/ingest':        { target: 'https://eu.i.posthog.com', changeOrigin: true, rewrite: strip },
// ui_host: https://eu.posthog.com
```

</details>

## SDK API misuse

<details>
<summary>tracing_headers set with host including port</summary>

**Problem.** window.location.host and hostname look interchangeable, but the extension matches hostname only, and the failure is silent.

**Impact.** In dev no tracing headers are sent, so frontend and backend sessions are not linked.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(key, {
  api_host: apiHost,
  tracing_headers: [window.location.host],  // 'localhost:4321' != 'localhost'
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(key, {
  api_host: apiHost,
  tracing_headers: typeof window !== 'undefined'
    ? [window.location.hostname] : [],   // SSR guard (TanStack Start)
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Enabling web vitals overwrites other performance options</summary>

**Problem.** To enable web vitals, the agent writes a fresh capture_performance object. It replaces existing keys instead of merging.

**Impact.** Silent. Either no LCP, CLS or INP data, or other performance options such as network timing are lost.

```ts file=Generic agent
// before: capture_performance: { network_timing: true }
posthog.init(token, { capture_performance: { web_vitals: true } })   // network_timing dropped
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(token, {
  capture_performance: { network_timing: true, web_vitals: true },   // keep existing keys
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Unfiltered source catalog floods agent context</summary>

**Problem.** source_type looks optional, so the agent calls the tool bare to explore. The reply is the whole catalog.

**Impact.** A reply of about 200 kB uses up the agent's context and forces file-grep workarounds. It was the top cause of aborted warehouse runs.

```js file=Generic agent
external-data-sources-wizard({})
-> ~200 kB describing every source type
```

```js file=Wizard
// always call it for a kind before creating that kind, always pass source_type
external-data-sources-wizard({ "source_type": "Postgres,Stripe" })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Database schemas array placed outside payload</summary>

**Problem.** The tool description was unclear, and a top-level schemas argument next to payload looks natural.

**Impact.** The create is rejected or no tables are selected, so the database is never connected.

```js file=Generic agent
external-data-sources-create({
  "payload": { "source_type": "Postgres", "host": "..." },
  "schemas": [{ "name": "orders", "should_sync": true }]
})
```

```js file=Wizard
external-data-sources-create({ "payload": {
  "source_type": "Postgres", /* credential fields */
  "access_method": "warehouse",
  "schemas": [ /* incremental where a field exists, else full_refresh */ ]
}})   // schemas goes INSIDE payload
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Webhook pending inputs ignored after source setup</summary>

**Problem.** The setup call returns success, so the agent reports the source as connected. It does not read the nested webhook key that still needs input.

**Impact.** Silent: the source exists but its webhook is never registered, so the data goes stale.

```js file=Generic agent
data-warehouse-source-setup({ "source_type": "Stripe", ... })
-> { "webhook": { "pending_inputs": ["signing_secret"] } }
"Connected Stripe"   // webhook never finished
```

```js file=Wizard
// read the `webhook` key in the response;
// if webhook.pending_inputs is non-empty, ask the user for those values
// and submit them before reporting the source as connected
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Credential question sent without question kind</summary>

**Problem.** The pi harness passes tool arguments through without validation. The agent marks a question sensitive but leaves out kind, and sensitive is only legal on text.

**Impact.** The whole batch is dropped, or the overlay shows a prompt with no input. Two dismissals send the remaining sources to browser setup.

```js file=Generic agent
wizard_ask({ "subject": "Stripe", "questions": [
  { "id": "api_key", "prompt": "Stripe restricted key", "sensitive": true } ] })
// no kind: batch refused
```

```js file=Wizard
// kind is optional; resolveAskQuestionKinds fills it in:
//   options supplied -> 'single', otherwise 'text'
wizard_ask({ "subject": "Stripe", "questions": [
  { "id": "api_key", "kind": "text", "sensitive": true, ... }] })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Task handoff fields sent flat, not nested</summary>

**Problem.** Flat arguments are the default shape for tool calls, and the description did not say the fields nest under handoff.

**Impact.** Schema validation rejects the call, which wastes a turn on a task that already finished.

```js file=Generic agent
complete_task({ "status": "done", "goals": "...", "did": "...", "forNextAgent": "..." })
-> schema validation error
```

```js file=Wizard
complete_task({ "status": "done",
  "handoff": { "goals": "...", "did": "...", "forNextAgent": "..." } })
// COMPLETE_TASK_DESCRIPTION names the nesting and the three required fields
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>SDK init cancels products enabled in project</summary>

**Problem.** The agent turns on Session Replay and Error Tracking in project settings and treats that as done. It never checks client init options that override the server switch.

**Impact.** Silent: the product shows as enabled but the client sends no recordings or exceptions, so the inbox stays empty.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  api_host: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST,
  disable_session_recording: true,  // cancels the replay enable
  capture_exceptions: false,        // cancels the error-tracking enable
})
```

```text file=Wizard
products-enable { products: [session_replay, error_tracking, conversations] }
then find posthog.init(...):
- disable_session_recording: true -> remove (or false)
- capture_exceptions: false -> remove (or true)
- cannot locate the init confidently -> record a follow-up, do not guess
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Go identify faked with a $identify capture</summary>

**Problem.** The Go commandments mention $set but name no identify API, so the agent builds one from a Capture message with a literal event name.

**Impact.** A fake event named $identify goes out instead of the SDK's identify path, and person properties may not apply.

```go file=Generic agent
client.Enqueue(posthog.Capture{
    DistinctId: userID,
    Event:      "$identify",
    Properties: posthog.NewProperties().Set("email", email),
})
```

```go file=Wizard
client.Enqueue(posthog.Identify{
    DistinctId: userID,
    Properties: posthog.NewProperties().Set("email", email),
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Rust async client in a crate without runtime</summary>

**Problem.** posthog-rs defaults to its async client, and a plain `cargo add` picks that up even when the crate has no tokio, async-std or smol. The agent then patches around build errors or adds tokio just for PostHog.

**Impact.** Build errors in a sync app, or an unrequested async runtime and refactor.

```rust file=Generic agent
# Cargo.toml has no tokio / async-std / smol
$ cargo add posthog-rs   # default async client
let client = posthog_rs::client(options).await; // main() is sync
```

```ts file=Wizard
// harness: detect a runtime in Cargo.toml
asyncRuntime = /^\s*(tokio|async-std|smol)\s*[=.]/m.test(cargoToml)
// runtime present: cargo add posthog-rs (async client)
// none: cargo add posthog-rs --no-default-features (blocking client)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Exception middleware swallows errors instead of rethrowing</summary>

**Problem.** A capture-and-respond-500 middleware looks complete. The agent does not see that it short-circuits the app's own error pipeline.

**Impact.** The app's developer page, exception handlers and logging are bypassed, so behavior changes silently.

```csharp file=Generic agent
app.Use(async (ctx, next) => {
    try { await next(); }
    catch (Exception ex) {
        posthog.CaptureException(ex, distinctId);
        ctx.Response.StatusCode = 500; // chain bypassed
    }
});
```

```csharp file=Wizard
catch (Exception ex)
{
    posthog.CaptureException(ex, distinctId);
    throw; // existing ASP.NET Core error handling still runs
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>person_profiles 'always' on mostly anonymous traffic</summary>

**Problem.** 'always' sounds like the complete, safe choice, and the cost difference is not visible in code.

**Impact.** Every anonymous event creates a person profile and is billed as identified, at roughly four times the price.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.init(key, {
  api_host: '/ingest',
  person_profiles: 'always',
})
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(key, { api_host: '/ingest' })
// default 'identified_only'; keep 'always' only when identified traffic >= 90%
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Copy and dead-click autocapture switched on</summary>

**Problem.** The agent enables extra autocapture options as 'more signal' without knowing each one is a billed event that heatmaps already cover.

**Impact.** Every copy or dead click becomes a billed event, although heatmaps collect dead-click coordinates for free.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.init(key, {
  capture_copied_text: true,
  capture_dead_clicks: true,
})
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(key, { api_host: '/ingest' })
// leave capture_copied_text / capture_dead_clicks unset;
// heatmaps already get dead-click coordinates free
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Statsig logEvent value dropped in migration</summary>

**Problem.** capture(name, props) has no slot for the positional value, so the agent drops it or renames the event to look tidier.

**Impact.** Numeric value metrics disappear and event names stop matching history. Nothing reports it.

```diff file=Generic agent
- statsig.logEvent('purchase', 49.99, { sku })
+ posthog.capture('purchase', { sku })   // value dropped
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.capture('purchase', { value: 49.99, sku })
// logEvent({ eventName, value, metadata }) -> capture(eventName, { value, ...metadata })
// event names pass through verbatim
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>LLM wrapper on pinned pre-1.0 openai emits nothing</summary>

**Problem.** The PostHog wrapper and OTel instrumentors target openai&gt;=1.x. The agent does not check the pinned version, and an install with nothing to patch raises no error.

**Impact.** Silent: zero AI events. Upgrading openai instead would break the app's own openai.ChatCompletion calls.

```python file=Generic agent
# requirements.txt: openai==0.27.5
from opentelemetry.instrumentation.openai import OpenAIInstrumentor
OpenAIInstrumentor().instrument()   # patches the 1.x client only
resp = openai.ChatCompletion.create(model="gpt-4", messages=msgs)
```

```python file=Wizard
# openai pinned below 1.0: do not upgrade, use manual capture
resp = openai.ChatCompletion.create(model=model, messages=msgs)
posthog.capture(distinct_id=user_id, event="$ai_generation", properties={
    "$ai_model": model, "$ai_trace_id": trace_id,
    "$ai_session_id": session_id})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Disabled Python client still raises on capture</summary>

**Problem.** Agents guard only the init block behind a missing-config flag and assume later calls are no-ops. posthog-python's module API raises when api_key is None, even with disabled set.

**Impact.** The first capture raises ValueError('API key is required') and crashes the request (login) in exactly the opt-out case.

```python file=Generic agent
if not app.config['POSTHOG_DISABLED']:
    posthog.api_key = app.config['POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN']
    posthog.host = app.config['POSTHOG_HOST']
# disabled: api_key stays None
capture('user_logged_in')  # ValueError: API key is required
```

```python file=Wizard
# disabled + a non-empty key makes every call a no-op
posthog.api_key = app.config['POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN'] or 'unset'
posthog.host = app.config['POSTHOG_HOST']
posthog.disabled = (app.config['POSTHOG_DISABLED']
                    or not app.config['POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN'])
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Session replay records bounces and test runs</summary>

**Problem.** The basic docs snippet just turns replay on. Minimum duration and test gating are optional settings the agent never adds.

**Impact.** Bounce sessions and synthetic CI/Playwright sessions inflate recording volume and cost.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(key, { session_recording: {} })
// runs under vitest, Playwright and CI; no minimum duration
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(key, {
  disable_session_recording: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'test' || !!process.env.CI,
  session_recording: { minimumDurationMilliseconds: 2000,
                       strictMinimumDuration: true },
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Wrapping the SDK in a guarded require</summary>

**Problem.** When install fails or the build is red, the quick way to get green is a try/catch `require` or an "if installed" check. It compiles, but the SDK is never initialized, so every capture silently does nothing.

**Impact.** The app builds and the check passes, but no events are ever sent.

```js file=Generic agent
let posthog;
try { posthog = require('posthog-node') } catch {}
// later
posthog?.capture?.({ event: 'signed_up' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
import { PostHog } from 'posthog-node'
// initialize at the entry point of every runtime, before any capture
export const posthog = new PostHog(process.env.POSTHOG_KEY!, { host: process.env.POSTHOG_HOST })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>React Native logs hand-wired through OpenTelemetry</summary>

**Problem.** PostHog logs docs for other languages say to wire OpenTelemetry directly, so the agent generalizes that pattern. Only the React Native page says the SDK handles it.

**Impact.** A hand-built OTLP pipeline that misses the SDK's batching, offline persistence and app lifecycle handling.

```ts file=Generic agent
import { OTLPLogExporter } from '@opentelemetry/exporter-logs-otlp-http'
const exporter = new OTLPLogExporter({ url: '<posthog otlp logs endpoint>' })
// wired into a LoggerProvider by hand
```

```ts file=Wizard
// posthog-react-native >= 4.44.0
posthog.logger.info('checkout started', { order_id })
posthog.logger.error('payment failed', { code: 'E001' })
// or full control: posthog.captureLog({ ... })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Made-up PostHog wrapper client for AWS Bedrock</summary>

**Problem.** Every other provider uses a PostHog wrapper client, so the agent assumes one exists for boto3 bedrock-runtime and invents the import.

**Impact.** The import fails or nothing is captured. Bedrock still needs OpenTelemetry instrumentation of the AWS SDK.

```python file=Generic agent
# app calls boto3.client('bedrock-runtime')
from posthog.ai.bedrock import BedrockRuntime  # no such wrapper
```

```text file=Wizard
AWS Bedrock has no wrapper client. It instruments the AWS SDK through
OpenTelemetry, so its package list differs from every other provider.
(Install table row: OTel packages the install doc names.)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Scaffolding features the Rust or Java SDK lacks</summary>

**Problem.** Training data and JS docs describe alias, replay, surveys and exception autocapture, so the agent assumes every SDK has them.

**Impact.** Code that does not compile or silently does nothing, and a report claiming capabilities the user lacks.

```text file=Generic agent
// Rust
client.alias(old_id, new_id)?;          // no alias in posthog-rs
// Java Spring
PostHog.builder(key).enableExceptionAutocapture(true)  // not in posthog-server
```

```text file=Wizard
Rust: no identify/alias helper, no surveys or session replay; set person props by inserting a $set property on an event.
Java server SDK: no automatic error tracking, surveys or session replay; do not promise or scaffold them. Identify via userProperty(...) on a capture; report failures as an explicit capture event.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Exceptions captured as strings or dynamic messages</summary>

**Problem.** captureException accepts a string and capture('$exception') looks equivalent, so an agent writes whatever compiles. The grouping cost of ids in messages and dropped causes never shows up locally.

**Impact.** Issues do not group, fingerprints fragment, and root causes are lost.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.captureException('failed to save')
posthog.captureException(new Error(`order ${id} failed`))
posthog.capture('$exception', { message: e.message })
```

```ts file=Wizard
// a real Error, a stable message, the cause kept
posthog.captureException(new Error('Order save failed', { cause: e }), distinctId)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Ruby gem required by its package name</summary>

**Problem.** The gem is named posthog-ruby, so require 'posthog-ruby' looks correct. The library file is actually posthog.

**Impact.** LoadError at boot, a hard crash on the first line that touches PostHog.

```ruby file=Generic agent
# Gemfile: gem 'posthog-ruby'
require 'posthog-ruby'
```

```ruby file=Wizard
# Gemfile: gem 'posthog-ruby'
require 'posthog'   # NOT 'posthog-ruby'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Low-level create used for SaaS sources</summary>

**Problem.** The generic create tool looks like it works for every source, and its own docs say schemas are optional. The server still requires them for SaaS sources.

**Impact.** Stripe, Resend and Sentry creates are rejected for a missing schemas array, so the source falls back to a browser link.

```js file=Generic agent
external-data-sources-create({ "payload": {
  "source_type": "Stripe", "api_key": "rk_live_..."
}})   // no schemas
-> rejected: schemas required
```

```js file=Wizard
data-warehouse-source-setup({
  "source_type": "Stripe",
  "payload": { "api_key": "rk_live_..." }
})   // validates, discovers tables, applies sync defaults; pass no schemas
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Browser SDK options on the Node client</summary>

**Problem.** The agent writes init options from memory, or copies the posthog-js options it just wrote, and blends posthog-js and posthog-node, which share a name but not an options type. It does not read the installed types in node_modules.

**Impact.** next build fails type-check. In loosely typed projects the unknown option is silently ignored.

```ts file=Generic agent
const posthogClient = new PostHog(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY!, {
  host: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST,
  capture_pageviews: 'history_change',   // posthog-js option
  flushAt: 1,
  flushInterval: 0,
});
// Type error: 'capture_pageviews' does not exist in type 'PostHogOptions'
```

```ts file=Wizard
// read node_modules/posthog-node/dist/types.d.ts, use only options that exist
const posthog = new PostHog(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN!, {
  host: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST,
  flushAt: 1,
  flushInterval: 0,
})
// pageview mode is a posthog-js init option (capture_pageview), never a posthog-node one
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Browser SDK imported in server code</summary>

**Problem.** posthog-js is the best-known package, and in SSR frameworks (Next.js, TanStack Start, SvelteKit) server routes sit next to client files, so the agent reuses one import everywhere. Nothing fails at build or import time. A lockfile-less static site can also be detected as Node, which installs a server SDK.

**Impact.** Silent or crashing: posthog-js has no window or init on the server, so backend events are lost. A Node SDK on a static site instruments a server that does not exist.

```ts file=Generic agent
// app/api/stripe/route.ts
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
export async function POST(req: Request) {
  posthog.capture('checkout_completed', { plan })
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
import { PostHog } from 'posthog-node'
let posthogClient: PostHog | null = null
export function getPostHogClient() {
  if (!posthogClient) posthogClient = new PostHog(token,
    { host, flushAt: 1, flushInterval: 0 })
  return posthogClient
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Server SDK imported into client components</summary>

**Problem.** Once a posthog-node helper exists in the project, the agent reuses it everywhere, including in 'use client' files. The import path does not show that it is Node-only.

**Impact.** A Node-only SDK ends up in the browser bundle, which breaks the build or runtime, or client events never fire.

```ts file=Generic agent
'use client'
import { getPostHogClient } from '@/lib/posthog-server'   // posthog-node
const posthog = getPostHogClient()
posthog.capture({ distinctId: 'x', event: 'button_clicked' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
'use client'
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.capture('button_clicked', { buttonId: 'submit' })
// posthog-node stays server-side only (route handlers, server actions)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>posthog-js call shape used in posthog-node</summary>

**Problem.** The agent knows posthog-js's capture(event, props) best and reuses it on the server, where capture takes one object with a required distinctId.

**Impact.** Wrong signature: a TypeScript error, or events dropped or attributed to the wrong person in JS.

```js file=Generic agent
app.post('/todos', (req, res) => {
  posthog.capture('todo_added', { todo_id: todo.id })
  res.status(201).json(todo)
})
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.capture({
  distinctId: userId,
  event: 'todo_added',
  properties: { todo_id: todo.id },
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PostHog methods called before init runs</summary>

**Problem.** In plain JS, ES module imports run before the entry file's body. An init placed in main.js therefore runs after modules that capture on load.

**Impact.** Early events are dropped with only a console warning, so first-load tracking is lost.

```js file=Generic agent
// main.js
import { setupTodos } from './todos.js'  // captures on load
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(key, { api_host: host })     // runs too late
```

```js file=Wizard
// src/posthog.js: init once and export the instance
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(projectToken, { api_host: apiHost })
export default posthog
// main.js and every other module: import posthog from './posthog.js'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Two documented SDK setups mixed together</summary>

**Problem.** Docs show a global initializer and an explicit client side by side, and Python framework commandments still use module-level helpers while newer guidance uses a Posthog() instance. The agent takes wiring from one and calls from the other, and the code reads fine.

**Impact.** In Python, module-level helpers route to a hidden, never-configured default client, so every event is silently dropped. In other SDKs (for example iOS PostHogSDK.setup then calling methods on a never-built client) the code does not compile, because the initializer returns nothing to call methods on.

```python file=Generic agent
from posthog import Posthog, capture
posthog_client = Posthog(token, host=host)   # constructor client
capture('signup', distinct_id=uid)           # module-level helper: hidden default client, dropped
```

```python file=Wizard
# Follow ONE setup all the way through:
posthog_client = Posthog(token, host=host)
posthog_client.capture('signup', distinct_id=uid)
# or module config + module calls, never a mix
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Python configured through module globals</summary>

**Problem.** Older Python docs and training data set posthog.api_key on the module. That still runs, so the agent has no signal to switch to the instance API.

**Impact.** Global mutable config, and instance options such as exception autocapture and an explicit shutdown target are never set up, so the code drifts from the instance-based examples the skill assumes.

```python file=Generic agent
import posthog
posthog.api_key = os.environ["POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"]
posthog.host = "https://us.i.posthog.com"
posthog.capture(distinct_id=uid, event="todo_added")
```

```python file=Wizard
from posthog import Posthog
posthog = Posthog(
    project_token,
    host=os.getenv('POSTHOG_HOST', 'https://us.i.posthog.com'),
    enable_exception_autocapture=True,
)
# one shared client, imported where needed
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Removed Python identify and positional capture</summary>

**Problem.** Training data predates Python SDK v6, which removed identify() and the positional capture(distinct_id, event) shape, and copying the JS API also leads to identify(). Even PostHog's own FastAPI example and README had the old shape, and the agent does not read the source in site-packages.

**Impact.** AttributeError or TypeError on login and signup routes (500s), or person properties are silently never set.

```python file=Generic agent
posthog.identify(user.email, {"email": user.email})   # not in SDK v6+
posthog.capture(user.email, "user_logged_in",          # positional form removed
                properties={"is_new_user": is_new_user})
```

```python file=Wizard
from posthog import capture, identify_context, new_context
with new_context():
    identify_context(str(user.id))
    capture("user_logged_in", properties={
        "$set": {"email": user.email},
        "is_new_user": is_new_user})
# or posthog_client.set(distinct_id=..., properties={...})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Tracing headers internal, hand-rolled, or silently no-op</summary>

**Problem.** Older examples used the internal \_\_add_tracing_headers name or a hand-written fetch wrapper, and README comments claimed session ids were forwarded. The public tracing_headers option is ignored below posthog-js 1.380.0, and the install step says to leave an existing SDK alone, so even the right name can do nothing.

**Impact.** Silent: no X-POSTHOG-\* headers are sent, so server events never join the client session. Hand-rolled wrappers send only the session id and drift from the SDK.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.init(key, {
  __add_tracing_headers: ['your-backend-domain.com'],  // internal, deprecated
})
fetch('/api/login', { headers: { 'X-PostHog-Session-Id': posthog.get_session_id() } })
// package.json: "posthog-js": "^1.338.0" (via @posthog/react)
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(key, {
  tracing_headers: [window.location.hostname],
})
// package.json: "posthog-js": "^1.406.2"   (option exists from 1.380.0)
// public tracing_headers, never __add_tracing_headers; no hand-rolled fetch wrapper
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Ruby capture and capture_exception argument styles swapped</summary>

**Problem.** posthog-ruby takes keyword args for capture but positional args for capture_exception. Agents assume one style for both, and the mistake usually sits inside a rarely run rescue block.

**Impact.** ArgumentError on Ruby 3 raised inside the rescue block, a Hash used as the distinct id, or exception events with no distinct id.

```ruby file=Generic agent
client.capture('user_123', 'my_event', { key: 'value' })   # needs keywords
rescue StandardError => e
  posthog&.capture_exception(e, distinct_id: get_user_id)  # needs positional
```

```ruby file=Wizard
client.capture(distinct_id: 'user_123', event: 'my_event',
               properties: { key: 'value' })
rescue StandardError => e
  posthog&.capture_exception(e, get_user_id, { source: 'job' })  # positional
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PHP capture called with positional arguments</summary>

**Problem.** Most SDKs take (distinctId, event, props), so the agent writes the same shape. posthog-php takes one associative array through static methods, with camelCase 'distinctId'.

**Impact.** PostHog::capture(array $message) rejects a string first argument with a TypeError that fails the request or CLI run. Building a client per call instead of PostHog::init once also defeats the batch buffer.

```php file=Generic agent
PostHog::init($token);
PostHog::capture($userId, 'order_created', ['total' => 42]);
// TypeError: capture() expects array, string given
```

```php file=Wizard
PostHog::init($token, ['host' => $host]); // once
PostHog::capture([
    'distinctId' => (string) $userId,
    'event' => 'order_created',
    'properties' => ['total' => 42],
]);
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Elixir code calls capture_exception and identify</summary>

**Problem.** Other SDKs have capture_exception and identify, so the agent assumes Elixir does too. posthog-elixir captures errors through a Logger handler and has neither function.

**Impact.** UndefinedFunctionError, or duplicates of what Logger already captures. Without in_app_otp_apps, error grouping is noisy.

```elixir file=Generic agent
rescue
  error ->
    PostHog.capture_exception(error)              # not in the SDK
PostHog.identify(user.id, %{email: user.email})   # no such function
```

```elixir file=Wizard
config :posthog,
  api_key: System.get_env("POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"),
  in_app_otp_apps: [:my_app]
# controller rescue: PostHog auto-captures this as an exception
Logger.error("Burrito profile error: #{Exception.message(error)}")
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Elixir capture without distinct_id silently dropped</summary>

**Problem.** capture(event, props) looks complete, and Elixir returns an error tuple instead of raising. The ignored return value hides the failure.

**Impact.** When no distinct_id is in the properties or in the process context, capture returns &#123;:error, :missing_distinct_id&#125; and sends nothing, so the event is silently dropped.

```elixir file=Generic agent
PostHog.capture("burrito_considered", %{total_considerations: count})
# => {:error, :missing_distinct_id}, ignored
```

```elixir file=Wizard
PostHog.capture("burrito_considered", %{
  distinct_id: current_distinct_id(conn),
  total_considerations: count
})
# distinct_id is required; every other key becomes an event property
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Invented getInstance() accessor on Android SDK</summary>

**Problem.** Most Android SDKs use a singleton getInstance(), and the repo's own example README taught PostHog.getInstance(), an API that never existed in posthog-android.

**Impact.** Compile failure in the user's app. The diff looks complete but does not build.

```kotlin file=Generic agent
import com.posthog.android.PostHogAndroid

fun capturePostHogEvent(event: String, properties: Map<String, Any> = emptyMap()) {
    PostHogAndroid.getInstance().capture(event, properties)
}
// README variant: val posthog = PostHog.getInstance()
```

```kotlin file=Wizard
// com.posthog.android is only for setup(), in Application.onCreate
PostHogAndroid.setup(this, config)

import com.posthog.PostHog   // everywhere else: the static object
PostHog.capture(
    event = "burrito_considered",
    properties = mapOf("total_considerations" to count)
)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>KMP uses native imports and wrong context</summary>

**Problem.** The native Android package com.posthog looks right, and shared-code instinct says to build the context once. The agent does not know that PostHogContext needs the Application on Android and no argument elsewhere.

**Impact.** Fails to compile, or the Android target starts without the Application and has no usable SDK, or other targets get the wrong context.

```kotlin file=Generic agent
import com.posthog.PostHog                  // native package, not com.posthog.kmp
// androidMain
PostHog.setup(config, PostHogContext())    // needs the Application on Android
```

```kotlin file=Wizard
import com.posthog.kmp.PostHog
import com.posthog.kmp.PostHogConfig
import com.posthog.kmp.PostHogContext
// once, early
PostHog.setup(config, PostHogContext(application))   // androidMain
PostHog.setup(config, PostHogContext())              // iosMain / web
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flutter captureException called without named args</summary>

**Problem.** Other PostHog SDKs take the error as a positional argument, so an agent carries that shape over to Dart.

**Impact.** A compile error. Without an explicit stackTrace the captured exception has no real trace for the uploaded symbols to resolve against.

```dart file=Generic agent
Posthog().captureException(Exception("PostHog source maps test"));
```

```dart file=Wizard
Posthog().captureException(
  error: Exception("PostHog source maps test"),
  stackTrace: StackTrace.current,
);
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Anthropic stream() bypasses the PostHog LLM wrapper</summary>

**Problem.** messages.stream() is the idiomatic Anthropic streaming call, but the @posthog/ai wrapper only intercepts create(). No build or type-check catches it, and a smoke test of the non-streaming path passes.

**Impact.** Runtime TypeError 'withResponse is not a function' on streaming paths, or streaming calls send no $ai_generation events, while non-streaming paths work.

```ts file=Generic agent
const client = new PostHogAnthropic({ apiKey, posthog })
const stream = await client.messages.stream({ model, messages, max_tokens })
// TypeError: messages.create(...).withResponse is not a function
```

```ts file=Wizard
// Fix reported in issue 447 (not yet a skill rule): the wrapper only
// intercepts create(), so stream through it
const stream = await client.messages.create({
  model, messages, max_tokens, stream: true,
})
// Encoded today: the report checklist says to trigger each instrumented
// LLM call path and confirm $ai_generation events appear
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Stale instrument(server.server) workaround kept</summary>

**Problem.** The project already has the workaround and it looks deliberate, so the agent leaves it. The old compatibility check it worked around (versions before @posthog/mcp 0.11.2) has since been fixed.

**Impact.** The project keeps a workaround for a check that no longer exists, instrumenting the low-level handle instead of the supported instrument(server) call that works on both SDK majors.

```ts file=Generic agent
const server = new McpServer({ name: "my-mcp-server", version: "1.0.0" })
const analytics = instrument(server.server, posthog)   // old workaround kept
```

```ts file=Wizard
const server = new McpServer({ name: "my-mcp-server", version: "1.0.0" })
const analytics = instrument(server, posthog)   // change server.server back
// v2 tools register with registerTool(); server.tool() was removed
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Custom MCP dispatcher forwards raw tool arguments</summary>

**Problem.** Forwarding request.params.arguments is the obvious dispatcher code. The agent does not know the MCP analytics SDK injects an llm_model argument that its prepare helpers strip and advertise.

**Impact.** The injected llm_model argument reaches tool handlers, tools/list never advertises it, and model ownership breaks across replicas.

```ts file=Generic agent
const result = await runTool(request.params.name, request.params.arguments)
posthog.captureToolCall({ toolName: request.params.name,
  parameters: request.params.arguments, response: result, durationMs, isError: false })
```

```ts file=Wizard
const advertisedTools = posthog.prepareToolList(serverTools) // return from tools/list
const { args, intent, llmModel } =
  posthog.prepareToolCall(name, request.params.arguments, { originalTool })
const result = await runTool(name, args) // cleaned args, never raw
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Private SDK method patched by its source name</summary>

**Problem.** The agent reads the unminified SDK source, patches a private method, and the unit test passes. The production bundle renames the method.

**Impact.** The patch never runs in the browser bundle. In Kestrel, valid flag responses arrived but the UI stayed disabled.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.featureFlags._someInternalMethod = patched   // renamed in the bundle
```

```js file=Wizard
// public API only: onFeatureFlags, getFeatureFlag, reloadFeatureFlags
posthog.onFeatureFlags(() => render(posthog.getFeatureFlag('flag-key')))
// don't patch SDK internals; label a member private/protected only if its .d.ts says so
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>First-touch properties written with $set</summary>

**Problem.** identify(id, props) is the obvious place for user attributes, and its second argument is $set. Agents rarely know about the third, $set_once, argument.

**Impact.** Every later identify() overwrites the original value, which corrupts attribution data.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.identify(user.id, {
  signup_landing_page: landingPath,
  original_partner_id: partnerId,
})
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.identify(user.id, { plan: user.plan }, {
  signup_landing_page: landingPath,   // $set_once
  original_partner_id: partnerId,
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>group() called with unstable key on render</summary>

**Problem.** The agent puts group() in a render or mount path, sometimes with a throwaway id. The SDK accepts any key without complaint.

**Impact.** Groups multiply per session, group properties churn, and $groupidentify events inflate the bill.

```tsx file=Generic agent
function Layout() {
  posthog.group('organization', crypto.randomUUID())   // every render, new key
  return <Outlet />
}
```

```tsx file=Wizard
// on login or org switch only, with the stable org id
posthog.group('organization', org.id, { name: org.name, plan: org.plan })
// groupIdentify only when group properties actually change
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Statsig config mapped to a flag return value</summary>

**Problem.** A one-to-one rename from getConfig to getFeatureFlag looks right. getFeatureFlag returns a variant key or boolean, not a bag of values.

**Impact.** Config values come back undefined, so the defaults silently win.

```js file=Generic agent
const limit = statsig.getConfig('checkout').get('limit', 10)
// migrated to:
const limit = posthog.getFeatureFlag('checkout').limit ?? 10
```

```js file=Wizard
const limit = (posthog.getFeatureFlagResult('checkout')?.payload ?? {})['limit'] ?? 10
// experiment variant: posthog.getFeatureFlag('my-exp') ?? fallback
// React: useFeatureFlagPayload / useFeatureFlagVariantKey
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Server logs sent as analytics events</summary>

**Problem.** posthog.capture is the API the agent already knows, so forwarding log lines through it seems natural. PostHog logs actually ingest over OTLP.

**Impact.** Logs land as analytics events (billed and queried as events), not in PostHog Logs, so log search does not find them.

```js file=Generic agent
// winston logger piped into analytics events
logger.on('data', (l) =>
  posthog.capture({ distinctId: 'server', event: 'app_log', properties: l }))
```

```js file=Wizard
// Configure an OTLP log exporter pointed at PostHog's logs ingest endpoint
// (token and endpoint from env vars); prefer structured key-value logging
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>OTLP exporter added to mobile apps</summary>

**Problem.** The generic answer for PostHog logs is OTLP, so the agent adds an OpenTelemetry exporter to mobile apps too. The mobile SDKs have their own log capture.

**Impact.** An extra dependency and a parallel logging path next to the SDK's own log capture.

```text file=Generic agent
// iOS/Android/RN app
// add OpenTelemetry SDK + OTLP exporter
endpoint = "https://us.i.posthog.com/i/v1/logs"
```

```swift file=Wizard
// posthog-ios >= 3.58.0: set config.logs fields before setup,
PostHogSDK.shared.setup(config)
// then capture with PostHogSDK.shared.logger or captureLog
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Generic OpenTelemetry instead of posthog-node traces</summary>

**Problem.** Told to add tracing, the agent reaches for the OpenTelemetry SDK from general knowledge. It cannot know that posthog-node 5.52.0 and later exports spans itself.

**Impact.** Needless OTel dependencies and wiring when the installed client already exports spans.

```bash file=Generic agent
npm install @opentelemetry/sdk-node @opentelemetry/exporter-trace-otlp-http
# manual NodeSDK + exporter wiring, though posthog-node >= 5.52.0 is installed
```

```ts file=Wizard
// posthog-node >= 5.52.0: set the `traces` option on the existing client
const posthog = new PostHog(token, { host, traces: { serviceName: 'api' } })
// wrap operations in withSpan (startSpan when a callback can't wrap)
// OTel already in project: point its OTLP exporter at /i/v1/traces
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>OTel exporter added where Python native spans exist</summary>

**Problem.** The tracing skill used to say only posthog-node could send spans without OpenTelemetry, so Python installs always got an OTel exporter pipeline. AsyncPosthog has no span API, which makes this easy to get wrong in either direction.

**Impact.** Needless OTel dependencies and config on the sync client, or a broken setup if native spans are used on AsyncPosthog.

```python file=Generic agent
# posthog >= 7.58.0, sync Posthog client already in use
from opentelemetry.sdk.trace.export import BatchSpanProcessor
from opentelemetry.exporter.otlp.proto.http.trace_exporter import OTLPSpanExporter
provider.add_span_processor(BatchSpanProcessor(OTLPSpanExporter(
    endpoint=f"{host}/i/v1/traces",
    headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"})))
```

```python file=Wizard
# posthog >= 7.58.0, sync client (AsyncPosthog has no span API: keep OTel)
posthog = Posthog(token, host=host, traces={"service_name": "api"})
with posthog.start_span("charge_card"):
    ...
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Hand-built OpenTelemetry metrics instead of posthog.metrics</summary>

**Problem.** posthog.metrics is new, so the agent improvises an OTel meter and exporter even when a PostHog SDK is already installed.

**Impact.** Needless packages and auth setup, a parallel metrics pipeline, and drift from the supported API.

```python file=Generic agent
from opentelemetry.sdk.metrics import MeterProvider
from opentelemetry.exporter.otlp.proto.http.metric_exporter import OTLPMetricExporter
exporter = OTLPMetricExporter(endpoint="https://us.i.posthog.com/i/v1/metrics")
meter = MeterProvider(...).get_meter("app")   # posthog SDK already installed
```

```python file=Wizard
# PostHog SDK present: posthog.metrics (count / gauge / histogram),
# no OTel packages, metrics={"service_name": ...} at client init.
# OTLP only when the code already exports OTel metrics or the language has no SDK.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>OpenTelemetry chosen over PostHog LLM wrapper</summary>

**Problem.** Older PostHog AI docs and the wider ecosystem push OTel instrumentors for LLM tracing.

**Impact.** Single generations arrive, but the session &gt; trace &gt; span &gt; generation tree is hard to build and maintain, and the project gains extra OTel dependencies.

```python file=Generic agent
provider = TracerProvider(resource=Resource(attributes={SERVICE_NAME: 'my-app'}))
provider.add_span_processor(PostHogSpanProcessor(
    api_key=os.environ['POSTHOG_API_KEY'], host=os.environ['POSTHOG_HOST']))
trace.set_tracer_provider(provider)
OpenAIInstrumentor().instrument()
```

```python file=Wizard
from posthog import Posthog
from posthog.ai.openai import OpenAI
posthog = Posthog(os.environ['POSTHOG_API_KEY'], host=os.environ['POSTHOG_HOST'])
client = OpenAI(posthog_client=posthog)  # existing calls unchanged
# install only `posthog` (or @posthog/ai + posthog-node), no opentelemetry-*
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Generic instrumentor used instead of Agents SDK processor</summary>

**Problem.** An OpenAI app looks like a job for OpenAIInstrumentor. The OpenAI Agents SDK traces through its own layer, so the instrumentor installs and builds cleanly while capturing nothing.

**Impact.** Silent: the install succeeds, the build passes, instrumentation reports success, and no trace tree or AI events are captured.

```python file=Generic agent
from opentelemetry.instrumentation.openai import OpenAIInstrumentor
OpenAIInstrumentor().instrument()     # Agents SDK traces elsewhere; captures nothing
```

```python file=Wizard
from posthog.ai.openai_agents import instrument
instrument(client=posthog_client, properties={'$ai_session_id': session_id})
# the SDK's tracing layer emits the whole tree; no OTel, no manual spans
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Wrapped Gemini client used instead of ADK plugin</summary>

**Problem.** The model-wrapper pattern works for plain provider calls, so agents apply it to Google ADK too, or hand-write spans. ADK ships a PostHog plugin that already emits the agent structure.

**Impact.** The run structure is lost: no agent or tool spans, no per-invocation trace and no identity taken from ADK.

```ts file=Generic agent
import { GoogleGenAI } from '@posthog/ai/gemini'
const genai = new GoogleGenAI({ apiKey, posthog })  // wrapped Gemini client
// model calls only: loses ADK's agent, tool and invocation structure
const runner = new Runner({ agent: new LlmAgent({ name, model, tools }), appName, sessionService })
```

```ts file=Wizard
import { PostHogADKPlugin } from '@posthog/ai/adk'
const runner = new Runner({ agent, appName, sessionService,
  plugins: [new PostHogADKPlugin({ client: posthog })] })
// plugin emits $ai_trace, agent/tool $ai_span, $ai_generation
// userId -> distinct id, sessionId -> $ai_session_id; flush with posthog.shutdown()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Extra PostHog client or doubled AI instrumentation</summary>

**Problem.** General rules about the Posthog() constructor, shutdown and flushing are always in context, so the agent adds a client on OTel and framework-hook paths that export without one, or next to an existing client. It may also stack the wrapper client and an OTel instrumentor on one provider.

**Impact.** Dead client scaffolding and atexit shutdowns, a second sender next to the span processor, and double-reported generations.

```python file=Generic agent
# OTel path: the span processor needs only token + host
posthog = Posthog(token, host=host)      # app already has a client, or none is needed
atexit.register(posthog.shutdown)
# plus the wrapper client AND an OTel instrumentor on the same provider
```

```text file=Wizard
# Only wrapper-client and manual-capture paths construct a PostHog client.
# OTel/framework-hook paths: register the span processor or hook only; the
# Posthog() constructor, shutdown and flush rules do not apply. Never invent
# a client. If the project already has one, reuse it and its env-var names.
# Wrapper variants: do not add OpenTelemetry packages.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Vercel AI calls without experimental_telemetry enabled</summary>

**Problem.** The agent wires NodeSDK with the PostHog span processor and stops. Vercel AI emits spans only when each call opts in, and the flag's 'experimental' name makes it easy to skip.

**Impact.** No generations reach PostHog, silently.

```ts file=Generic agent
const result = await generateText({ model: openai('gpt-5-mini'), prompt })
// NodeSDK + PostHogSpanProcessor configured, but no spans are emitted
```

```ts file=Wizard
const result = await generateText({
  model: openai('gpt-5-mini'),
  prompt,
  experimental_telemetry: { isEnabled: true, functionId: 'my-ai-function' },
})
// NodeSDK started once in instrumentation.ts with PostHogSpanProcessor only
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>AI spans silently dropped by the PostHog filter</summary>

**Problem.** Naming a span after the app's operation is normal OTel practice. The PostHog AI span processor forwards only spans whose name or an attribute starts with gen_ai., llm., ai. or traceloop., and drops the rest silently.

**Impact.** No error. Generations arrive with no root trace and tool-run spans disappear.

```go file=Generic agent
ctx, span := tracer.Start(ctx, "handle turn") // dropped: no gen_ai./llm./ai./traceloop. prefix
// tool run recorded as a plain span named after the tool
```

```go file=Wizard
ctx, span := tracer.Start(ctx, "gen_ai.turn")  // or ai.ask, or a gen_ai.* attribute
span.SetAttributes(attribute.String("$ai_session_id", sessionID))
// tool run: child span of the turn with gen_ai.* attributes
// ForceFlush/Shutdown the provider before exit; verify no 'trace with no root'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Raw thread id fails session id validation</summary>

**Problem.** Passing the app's existing conversation id straight through is the natural choice. The restricted character set of $ai_session_id is easy to miss, and failures are silent.

**Impact.** Silent: ids with a slash or a hash fail validation, so session grouping is lost for those conversations.

```python file=Generic agent
posthog_properties={'$ai_session_id': thread.id}
```

```python file=Wizard
# $ai_session_id allows letters, numbers and - _ ~ . @ ( ) ! ' : | only
session_id = re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9\-_~.@()!':|]", "-", thread.id)
posthog_properties={'$ai_session_id': session_id}
```

</details>

## Distinct IDs

<details>
<summary>Email used as the distinct id</summary>

**Problem.** Email is the one field always in scope, and PostHog's own server docs examples passed session.user.email or identify_context(user.email), so the agent copies it even when a primary key exists. Nothing fails at runtime.

**Impact.** Silent: PII becomes every event's identity, a changed email splits one person in two, and server events never merge with the frontend's id-based person. Same for posthog-rails, where posthog_distinct_id must return id.to_s.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.identify(user.email)
// server
posthog.capture({ distinctId: session.user.email, event: 'checkout_started' })
# python
with new_context():
    identify_context(user.email)
    tag('email', user.email)
```

```ts file=Wizard
// stable id from auth as the distinct id, PII as person properties
posthog.identify(String(user.id), { email: user.email, name: user.name })
# python
with new_context():
    identify_context(str(user.id))
    capture('user_logged_in', properties={'$set': {'email': user.email}})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Visible placeholder when no stable id reaches</summary>

**Problem.** When accounts exist but the id is not in scope at a call site (jobs, webhooks), the agent guesses email or 'unknown' to finish the task, and then mentions it only as a vague caveat, if at all.

**Impact.** Events attach to the wrong person or nobody, and the user cannot find which call sites to fix.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.capture({
  distinctId: user?.email ?? 'unknown',
  event: 'export_started',
});
// report: "Note: some events may not be fully attributed to users."
```

```ts file=Wizard
// identity exists, no stable id reaches this call site
posthog.capture({ distinctId: 'DISTINCT_ID', event: 'invoice_paid' });
// report, own issue:
// Replace DISTINCT_ID in src/jobs/billing.ts:42, src/jobs/mail.ts:17
// Cost if left alone: those events belong to nobody.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Per-login or per-session id passed to identify</summary>

**Problem.** Session ids and UUIDs are always in scope and unique, so they look like valid user ids. Mock auth objects even hand the agent a crypto.randomUUID() per login. The reset happens later and silently.

**Impact.** Every login, session or device creates a new person, so retention, per-user funnels and cross-device counts break. Silent.

```ts file=Generic agent
// mock auth: credentials.id = crypto.randomUUID() on each login
this.posthogService.posthog.identify(credentials.id, {
  username: credentials.username,
  email: credentials.email,
})
// also: posthog.identify(session.sessionId), posthog.identify(req.cookies.sid)
```

```ts file=Wizard
// stable app id (primary key, uuid, resource name), never regenerated per login
posthog.identify(stableUserId, { username, email })
// audit: error when the id sources from a session, request or device id that resets
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Capture instrumented before identity is settled</summary>

**Problem.** Event instrumentation and identify look independent, so they run in parallel. Capture then invents its own attribution at each call site because it never saw how identity is bound.

**Impact.** Silent: some events are double-wrapped, others personless or under a guessed id, depending on ordering.

```yaml file=Generic agent
# seed graph before
identify  dependsOn: [install, init]
capture   dependsOn: [install, init]   # races identify
# capture writes bare calls or its own identify_context per call
```

```yaml file=Wizard
# capture.md frontmatter
dependsOn: [install, init, identify]
# read the identify handoff first: identity established -> plain capture;
# not established -> tag the call with distinct id and session id;
# identity exists but no id reaches -> DISTINCT_ID placeholder and say so
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Audit wrongly demands identify() on server SDKs</summary>

**Problem.** Client SDK habits make an auditor expect an identify() call, but server SDKs identify per call. The real failure is a literal or missing distinctId, which the auditor can overlook.

**Impact.** False findings push agents to add pointless server identify calls while literal 'server' ids that pool all events go unflagged.

```ts file=Generic agent
client.capture({ distinctId: 'server', event: 'invoice_paid' })
// auditor: "FAIL: no identify() call found on server"
```

```ts file=Wizard
client.capture({ distinctId: user.id, event: 'invoice_paid' })
// server SDKs identify per call: pass if most rows have distinct_id_kind == variable;
// fail when the dominant pattern is "missing" or "literal"
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Rails job errors carry no user</summary>

**Problem.** posthog-rails auto-captures job failures, so the agent assumes attribution is automatic and never adds the posthog_distinct_id DSL to the job.

**Impact.** Silent: failed-job and exception events reach Error Tracking with no person attached.

```ruby file=Generic agent
class ExampleJob < ApplicationJob
  def perform(distinct_id, should_fail: false)
    raise 'Job failed'   # captured, but with no user attached
  end
end
```

```ruby file=Wizard
class ExampleJob < ApplicationJob
  posthog_distinct_id ->(distinct_id, *) { distinct_id }
  def perform(distinct_id, should_fail: false)
    raise StandardError, 'Example job failure' if should_fail
  end
end
# ExampleJob.perform_later(current_user.posthog_distinct_id)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>CLI generates a new id every run</summary>

**Problem.** A CLI has no login, so the agent reaches for uuid4() per call or the OS username, not realising the id must be persisted once per install.

**Impact.** Every invocation is a new person, so retention and per-user counts are meaningless. OS usernames add PII.

```python file=Generic agent
posthog.capture(
    distinct_id=str(uuid.uuid4()),   # new person on every call
    event="todo_added",
)
```

```python file=Wizard
def get_user_id():
    """Uses a UUID stored in the data file to represent this user."""
    if DATA_FILE.exists():
        data = json.loads(DATA_FILE.read_text())
        if 'user_id' in data:
            return data['user_id']
    return f"user_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"   # persist on first save
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Spans not joined to person or replay</summary>

**Problem.** Tracing works without identity, so spans show up and look fine. Joining them needs posthogDistinctId/sessionId attributes or reading the browser's tracing headers, which generic OTel setup never does.

**Impact.** Silent: spans appear in Tracing but cannot be tied to a person or a Session Replay recording.

```python file=Generic agent
with posthog.start_span("checkout"):   # no context: no distinct id, no session id
    process_order(order)
```

```python file=Wizard
with posthog.new_context():
    posthog.identify_context(user_id)
    posthog.set_context_session(session_id)
    with posthog.start_span("checkout"):
        process_order(order)
# Django: contexts middleware does this per request
# OTel: set attrs from X-POSTHOG-DISTINCT-ID / X-POSTHOG-SESSION-ID headers
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Per-request identity on OTel Resource attributes</summary>

**Problem.** OTel examples put attributes on the Resource, so the agent places userId and threadId there, or starts the SDK inside the handler to get them in scope.

**Impact.** Silent: every request in the process shares one identity, or the SDK is re-created per request.

```ts file=Generic agent
// inside POST /api/chat
new NodeSDK({ resource: resourceFromAttributes({
  'posthog.distinct_id': userId, '$ai_session_id': threadId,
}) }).start()
```

```ts file=Wizard
// instrumentation.ts: PostHogSpanProcessor started once, no identity on the Resource
// route handler: identity per request
generateText({ model, prompt,
  experimental_telemetry: { isEnabled: true,
    metadata: { posthog_distinct_id: userId, $ai_session_id: threadId } } })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Marketing site and app split identity</summary>

**Problem.** Identical init on both hosts looks correct. The split into two anonymous profiles is silent. Caveat: posthog-js usually defaults cross_subdomain_cookie to true, so the split mainly appears when it is off, on a public-suffix host, or the cookie domain is set too narrowly. The audit rule does not state this.

**Impact.** UTMs on the marketing host never reach the app-side signup.

```js file=Generic agent
// example.com and app.example.com
posthog.init(KEY, { api_host: HOST })
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(KEY, {
  api_host: HOST,
  cross_subdomain_cookie: true,
})   // if cookie_domain is set, use the parent: .example.com
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Identify reads a field the backend never shares</summary>

**Problem.** The agent assumes the obvious bridged field (Inertia props.auth.user, next-auth session.user.id) exists because most apps have it. next-auth leaves session.user.id empty by default, and nothing errors when it is undefined, so agents paper over it with a fallback and the gap stays invisible without checking the server side.

**Impact.** Silent: identify never fires, fires with undefined, or falls back to a shared literal, so every user stays anonymous or merges onto one person.

```js file=Generic agent
// resources/js/app.js (Inertia)
router.on('navigate', (e) => {
  const user = e.detail.page.props.auth?.user   // monica never shares auth.user
  if (user) posthog.identify(String(user.id))
})
// next-auth: getServerSession(req, res) without authOptions -> session.user.id undefined
posthog.identify(session?.user?.id ?? 'anonymous')
```

```php file=Wizard
// Prove the id reaches the line; expose it at the source if missing
public function share(Request $request): array
{
    return array_merge(parent::share($request), [
        'auth' => ['user' => $request->user()?->only('id', 'email', 'name')],
    ]);
}
// next-auth: getServerSession(req, res, authOptions) + session callback sets session.user.id = token.sub
// then: if (session?.user?.id) posthog.identify(session.user.id)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Coerced missing id becomes the string undefined</summary>

**Problem.** Wrapping the id in String() or a cast looks like type hygiene and keeps the call running. The agent never notices the result is the literal 'undefined' shared by everyone.

**Impact.** Every user is identified as 'undefined' (or '') and pooled onto one person. Silent.

```ts file=Generic agent
onSuccess: (page) => {
  const user = page.props.auth?.user
  if (user) posthog.identify(String(user.id))   // user exists, id absent -> 'undefined'
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
// never pass a value that can be undefined; never substitute an email
if (!user?.id) return
posthog.identify(user.id, { email: user.email })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PHP cast before ?? yields an empty id</summary>

**Problem.** (string) binds tighter than ??, so (string) null becomes '' which is not null. The fallback chain reads correctly but never falls through.

**Impact.** Events and exceptions are captured with an empty distinct id. Silent.

```php file=Generic agent
$distinctId = $distinctId
    ?? (string) Auth::user()?->id
    ?? 'anonymous'; // (string) null === "", never reaches here
```

```php file=Wizard
$userId = Auth::user()?->id;
$distinctId = $distinctId
    ?? ($userId !== null ? (string) $userId : null);
// no user: skip person attribution, never a shared literal
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Literal anonymous fallback pools all users</summary>

**Problem.** A ?? 'anonymous' fallback or a constant 'server'/'backend' id satisfies the SDK's required distinctId and the type checker, so it looks like defensive coding and never errors. The pooled person only shows up later in data.

**Impact.** Silent: every logged-out visitor or unattributed server event lands on one giant person, corrupting counts, funnels, retention and person merges.

```ts file=Generic agent
signIn: async ({ user, account }) => {
  const distinctId = user?.id ?? user?.email ?? "anonymous";
  posthog.identify({ distinctId, properties: { name: user?.name, email: user?.email } });
  posthog.capture({ distinctId, event: "server_sign_in" });
}
// go
client.Enqueue(posthog.Capture{DistinctId: "server", Event: "order_created"})
```

```ts file=Wizard
callbacks: {
  session({ session, token }) {
    if (session.user && typeof token.sub === "string") session.user.id = token.sub;
    return session;
  },
},
// identify only when the stable id is a string; otherwise skip and stay anonymous
if (typeof session.user?.id === 'string') posthog.identify(session.user.id, { email: session.user.email })
// truly system-level: no person profile
posthog.capture({ distinctId: 'cron', event: 'nightly_sync', properties: { $process_person_profile: false } })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Accountless apps get personless events, not fake ids</summary>

**Problem.** Docs examples always pass a distinctId and agents treat identify as a required step, so for an app with no login the agent invents identify('user'), a constant 'anonymous', or ships the DISTINCT_ID placeholder instead of capturing personless.

**Impact.** Silent: every visitor collapses into one made-up person, identification is claimed that does not exist, or a placeholder ships to production.

```ts file=Generic agent
// app has no accounts or login
posthog.identify('user')                                   // invented
posthog.capture({ distinctId: 'anonymous', event: 'newsletter_subscribed' })
// or a placeholder shipped to production
posthog.capture({ distinctId: 'DISTINCT_ID', event: 'file_uploaded' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
// App has no accounts: identify says so and stops; capture is personless
posthog.capture({ distinctId: randomUUID(), event: 'newsletter_subscribed',
  properties: { $process_person_profile: false } })
// handoff: events are personless because the app has no identity
// DISTINCT_ID placeholder only when identity exists but no stable id reaches the call site
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Example READMEs still teach email ids</summary>

**Problem.** The agent anchors on shipped example READMEs, which still pass user.email even though most of the Flask/FastAPI example code was fixed, so the reference contradicts the commandment. Laravel code itself still uses email too.

**Impact.** The agent may pick the README shape and ship PII distinct ids. Silent.

```php file=Generic agent
// example-apps/laravel/README.md (ships into references/EXAMPLE.md)
$posthog->identify($user->email, $user->getPostHogProperties());
$posthog->capture($user->email, 'user_logged_in');
# example-apps/fastapi/README.md
identify_context(user.email)
```

```python file=Wizard
# the code in the same Python examples
identify_context(str(user.id))          # fastapi app/middleware.py, routers/main.py
identify_context(str(current_user.id))  # flask app/api/routes.py
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Agent adds a schema migration to get an id</summary>

**Problem.** When no obvious stable key exists, an ambitious agent 'fixes' it by adding a userId column, which feels thorough but is an unrequested change to the user's database.

**Impact.** Unrequested migration in the user's app for an analytics task. Review burden and risk.

```sql file=Generic agent
ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN userId TEXT;
-- then: posthog.identify(user.userId)
```

```text file=Wizard
The distinct id is the app's own stable identifier for the user - a primary key,
a uuid, a resource name. Not an email and not a username: those are PII and they
change; send them as person properties instead. Fall back to email only when the
app truly has no other stable key, and say so in your handoff - do not invent one
by altering the app's schema.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Server events use a different id than the browser</summary>

**Problem.** Server SDKs require a distinctId on each call, so the agent fills it from whatever is local (username, IP, body field, email, random UUID) and never forwards the browser's distinct id and session id.

**Impact.** Silent: one user becomes two persons and server events are missing from the browser session and its replay.

```ts file=Generic agent
// client identifies with user.id
fetch('/api/checkout', { method: 'POST' })
// server
posthog.capture({ distinctId: crypto.randomUUID(), event: 'checkout_completed' })
// or distinctId: username / req.ip / user.email / a body field
```

```ts file=Wizard
// browser: init with tracing_headers: ['api.example.com'] adds both headers
// middleware: bind the browser's ids once per request
const sessionId = request.headers.get('X-POSTHOG-SESSION-ID')
const distinctId = request.headers.get('X-POSTHOG-DISTINCT-ID')
return posthog.withContext(
  { sessionId: sessionId ?? undefined, distinctId: distinctId ?? undefined }, next)
// route handler: no distinctId argument
posthog.capture({ event: 'burrito_considered' })
```
