</details>

<details>
<summary>Form API route keys events on submitted email</summary>

**Problem.** On a marketing site with no login, the submitted email is the only identifier in the handler, so the agent uses it (or 'anonymous', or omits distinctId) instead of reading the forwarded browser id.

**Impact.** Server events fragment across emails or pool onto one person, never join the browser person, and with no distinctId the capture fails.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.capture({
  distinctId: distinctId || data.email || 'anonymous',
  event: 'contact_form_received',
  properties: { name: data.name },
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
const distinctId = request.headers.get('X-POSTHOG-DISTINCT-ID') ?? undefined;
const sessionId = request.headers.get('X-POSTHOG-SESSION-ID') ?? undefined;
// ids come from the browser's tracing_headers; never email, 'anonymous' or a random UUID
posthog.withContext({ distinctId, sessionId }, () =>
  posthog.capture({ event: 'contact_form_received', properties: { source: 'api' } }))
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Stripe metadata gets a different id than identify</summary>

**Problem.** Stripe code already has a customer id, org id, client_reference_id or email in scope, and the agent assumes one of them is the PostHog distinct id instead of finding what posthog.identify() is called with. It often also never captures in the webhook.

**Impact.** Silent: revenue never joins to the person who paid, Top Customers and persons_revenue_analytics stay empty or wrong, and the bad metadata blocks correct identification later.

```ts file=Generic agent
// app calls posthog.identify(user.email, ...)
const session = await stripe.checkout.sessions.create({
  client_reference_id: org.id,
  metadata: { posthog_person_distinct_id: user.stripeCustomerId },
})
// or: posthog_person_distinct_id: user.distinct_id || user.email
// webhook: console.log(event.type)   // no posthog.capture
```

```ts file=Wizard
// find it first: posthog.identify(<first arg>, ...) is the distinct_id
// never infer it from client_reference_id (it MAY NOT be the distinct_id)
const session = await stripe.checkout.sessions.create({
  client_reference_id: distinctId,   // set so webhooks can read it
  metadata: { posthog_person_distinct_id: distinctId }, // threaded from a caller
})
// webhook (posthog-node)
posthog.capture({ distinctId, event: 'invoice_paid',
  properties: { revenue: invoice.amount_paid, currency: invoice.currency } })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Placeholder distinct id written to Stripe</summary>

**Problem.** A TODO string keeps the code compiling and looks like a helpful marker, and an earlier skill version even suggested it.

**Impact.** Silent: every customer is tagged with the same fake id, nothing joins, and the missing identity is hidden.

```ts file=Generic agent
const customer = await stripe.customers.create({
  email,
  metadata: { posthog_person_distinct_id: 'TODO_POSTHOG_DISTINCT_ID' },
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
// distinct_id not in scope: thread it from a caller as an optional parameter.
// No caller has it: skip the call site and leave a TODO comment.
// No distinct_id anywhere: [ABORT] Could not find a PostHog distinct_id
// (Tenet 1 still says a TODO_POSTHOG_DISTINCT_ID string is the fallback)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Revenue setup aborts when users are never identified</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to tag Stripe customers, a generic agent invents an id mapping (often email) even though the app never calls identify, so the tag points at no real person.

**Impact.** Revenue data never joins to real persons. Wrong data, no error.

```js file=Generic agent
stripe.customers.create({
  email,
  metadata: { posthog_distinct_id: email },   // invented, no identify() anywhere
})
```

```text file=Wizard
[ABORT] Could not find a PostHog distinct_id
-> "Your users must be identified in PostHog before they can be tagged in Stripe."
[ABORT] Could not find a Stripe integration
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Elixir context lost in spawned processes</summary>

**Problem.** PostHog.set_context looks like request-wide state, but it is Logger metadata scoped to one BEAM process, which agents from other runtimes don't expect.

**Impact.** Silent: captures from a Task or job carry no distinct_id or request context, and capture returns an error nobody checks.

```elixir file=Generic agent
PostHog.set_context(%{distinct_id: user.id})   # plug process
Task.start(fn ->
  PostHog.capture("export_finished")           # other process: no context
end)
```

```elixir file=Wizard
Task.start(fn ->
  PostHog.set_context(%{distinct_id: user.id}) # set in the capturing process
  PostHog.capture("export_finished")
end)
# or pass distinct_id explicitly on the capture
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Hardcoded user id on LLM events</summary>

**Problem.** The skill's own manual-capture example hardcoded distinct_id='user_123', and agents copy it when no user is in scope.

**Impact.** Silent: every generation attributes to one fake person, corrupting person-level LLM analytics.

```python file=Generic agent
posthog.capture(
    distinct_id='user_123',
    event='$ai_generation',
    properties={'$ai_model': 'gpt-4', '$ai_provider': 'openai'},
)
```

```text file=Wizard
| posthog_distinct_id | the user | the person |

If the app has no user id, leave `posthog_distinct_id` out.
Anonymous is a finding for the report. Do not invent an id.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Invented ids for MCP tool calls</summary>

**Problem.** Stateless MCP transports have no obvious user in scope, so the agent fills distinctId with a constant and sessionId with a fresh UUID to satisfy the call.

**Impact.** Silent: every call goes to one fake person, or each call becomes a new session, so per-user MCP analytics are wrong.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.captureToolCall({
  toolName: request.params.name,
  distinctId: 'anonymous',
  sessionId: crypto.randomUUID(),   // new session every call
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
// Path B, stateless mcp-handler: group calls by user
instrument(server, posthog, {
  identify: async (request, extra) => {
    const token = getRequestHeaders(extra)?.["authorization"]
    return token ? { distinctId: await resolveUserId(token) } : null
  },
})
// Path C custom dispatcher: resolve from existing auth/session; omit rather than invent
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Stripe person link only on Customer creation</summary>

**Problem.** Adding metadata to Customer.create looks like it links the person everywhere. Revenue rows come from PaymentIntents, Subscriptions, Invoices and webhook-created customers, which are separate objects.

**Impact.** Silent. Revenue created through the untouched objects has no person link, so most revenue never joins.

```python file=Generic agent
customer = stripe.Customer.create(email=email,
    metadata={'posthog_person_distinct_id': distinct_id})
intent = stripe.PaymentIntent.create(amount=amount, currency='usd', customer=customer.id)
sub = stripe.Subscription.create(customer=customer.id, items=items)
```

```python file=Wizard
meta = {'posthog_person_distinct_id': distinct_id}
intent = stripe.PaymentIntent.create(amount=amount, currency='usd',
    customer=customer.id, metadata=meta)
sub = stripe.Subscription.create(customer=customer.id, items=items, metadata=meta)
```

</details>

## Identify and reset

<details>
<summary>Login tracked but user never identified</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to 'track login', a generic agent adds a user_logged_in capture and stops. Nothing errors, and events still show up, so the missing identify is invisible until someone looks for per-user data.

**Impact.** Silent: every event stays anonymous, so per-user funnels, retention and replay attribution do not work.

```js file=Generic agent
async function login(username) {
  await auth.login(username)
  posthog.capture('user_logged_in')   // no identify
}
```

```js file=Wizard
await auth.login(username)
// person properties go in identify(), not in capture()
posthog.identify(username, { name: username })
posthog.capture('user_logged_in')
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Returning logged-in users never identified</summary>

**Problem.** Docs and most example apps show identify() inside the login handler, so the agent wires it there. Sessions restored from a cookie, stored credentials or app restart skip that handler, and nothing signals the gap.

**Impact.** Silent: returning sessions (new tab, cleared PostHog storage, new device with a live server session) stay on anonymous distinct ids, splitting one user across anonymous and identified persons.

```js file=Generic agent
async function onSubmit(values) {
  const user = await login(values)
  posthog.identify(user.id, { email: user.email })   // only path
}
// cookie-restored sessions auto-redirect past this handler
```

```tsx file=Wizard
// on login success AND on any load where a session already exists
useEffect(() => {
  if (user && posthog.get_distinct_id() !== user.id) {
    posthog.identify(user.id, { email: user.email })
  }
}, [user])
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>MCP identify reads headers with old SDK shape</summary>

**Problem.** Training data shows the v1 MCP SDK shape extra.requestInfo.headers. On v2 the request moved to extra.http.req, and the stale read returns undefined without throwing.

**Impact.** Silent: identify() returns null and every MCP event goes out anonymous with no error anywhere.

```ts file=Generic agent
instrument(server, posthog, {
  identify: (request, extra) => ({
    distinctId: extra.requestInfo?.headers?.['x-user-id'],   // v1-shaped read
  }),
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
import { instrument, getRequestHeaders } from '@posthog/mcp'
instrument(server, posthog, {
  identify: async (request, extra) => {
    const token = getRequestHeaders(extra)?.['authorization']
    return token ? { distinctId: await resolveUserId(token) } : null
  },
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Person properties set on every capture</summary>

**Problem.** $set on capture is documented and works, so the agent attaches it everywhere, including first-touch values that should be written only once.

**Impact.** Person-property versions bloat, and first-touch values such as initial referrer get overwritten. Nothing reports it.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.capture('page_viewed', {
  $set: { initial_referrer: document.referrer, plan: user.plan }
})
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.identify(user.id,
  { plan: user.plan },                                  // $set: changing values
  { initial_referrer: document.referrer, signup_date }  // $set_once: first touch
)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Logout never resets the PostHog identity</summary>

**Problem.** The agent treats logout as app auth state only, clearing its own storage and maybe capturing user_logged_out, without knowing PostHog keeps its own distinct id. The bleed only shows when a second person uses the same device.

**Impact.** Silent: the next user on the same browser or device inherits the previous person, and their identify() can merge both accounts into one person.

```js file=Generic agent
async function signOut() {
  await fetch('/api/logout', { method: 'POST' })  // server capture only
  setUser(null)
  localStorage.removeItem('currentUser')   // PostHog id untouched
  router.push('/login')
}
```

```js file=Wizard
async function signOut() {
  posthog.capture('user_logged_out')
  await fetch('/api/logout', { method: 'POST' })
  // IMPORTANT: Reset the PostHog instance to clear the user session
  posthog.reset()   // also before identify() when switching accounts directly
  router.push('/login')
}
// iOS: PostHogSDK.shared.reset()  Flutter: await Posthog().reset()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Reset runs for anonymous visitors on page load</summary>

**Problem.** Told to 'reset when logged out', the agent keys reset() on 'no session' or 'no user' state, which is also true on every first visit. The over-correction looks right in review and fails silently.

**Impact.** Silent: every visit mints a new anonymous id, detaching pre-signup pageviews from the account that follows and inflating unique visitors.

```js file=Generic agent
supabase.auth.onAuthStateChange((_event, session) => {
  if (!session) posthog.reset()   // also true on every anonymous page load
})
// or
useEffect(() => { if (!user) posthog.reset() }, [user])
```

```js file=Wizard
} else if (status === 'unauthenticated' && identifiedUserId.current) {
  posthog.reset()            // only the identified-to-anonymous transition
  identifiedUserId.current = null
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Reset called right before identify on login</summary>

**Problem.** Resetting 'to start clean' before identifying feels defensive, but the SDK only links an anonymous predecessor during identify, and reset has just thrown it away.

**Impact.** Silent: the visitor's pre-login activity is never linked to the user. Identify links a freshly minted, empty anonymous id.

```js file=Generic agent
function onLogin(user) {
  posthog.reset()              // discards the original anonymous id
  posthog.identify(user.id)    // links a brand-new anonymous id
}
```

```js file=Wizard
function onLogin(user)  { posthog.identify(user.id) }  // links the existing anonymous id
function onLogout()     { posthog.reset() }            // only on logout or account switch
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Identify fires on every navigation or render</summary>

**Problem.** Following 'identify on login AND on refresh', the agent puts identify in a component body or an effect that mounts on every page, or strips an existing dedupe guard as 'redundant'. Nothing breaks, it just floods events.

**Impact.** A redundant identify with a full $set payload on every navigation or render, and identify can run before the user id is known.

```tsx file=Generic agent
export function PostHogIdentify({ userId, email }) {
  useEffect(() => {
    posthog.identify(userId, email ? { email } : undefined)   // guard removed
  }, [userId, email])
}
// or: useEffect(() => { if (user) posthog.identify(user.id) })  // no deps
```

```tsx file=Wizard
if (identifiedUserId.current !== session.user.id) {
  if (identifiedUserId.current) posthog.reset()
  posthog.identify(session.user.id, { email, name })
  identifiedUserId.current = session.user.id
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Identify called late, after captures and flags</summary>

**Problem.** The agent drops identify wherever user data is handy (a settings or profile page mount) because the docs only say 'call identify', not when relative to other captures and flag evaluations. Nothing fails when early calls go out anonymous.

**Impact.** Silent: startup captures and flag evaluations run anonymously, so flags targeted by person properties return the wrong value and events are split from the person.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, { api_host: '/ingest' })
const beta = posthog.isFeatureEnabled('beta')   // anonymous
posthog.capture('app_opened')
// identify() only when the settings page mounts
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(token, { api_host: '/ingest' })
// on login / on load for a signed-in user, before captures and flags
posthog.identify(user.id, { email: user.email })
posthog.onFeatureFlags(() => {
  const beta = posthog.isFeatureEnabled('beta')
})
posthog.capture('app_opened')
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Logout event captured after reset</summary>

**Problem.** Order looks cosmetic, so agents reset first as 'clean up then log' (or capture login before identify). Reset swaps the distinct id immediately, so the event lands on the wrong person with no error. Even the javascript-web example app resets first.

**Impact.** Silent: user_logged_out belongs to a fresh anonymous id, and a login event captured before identify belongs to the pre-login visitor.

```js file=Generic agent
function handleLogout() {
  posthog.reset()                    // wipes the distinct id first
  posthog.capture('user_logged_out') // lands on the new anonymous id
  localStorage.removeItem('currentUser')
}
// Flutter: await Posthog().reset(); await Posthog().capture(eventName: 'user_logged_out');
```

```dart file=Wizard
// login: identify, then capture
await Posthog().identify(userId: id, userProperties: props);
await Posthog().capture(eventName: 'user_logged_in');
// logout: capture first, so the event is still attributed to the user
await Posthog().capture(eventName: 'user_logged_out');
await Posthog().reset();
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>next-auth session has no user id</summary>

**Problem.** The agent writes posthog.identify(session.user.id) from habit, but next-auth's default session callback returns only name, email and image, so the id is undefined.

**Impact.** Silent: the SDK rejects the undefined id and nobody is ever identified.

```js file=Generic agent
// pages/_app.jsx
posthog.identify(session.user.id)   // session.user = { name, email, image }
// callbacks.session returns session unchanged
```

```js file=Wizard
callbacks: {
  session({ session, token }) {
    if (session.user && typeof token.sub === 'string') session.user.id = token.sub
    return session
  },
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Django login event captured as nobody</summary>

**Problem.** The agent trusts PosthogContextMiddleware and writes a bare capture after login(), not knowing the middleware read request.user before the view ran. The obvious over-correction, wrapping every capture in its own context, adds churn.

**Impact.** Silent: login and signup events (the key funnel steps) are personless while later requests look fine.

```python file=Generic agent
def login_view(request):
    user = authenticate(request, **creds)
    login(request, user)
    capture('user_logged_in')   # context identified as nobody before login
```

```python file=Wizard
@receiver(user_logged_in)
def identify_posthog_user(sender, request, user, **kwargs):
    identify_context(str(user.pk))  # fixes the ambient context for this request
    posthog.set(distinct_id=str(user.pk),
                properties={'email': user.email, 'username': user.username})
# apps.py ready(): from . import signals  # noqa: F401
# the view's plain capture('user_logged_in') is now attributed
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>FastAPI login event uses pre-login context</summary>

**Problem.** The request context was built from the cookie before the user logged in, and the agent captures under it without realising the handler is what creates the session.

**Impact.** Silent: user_logged_in and user_signed_up are attributed to nobody.

```python file=Generic agent
if user:
    user.record_login(db)
    capture("user_logged_in")   # middleware context has no user yet
    response.set_cookie("session_token", create_session_token(user.id))
```

```python file=Wizard
if user:
    is_new_user = user.record_login(db)
    with new_context():
        identify_context(str(user.id))
        capture("user_logged_in", properties={
            "$set": {"email": user.email, "is_staff": user.is_staff},
            "is_new_user": is_new_user})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Server logout event captured after session ends</summary>

**Problem.** Logging after the action feels natural, and the agent does not notice that the distinct id is read from the session it just cleared. The capture still succeeds, so nothing fails.

**Impact.** Silent: the logout event falls back to an anonymous or missing distinct id, so logouts are not attributed to the user.

```elixir file=Generic agent
def logout(conn, _params) do
  conn = delete_session(conn, :distinct_id)
  PostHog.capture("user_logged_out", %{distinct_id: current_distinct_id(conn)}) # now anonymous
  redirect(conn, to: ~p"/")
end
# Flask: logout_user(); capture('user_logged_out')
```

```python file=Wizard
# Flask: capture logout event before session ends
with new_context():
    identify_context(str(current_user.id))
    capture("user_logged_out")

logout_user()
# Django: capture before calling logout()
# Elixir: capture before delete_session(conn, :distinct_id)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Python context opened around every capture</summary>

**Problem.** SDK examples show new_context() and identify_context() next to a single capture, so agents copy that block to every call site instead of binding identity once per request.

**Impact.** Boilerplate at every call site. Any route that misses it, or any capture outside the wrapper, is personless or mis-attributed.

```python file=Generic agent
@router.post('/burrito/consider')
async def consider(current_user: RequiredUser):
    with new_context():
        identify_context(current_user.email)
        capture('burrito_considered')
```

```python file=Wizard
class PostHogMiddleware:   # pure ASGI, one context per request
    async def __call__(self, scope, receive, send):
        user = self._get_user_from_scope(scope)
        with new_context():
            if user:
                identify_context(str(user.id))
            await self.app(scope, receive, send)
# routes just call capture(...)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Node errors captured without the user</summary>

**Problem.** The agent passes distinctId on each capture, which works for events, but the error handler's captureException has no user in scope, and nothing warns about it.

**Impact.** Silent: exceptions, and any capture that omits the id, carry no user.

```js file=Generic agent
app.post('/orders', (req, res) => {
  posthog.capture({ distinctId: req.user.id, event: 'order_created' })
})
app.use((err, req, res, next) => {
  posthog.captureException(err)   // no distinctId, no context
  next(err)
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
// middleware: bind identity to the whole request
await posthog.withContext(
  { sessionId: sessionId ?? undefined, distinctId: distinctId ?? undefined },
  next   // captures and exceptions inside inherit it
)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Users identified on the server, or twice</summary>

**Problem.** In SSR apps the agent cannot tell which files run client-side, so it calls identify wherever it first finds the user, often a server login route or both sides. Server identify never sees the browser's anonymous id, and nothing errors.

**Impact.** Silent: pre-login browser activity stays on a separate anonymous person, server events are not tied to the client's person or session, and duplicate identify calls split profiles.

```ts file=Generic agent
// server/api/auth/login.post.ts
posthog.identify({ distinctId: username, properties: { username } })
posthog.capture({ distinctId: username, event: 'server_login' })
// plus layout.tsx calling posthog.identify on every render
```

```ts file=Wizard
// client identifies once on login: posthog.identify(username)
const sessionId = getHeader(event, 'x-posthog-session-id')
const distinctId = getHeader(event, 'x-posthog-distinct-id')
posthog.capture({ distinctId, event: 'server_login',
  properties: { $session_id: sessionId } })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Rails browser and server identities never joined</summary>

**Problem.** The agent identifies with posthog-rails in the sessions controller and treats the job as done, leaving the posthog-js snippet in the layout without a matching identify. The README claims they link when ids match, but the example layout itself omits the client call.

**Impact.** Silent: browser pageviews and replays stay on an anonymous person while backend events land on the user, so the two halves never join.

```ruby file=Generic agent
# SessionsController#create (server)
PostHog.identify(distinct_id: user.posthog_distinct_id, properties: user.posthog_properties)
# layout: posthog.init(token, { person_profiles: 'identified_only' })  -- no posthog.identify
```

```html file=Wizard
<%# layout, after posthog.init, when signed in %>
<% if current_user %>
  <script>posthog.identify('<%= j current_user.posthog_distinct_id.to_s %>')</script>
<% end %>
<%# posthog-rails >= 3.9.0 also reads X-POSTHOG-DISTINCT-ID / X-POSTHOG-SESSION-ID %>
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>distinct_id stuffed into capture properties</summary>

**Problem.** Passing distinct_id as a property is a shortcut agents copy from server SDK habits, and the React Router 6 example app itself ships this shape, so it reads as sanctioned.

**Impact.** Silent: only that event goes to a username person. Pageviews, autocapture and replay stay on the anonymous id and are never merged.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.capture('user_logged_in', {
  username: username,
  distinct_id: username,   // per-event override, no $identify
})
// logout: capture only, no posthog.reset()
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.identify(username)
posthog.capture('user_logged_in')
// logout
posthog.capture('user_logged_out')
posthog.reset()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Two identify calls with different ids</summary>

**Problem.** With an auth provider UID and an internal user id both at hand, the agent identifies with each, not knowing the first call marks the device identified and blocks the second merge.

**Impact.** The anonymous-to-internal-id merge is blocked and the user splits across two profiles. Cited as 30k+ blocked merges/day at multi-SDK SaaS customers.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.identify(authProviderUid)
posthog.identify(internalUserId)
```

```js file=Wizard
// one identify with the stable internal id
posthog.identify(internalUserId, { email: user.email })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Server $set capture with no identify</summary>

**Problem.** Putting $set on a server capture makes the person show properties and look identified, so the agent assumes identification is done.

**Impact.** Silent: no $identify event fires and no anonymous-to-identified merge happens, leaving earlier anonymous activity orphaned.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.capture({
  distinctId: user.id,
  event: 'plan_changed',
  properties: { $set: { plan: 'pro' } },
})
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.identify({ distinctId: user.id, properties: { plan: 'pro' } })
// or confirm the client already identified this same distinct id
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Imports an auth library the app lacks</summary>

**Problem.** Training data pairs identify() with Clerk or Auth.js hooks, so the agent reaches for one to get a user id even when the project has no such dependency.

**Impact.** The build breaks on the missing import, or identify runs with an invented user id and splits persons.

```ts file=Generic agent
import { useUser } from '@clerk/nextjs'   // not a dependency
const { user } = useUser()
posthog.identify(user?.id, { email: user?.email })
```

```text file=Wizard
- When identity comes from framework-bridged state (Inertia or SSR shared props, a serialized session), confirm the backend actually shares that field - add the share server-side if missing - before identifying from it
- Call posthog.identify(userId, { email, name, role }) on login AND on page refresh if the user is already logged in
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Later tasks re-add identify already written</summary>

**Problem.** A fresh task agent sees only its direct dependency's handoff, so it does not know an earlier task already added init, identify or reset, and adds them again.

**Impact.** Duplicate PostHog.identify calls or setup in the same flow, or wasted turns rediscovering earlier work.

```kotlin file=Generic agent
// MainViewModel.login(): identify task
PostHog.identify(distinctId = username)
// capture task (saw only its direct dependency) adds it again
PostHog.identify(distinctId = username,
    userProperties = mapOf("username" to username))
```

```text file=Wizard
## Context from previous steps
### identify
- did: Added PostHog.identify() and PostHog.reset() in MainViewModel.kt.
- for you: Identify fires in MainViewModel.login() with
  distinctId = username. Reset fires in MainViewModel.logout().
- files: app/src/main/java/.../MainViewModel.kt
### capture   (also sees install + init via ancestorIds)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Redundant alias() added next to identify()</summary>

**Problem.** Older docs and other analytics tools need alias to link anonymous and known users, so the agent adds it to be safe.

**Impact.** Extra $create_alias events and self-alias calls that do nothing, with a false sense that identity is handled. Worse, a server-side alias of a browser anonymous UUID marks it identified and silently blocks the later client identify() merge.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.alias(user.id)
posthog.identify(user.id)
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.identify(user.id)
// anonymous -> identified merge is automatic on first identify()
// alias() only for narrow cases, e.g. server-only projects with no client SDK
```

</details>

## Event design

<details>
<summary>Same milestone captured in browser and API</summary>

**Problem.** Being thorough, or after being told to add server-side events, the agent captures in the API route without removing the browser capture for the same action. Nothing errors when the conversion counts twice.

**Impact.** Silent: each conversion counts twice and may split across two person profiles.

```ts file=Generic agent
// checkout.tsx
posthog.capture('purchase_completed', { total })
// api/checkout.ts
client.capture({ distinctId: 'server', event: 'purchase_completed' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
// accuracy-critical milestone: capture once, on the backend
posthogServer.capture({ distinctId: id, event: 'purchase_completed' })
// client keeps UI journey events only
posthog.capture('checkout_form_viewed')
// audit: "fires from posthog-js at file:line and posthog-node at file:line, risks 2x counting"
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Signup and purchase left to autocapture</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to add analytics, the agent instruments visible UI clicks or leans on autocapture, assuming it covers the main journey. The missing signup, activation and purchase events fail silently.

**Impact.** Silent: funnels and retention have no business milestones to anchor on, and autocapture dominates billing volume.

```ts file=Generic agent
async function handleSubscribe() {
  await api.subscribe(plan)   // no capture; relies on $autocapture click
}
posthog.capture('button_clicked', { id: 'nav-pricing' })
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.capture('user_signed_up', { signup_method: 'email' })
posthog.capture('project_created')              // activation
async function handleSubscribe() {
  await api.subscribe(plan)
  posthog.capture('subscription_started', { plan })
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>New trace id minted for every model call</summary>

**Problem.** The wrapper works without posthog_trace_id, so the agent leaves it out or mints uuid4() inline per call. The SDK then gives each generation its own trace and the split traces look fine one at a time.

**Impact.** Silent: one user turn splits into several single-generation traces, the tool-use turn has no depth, and the AI Observability tree never forms while the diff looks right.

```python file=Generic agent
r1 = client.messages.create(**req1, posthog_distinct_id=uid)    # tool_use call
r2 = client.messages.create(**req2, posthog_distinct_id=uid)    # final answer: new trace
client.chat.completions.create(model=m, messages=msgs, posthog_distinct_id=uid,
    posthog_trace_id=str(uuid.uuid4()))                         # new per call
```

```python file=Wizard
trace_id = str(uuid.uuid4())   # one per turn
r1 = client.messages.create(**req1, posthog_distinct_id=uid,
    posthog_trace_id=trace_id, posthog_properties=props)
r2 = client.messages.create(**req2, posthog_distinct_id=uid,
    posthog_trace_id=trace_id, posthog_properties=props)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Fabricated MCP initialize and hardcoded protocol version</summary>

**Problem.** In a custom dispatcher, calling captureInitialize per connection with a fixed protocolVersion is the simplest way to fill the fields. The agent does not know the newer revision has no initialize at all.

**Impact.** Fake initialize events on a revision that has none, and wrong revision labels on every event.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.captureInitialize({ clientName, clientVersion, distinctId,
  protocolVersion: '2025-11-25' })   // every client, any revision
```

```ts file=Wizard
// only on a real 2025-11-25 initialize handshake: captureInitialize(...)
// every capture: protocolVersion from session state (2025-11-25)
//   or the request's MCP-Protocol-Version header (2026-07-28)
// neither available -> omit it; never hardcode
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Report promises MCP events that never exist</summary>

**Problem.** Names like $mcp_tool_failed or $mcp_prompt_get sound plausible, so the agent captures or promises them without checking the SDK's event list.

**Impact.** Silent. The report and dashboards reference events that never arrive.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.capture('$mcp_tool_failed', { tool, error })
// report: "you'll see $mcp_resources_list and $mcp_prompt_get events"
```

```text file=Wizard
Check every event name in the final report against events.md.
Failed tools stay $mcp_tool_call with $mcp_is_error = true (maybe a sibling $exception).
Resource and prompt events are reserved, not emitted yet.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>First PR instruments every possible event</summary>

**Problem.** The agent finds many valuable events and adds them all, since more coverage looks better.

**Impact.** A first integration PR that touches everything is hard to review, and the event plan only keeps 50 events.

```text file=Generic agent
45 capture() calls across 30 files in the first integration diff,
no list of what was left out
```

```text file=Wizard
Around ten to fifteen events is a rough guide, not a quota.
More than fifteen valuable events is fine, but do not put them
all in this run. Instrument the core set now, and record the rest
as suggestions for the setup report.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Dashboard insights use guessed event names</summary>

**Problem.** The agent writing insights picks plausible names ('Signup Completed') from memory instead of the exact strings the capture step sent, and nothing errors when an insight filters on an event that never fires.

**Impact.** Silent: funnels and trends stay empty forever because the names never match what the code sends.

```json file=Generic agent
"series": [
  { "kind": "EventsNode", "event": "Signup Completed" },
  { "kind": "EventsNode", "event": "checkout_done" }
]
// code actually sends user_signed_up / order_placed
```

```text file=Wizard
# dashboard.md frontmatter: dependsOn: [capture]
# insight skill: "Use the exact event names from the capture step's handoff;
# never invent events that were not instrumented."
"series": [{ "kind": "EventsNode", "event": "user_signed_up" }]
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Planned events invisible in headless runs</summary>

**Problem.** The agent writes its event plan to a file, but only the interactive TUI read it. Headless and cloud runs dropped the plan with no error.

**Impact.** Custom events from the wizard did not appear in PostHog until real traffic arrived.

```text file=Generic agent
// agent writes .posthog-events.json: [{"event_name": "todo_created", ...}]
// headless run: nothing reads it -> no event definitions until the first real event
```

```ts file=Wizard
eventPlanFile: EVENT_PLAN_FILE,   // posthog-integration program
// shared runner watcher: first valid non-empty plan captured, bounded final
// refresh before the terminal session push; <=50 events, name <=400 chars
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>User actions captured from effects or render</summary>

**Problem.** Watching state in a useEffect (or calling capture in the component body) is a common React habit and the quickest way to bolt tracking on without touching handlers. The duplicate fires from StrictMode, re-renders and restored state are silent.

**Impact.** Silent over-counting: events fire on mount, on reload for a restored user, on re-render and twice under StrictMode, so page loads are logged as logins and some events have no user action behind them.

```tsx file=Generic agent
useEffect(() => {
  if (user) {
    posthog.identify(user.username)
    posthog.capture('user_logged_in')   // every mount, refresh, StrictMode run
  }
}, [user])
```

```tsx file=Wizard
const success = await login(username, password)   // submit handler
if (success) {
  posthog.identify(username, { name: username })
  posthog.capture('user_logged_in')
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Client-side route changes send no pageviews</summary>

**Problem.** A plain posthog.init looks complete because the first load produces a $pageview. The agent never tests a soft navigation, and older docs taught a hand-rolled PostHogPageView component.

**Impact.** Silent: only the first hard load generates a $pageview. Later router or View Transition navigations are lost. Setting the defaults option turns on history_change pageviews.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, { api_host: host })
// router push / View Transitions produce no $pageview
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(token, {
  api_host: host,
  capture_pageview: 'history_change',   // or defaults: '2026-01-30'
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Manual pageview on top of automatic pageviews</summary>

**Problem.** Older docs and training data show a PostHogPageView component or a $pageview effect on route change. The agent copies it without noticing that defaults already turn on history_change pageviews.

**Impact.** Silent: every navigation counts twice, inflating traffic, funnels and paths.

```tsx file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, { api_host: host, defaults: '2025-05-24' })
useEffect(() => {
  posthog.capture('$pageview')   // history_change already captures this
}, [pathname])
```

```tsx file=Wizard
posthog.init(token, { api_host: host, defaults: '2025-05-24' })
// history_change pageviews; no PostHogPageView component, no manual $pageview
// capture actions only
posthog.capture('invite_sent', { role })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Custom page_viewed events on mount</summary>

**Problem.** 'Track key screens' reads to an agent like 'add a viewed event per page'. It does not know PostHog already captures pageviews.

**Impact.** Duplicates automatic pageviews and floods the project with noise events instead of user actions.

```tsx file=Generic agent
useEffect(() => {
  posthog.capture('checkout_page_viewed', { path: location.pathname })
}, [location.pathname])
```

```tsx file=Wizard
// track actions only; pageviews are automatic
// (exception: a 'viewed' event at the top of a conversion funnel)
<button onClick={() => posthog.capture('checkout_submitted', { cart_size })}>
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Success event fires before the server confirms</summary>

**Problem.** The submit handler is the obvious place for a 'completed' event, so the agent captures at the top before awaiting the response. The happy-path test passes.

**Impact.** Silent: rejected or failed requests still count as signups or purchases in funnels.

```js file=Generic agent
async function onSubmit(values) {
  posthog.capture('signup_completed')   // before the server answers
  await fetch('/api/signup', { method: 'POST', body: JSON.stringify(values) })
}
```

```js file=Wizard
async function onSubmit(values) {
  const res = await fetch('/api/signup', { method: 'POST', body: JSON.stringify(values) })
  if (res.ok) posthog.capture('signup_completed')
}
// Server Actions: capture inside the action after success
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Events captured with no properties</summary>

**Problem.** capture('invoice_created') is the shortest call that works, the docs' first example has no properties, and it passes every check that only looks for capture calls. The agent does not think about how the event will be broken down later.

**Impact.** Silent: events can only be counted, never segmented, filtered or broken down.

```kotlin file=Generic agent
PostHog.capture("user_logged_in")
PostHog.capture("message_sent")
PostHog.capture("chat_selected")
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.capture({
  distinctId: req.userId,
  event: 'todo_created',
  properties: { todo_count: todos.length, has_due_date: Boolean(todo.dueDate) },
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Filler events invented to hit a count</summary>

**Problem.** Told to pick 10 to 15 events, the agent treats the range as a quota and pads a small app with actions nobody performs, or instruments everything in one huge first PR.

**Impact.** Silent: noise in every insight built on those events. Oversized first PRs are hard to review.

```ts file=Generic agent
// app has 4 meaningful actions; instructions said "10-15 events"
posthog.capture('settings_opened')
posthog.capture('sidebar_toggled')
posthog.capture('footer_link_clicked')   // invented to reach 10
```

```text file=Wizard
Around ten to fifteen is a rough guide, not a quota. Fewer is right when a small
project has only a few actions that matter - instrument those and stop; never
invent an event to reach a number, since an event nobody performs is noise in
every insight built on it. More than fifteen genuinely valuable events (auth,
payment, and the like) is also fine, but do not put them all in this run - a
first integration PR that touches everything is hard to review. Instrument the
core set now, and record the rest as suggestions for the setup report.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>New event names ignore the project's existing ones</summary>

**Problem.** The agent applies its own naming habit (Title Case, PascalCase, camelCase or its preferred snake_case verb) without scanning for capture calls already in the repo, so it adds twins for actions already tracked. Splitting a metric across two names never fails a build.

**Impact.** Silent: funnels and retention split across near-duplicate events, and existing dashboards and insights miss the new data.

```ts file=Generic agent
// existing: analytics.track('Sign Up Completed', { plan })
posthog.capture('user_signed_up', { plan })   // twin, same action
posthog.capture('UserSignedUp', { 'Plan Type': plan })
```

```ts file=Wizard
// First scan for capture calls the project already makes, note how names are
// formatted: "reuse the existing names ... don't duplicate events that already exist"
posthog.capture('Sign Up Completed', { plan })   // existing name reused, no twin
// greenfield only: snake_case, never spaces (todo_created)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Audit migrates a consistent naming convention</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to improve naming, the agent recommends renaming everything to PostHog's snake_case even when the project is already coherent.

**Impact.** Historical continuity breaks and saved insights stop matching the renamed events.

```diff file=Generic agent
- posthog.capture('Added To Cart')
+ posthog.capture('cart_item_added')   // 40 events renamed, project was 90% consistent
```

```text file=Wizard
If 80%+ of names share a pattern, the project HAS a convention.
If it is somewhat compliant with PostHog's AND own compliance is >=80%,
recommend sticking with their convention and tightening to 100%. Don't force a migration.
If incompatible OR own compliance <80%, recommend migrating toward PostHog's standard.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Event names built from variables</summary>

**Problem.** Interpolating an id or label into the name feels expressive, DRY and type-checks fine. Nothing warns that each distinct string becomes its own event definition.

**Impact.** Silent: unbounded event and property definitions that cannot be charted, funneled or cleaned up, and audits cannot resolve what is captured.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.capture(`clicked_${button.id}`, {
  [`${field}_value`]: value,
})
posthog.capture('plan_selected', { [`plan_${id}`]: true })
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.capture('button_clicked', {
  button_id: button.id,
  field_name: field,
  value,
})
// "Never generate event names or property keys dynamically. Put variable data in property values."
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Payment, webhook and auth events only in the browser</summary>

**Problem.** The agent follows the client SDK quickstart and instruments UI handlers. API routes, webhooks and Server Actions have no visible button, so it never opens them.

**Impact.** Silent: business-critical events such as payments and signups are lost to ad blockers, closed tabs, and flows that never touch the browser.

```ts file=Generic agent
// Checkout.tsx
posthog.capture('checkout_clicked')
// app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts -> no PostHog call at all
```

```ts file=Wizard
// payment webhook handler
const posthog = getPostHogClient()   // posthog-node
posthog.capture({ distinctId: userId, event: 'payment_succeeded',
  properties: { plan } })
await posthog.flush() // shared client in a per-request handler
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Every server event creates or updates a person</summary>

**Problem.** Backend SDKs process a person profile on every capture with a distinct id, and attach runtime metadata ($os, $lib) that overwrites what the browser set. The agent leaves the flag off on system events. The obvious fix of disabling person processing everywhere over-corrects and strips signup and subscription too.

**Impact.** Person properties overwritten (a Linux server rewrites a macOS user's $os) and every event billed as identified. Over-correction leaves no person profiles.

```python file=Generic agent
# cron job; SDK default processes the person on every capture
posthog.capture('cron_cleanup_ran', distinct_id=uid)
# server $os/$lib now overwrite the browser's person props
```

```python file=Wizard
posthog.capture('cron_cleanup_ran', distinct_id=uid,
    properties={'$process_person_profile': False})
# signup/subscription events stay person-defining + identify()/$set
# never put the flag on every server capture
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Same number sent as number and string</summary>

**Problem.** Webhook payloads and form values arrive as strings, and the agent passes them straight through at one site while another sends numbers. Nothing fails at capture time.

**Impact.** Silent: sum and average aggregates drop or zero the string rows.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.capture('purchase', { revenue: 49.99 })    // cart.ts
posthog.capture('purchase', { revenue: '49.99' })  // webhook.ts
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.capture('purchase', { revenue: Number(payload.amount) })
// audit type-drift bullet format:
// "revenue - number at cart.ts:41, string at webhook.ts:12 - silently zeros aggregates"
// checked keys: revenue, amount, price, count, duration_*, quantity
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Captures on repository and service methods</summary>

**Problem.** In layered backends like ASP.NET the agent instruments every method it touches, mirroring code structure instead of user actions.

**Impact.** Noisy, high-volume events and duplicated counts that drown the real funnel steps.

```csharp file=Generic agent
public Order Save(Order o)
{
    _posthog.Capture(userId, "repository_save_called");
    return _db.Save(o);
}
```

```csharp file=Wizard
[HttpPost("orders")]
public async Task<IActionResult> Create(OrderDto dto)
{
    var order = await _orders.CreateAsync(dto);
    _posthog.Capture(userId, "order_created", new() { ["total"] = order.Total });
    return Ok(order);
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Pageleave capture off or missing in SPAs</summary>

**Problem.** capture_pageleave looks like an optional extra, and an agent may leave it off or explicitly set false. For SPAs, route changes do not fire pageleave unless history-based pageview capture is on. The cost to bounce rate and session duration is invisible in code.

**Impact.** Silent. Bounce rate and session duration under-report for that domain (doctor Check 3 flags a low $pageleave to $pageview ratio per host).

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, { api_host, capture_pageleave: false })
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(token, {
  api_host,
  capture_pageleave: true,
  capture_pageview: 'history_change', // SPA: fire on route changes
})
// remediation.md says: change only this option, don't touch unrelated init config
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Same page tracked under several hosts</summary>

**Problem.** The agent installs the snippet on every domain it finds and does not check whether the same path renders under multiple $host values (marketing site and app subdomain, www and apex, proxy).

**Impact.** Silent. One path's analytics split across $host values, for example /pricing under two hosts, so per-page numbers are halved.

```text file=Generic agent
// same snippet on www.example.com and example.com, no canonical/redirect
// /pricing -> $host 'www.example.com' and $host 'example.com'
```

```text file=Wizard
# Check 5 (Info), fix type manual, no automatic code change:
# report the path and its hosts, recommend reviewing $current_url/$host
# capture, reverse proxy and canonicalization across all domains
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Ad click ids never captured</summary>

**Problem.** PostHog auto-captures UTM parameters, so the agent assumes attribution is covered. gclid, fbclid and msclkid are not captured automatically.

**Impact.** Conversion APIs and ROAS measurement cannot credit specific ad clicks.

```ts file=Generic agent
// site loads gtag() / fbq(); PostHog only auto-captures utm_*
posthog.init(KEY, { api_host: HOST })
```

```ts file=Wizard
const p = new URLSearchParams(location.search)
const ids = Object.fromEntries(
  ['gclid', 'fbclid', 'msclkid'].map(k => [k, p.get(k)]).filter(([, v]) => v)
)
if (Object.keys(ids).length) {
  posthog.setPersonProperties({}, ids)   // set-once, on landing
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Conversion events carry no attribution</summary>

**Problem.** Person-level $initial_utm_\* properties exist, so the agent assumes conversions are attributed. The event itself carries none of those values.

**Impact.** Event insights cannot slice signups or purchases by utm_campaign or referring domain at event time. Only a person-property breakdown works, losing event-time fidelity.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.capture('signed_up', { plan })
```

```ts file=Wizard
const a = getAttribution()   // utm_*, gclid, referrer read at landing
posthog.capture('signed_up', {
  plan,
  utm_source: a.utm_source,
  utm_campaign: a.utm_campaign,
  gclid: a.gclid,
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Metrics tagged with user IDs or raw URLs</summary>

**Problem.** The agent treats metrics like events and attaches user ids, emails and full URLs for detail, not knowing each distinct attribute combination is a new series and there is a per-flush cap.

**Impact.** Series explode and hit the per-flush series cap. Per-user data and PII leak into metric series.

```python file=Generic agent
posthog.metrics.count("http.requests", 1, attributes={
    "user_id": current_user.id,
    "email": current_user.email,
    "url": request.url,          # /orders/8841?token=...
})
```

```python file=Wizard
posthog.metrics.count("http.requests", 1, attributes={
    "route": request.url_rule.rule,   # route pattern, bounded
    "status_class": "2xx",
})
# bounded sets only; 'which user did X' is capture(), not a metric
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Metrics hand-added to every route</summary>

**Problem.** Adding a count call next to each handler is the most local change, so the agent repeats it per route with ad hoc names.

**Impact.** Inconsistent metric names and gaps. Every new route needs its own call.

```python file=Generic agent
@app.get("/orders")
def list_orders():
    posthog.metrics.count("orders.list.requests")
@app.post("/orders")
def create_order():
    posthog.metrics.count("orders.create.requests")
```

```python file=Wizard
@app.after_request
def record(resp):
    posthog.metrics.count("http.requests", attributes={"route": request.url_rule.rule})
    posthog.metrics.histogram("http.request.duration", elapsed_ms, unit="ms")
    return resp
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Ids in span names and spans everywhere</summary>

**Problem.** Interpolating the real path makes a span look precise, and wrapping every function feels thorough. Neither error shows until the Tracing UI fails to group.

**Impact.** Every request gets its own span name, so operations do not aggregate, and noise swamps the real choke points.

```python file=Generic agent
with posthog.start_span(f"GET /users/{user_id}"):
    return load_user(user_id)
# plus a hand-written span around every function and route
```

```python file=Wizard
with posthog.start_span("GET /users/:id"):   # values go in attributes
    return load_user(user_id)
# one server span per request in middleware; outbound calls and jobs at choke points
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Outbound calls drop the traceparent header</summary>

**Problem.** Each service traces fine when tested alone. Trace propagation only matters across services, which a single-repo agent rarely runs.

**Impact.** Silent. Downstream spans start new traces instead of joining one.

```ts file=Generic agent
await fetch('http://billing/charge', { method: 'POST', body })   // no traceparent
```

```ts file=Wizard
await fetch('http://billing/charge', {
  method: 'POST', body,
  headers: { traceparent },   // W3C header, so spans join one trace
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Demo events seeded with no cleanup marker</summary>

**Problem.** To give an empty project something to show, an agent sends sample events with real-looking ids and the current time. It does not know PostHog events are immutable and cannot be removed later.

**Impact.** Fake funnel data mixes with real analytics forever and cannot be filtered out. Self-hosted hosts may not ingest it at all.

```ts file=Generic agent
for (let i = 0; i < 12; i++)
  client.capture({ distinctId: `user${i}`, event: 'signed_up' })   // now, no marker
```

```ts file=Wizard
{ event: 'signed_up',
  distinctId: `wizard-demo-user-${i + 1}`,
  timestamp: at(now, daysAgo, hour),          // backdated within 7 days
  properties: { ...properties, wizard_seed: true } }
// offered only for an empty project when isKnownCloudHost(apiHost); $exception not seeded
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Expected failures captured as exceptions</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to add error tracking, an agent wraps every catch in captureException. Nothing tells it that validation errors, 4xx responses and cancellations are normal outcomes, not bugs.

**Impact.** Noise and cost. A validation catch that fires tens of thousands of times a week from a few thousand users buries real issues.

```ts file=Generic agent
try { schema.parse(body) } catch (e) {
  posthog.captureException(e)   // ValidationError, an expected outcome
}
```

```text file=Wizard
warning | src/api/signup.ts:42 | ValidationError captured as an exception,
38k occurrences from 2.1k users in 7d. Log at warn instead.
(planned audit check et-expected-errors-captured, report-only; example output from the plan)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Errors sent as product events</summary>

**Problem.** Told to track failures, the agent reaches for capture() with an error property because it already uses capture() for everything else.

**Impact.** Stack traces land in product analytics instead of error tracking, and events carry unbounded text.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.capture('save_failed', { error: e.stack })
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.captureException(e)   // when someone acts on it, else a log line
// planned: wizard audit event-misrouted-error flags the wrong shape (report-only)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>AI session id minted per call or skipped</summary>

**Problem.** The agent reaches for uuid4() wherever an id is needed, or skips $ai_session_id when the app has no conversation field. Both look reasonable and neither errors.

**Impact.** Silent: every trace gets its own session, which groups nothing and is worse than omitting it.

```python file=Generic agent
props = {"$ai_session_id": str(uuid.uuid4())}   # new id every ask()
```

```python file=Wizard
session_id = thread_id or RUN_SESSION_ID   # one per conversation, never skipped
props = {"$ai_session_id": session_id}
# no conversation field: the process run is the session; never skip the step
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>AI session id under a misspelled key</summary>

**Problem.** Keys like session_id or ai_session_id look plausible and ingest drops them silently.

**Impact.** Silent: no session grouping at all.

```python file=Generic agent
posthog_properties={'session_id': thread_id}            # dropped by ingest
posthog_properties={'ai_session_id': thread_id}         # dropped by ingest
```

```python file=Wizard
posthog_properties={'$ai_session_id': conversation.thread_id}   # literal key, one id per conversation
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>OTel session id set on the resource</summary>

**Problem.** A conversation id seems like a resource-level attribute, so the agent sets it once. PostHog's filter reads only span attributes, and a $-prefixed key does not satisfy it alone.

**Impact.** Silent. Conversations do not group in AI Observability.

```go file=Generic agent
res := resource.NewWithAttributes(semconv.SchemaURL,
    attribute.String("$ai_session_id", sessionID))
// spans carry no session attribute
```

```go file=Wizard
// on EVERY span in the conversation; span attributes only
span.SetAttributes(
    attribute.String("$ai_session_id", sessionID),
    attribute.String("gen_ai.operation.name", "chat"), // turn span must pass the gen_ai./ai. filter
)
// carry posthog.distinct_id as the install doc shows; one trace per turn
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>LLM tool calls never captured as spans</summary>

**Problem.** The agent instruments the model call and assumes an OTel instrumentor covers tools. Vendor instrumentors patch only SDK methods and record tool use as attributes, never child spans.

**Impact.** Silent. Traces show generations with no tool spans, so there is no depth under each turn.

```python file=Generic agent
for call in reply.tool_calls:
    result = TOOLS[call.function.name](**json.loads(call.function.arguments))
    # nothing records the tool run
```

```python file=Wizard
for call in reply.tool_calls:
    result = TOOLS[call.function.name](**json.loads(call.function.arguments))
    posthog.capture(distinct_id=user_id, event='$ai_span', properties={
        '$ai_trace_id': trace_id,   # the turn's trace, so the span joins it
        '$ai_span_name': call.function.name,
    })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Fabricated or duplicate LLM spans</summary>

**Problem.** To get a richer trace tree, or once told to capture tool spans, the agent wraps helpers in manual spans everywhere, including apps with no tools and frameworks that already emit tool spans themselves.

**Impact.** Fabricated or duplicated spans clutter the trace tree, misreport what the app does, and app code is edited needlessly.

```python file=Generic agent
# agent framework already emits tool spans; app registers no tools
with tracer.start_as_current_span('get_weather'):   # wrapper around a plain helper
    result = get_weather(city)
posthog.capture(distinct_id=uid, event='$ai_span',
                properties={'$ai_span_name': 'chat_turn'})   # invented span
```

```python file=Wizard
# Registered tools on the wrapper path: one $ai_span next to the existing dispatch
result = TOOLS[call.function.name](**args)
posthog.capture(distinct_id=uid, event='$ai_span', properties={
    '$ai_trace_id': trace_id, '$ai_span_name': call.function.name})
# Agent frameworks and the Vercel AI SDK emit tool spans on their own: add nothing.
# An app that registers no tools has no spans: a complete result, not a gap.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Groq calls attributed to OpenAI</summary>

**Problem.** The app calls Groq through the OpenAI SDK with a baseURL override. Swapping in the PostHog OpenAI wrapper while keeping baseURL looks complete, but the wrapper hardcodes the provider as openai whatever host it calls.

**Impact.** Events carry $ai_provider 'openai' with a Groq model name, so PostHog's price lookup by model plus provider is wrong or fails, and cost and provider breakdowns are wrong. No error is raised.

```python file=Generic agent
client = OpenAI(posthog_client=posthog, base_url="https://api.groq.com/openai/v1")
client.chat.completions.create(
    model="llama-3.3-70b-versatile",
    messages=messages,
    posthog_distinct_id=user_id,
)  # event goes out with $ai_provider = "openai"
```

```python file=Wizard
client = OpenAI(posthog_client=posthog, base_url="https://api.groq.com/openai/v1")
client.chat.completions.create(
    model="llama-3.3-70b-versatile",
    messages=messages,
    posthog_distinct_id=user_id,
    posthog_properties={"$ai_session_id": session_id, "$ai_provider": "groq"},
)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>MCP 2026-07-28 traffic splits every request</summary>

**Problem.** Default instrumentation relies on the initialize handshake and Mcp-Session-Id header. The 2026-07-28 MCP revision removed both, which postdates most training data, so the default instrument() call looks complete.

**Impact.** Silent: every request becomes its own $session_id, so conversations never group.

```ts file=Generic agent
const analytics = instrument(server, posthog)
// 2026-07-28 traffic: no initialize, no Mcp-Session-Id -> $session_id per request
```

```ts file=Wizard
const analytics = instrument(server, posthog, {
  captureModel: true,
  enableConversationId: true,   // enable on supported v2 wrapping paths
})
// instrument once; never branch on the protocol revision
// Python mcp 2.x: MCPAnalyticsOptions(enable_conversation_id=True)
```

</details>

## Scope discipline

<details>
<summary>Builds missing Stripe setup instead of aborting</summary>

**Problem.** Agents are biased to finish the task, so when the Stripe calls or the PostHog distinct_id they should tag are missing, they create them. Nothing fails, so the made-up code looks real.

**Impact.** The user gets an invented Stripe client and made-up distinct_id choices, and revenue analytics rests on code that does not reflect their app.

```js file=Generic agent
// stripe.js, created by the agent (project had no Stripe calls)
import Stripe from 'stripe'
export const stripe = new Stripe(process.env.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY)
export const createCustomer = (email, distinctId) =>
  stripe.customers.create({ email, metadata: { posthog_person_distinct_id: distinctId } })
```

```text file=Wizard
[ABORT] Could not find a Stripe integration
[ABORT] Could not find a PostHog distinct_id
// the wizard middleware catches [ABORT] and ends the run; the agent must not exit itself
{ match: /^could not find a stripe integration$/i,
  message: 'Could not find a Stripe integration', docsUrl }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Replaces existing telemetry or analytics code</summary>

**Problem.** 'Add PostHog' reads like a migration, so the agent swaps the app's Prometheus, StatsD, logger, flag or other-vendor analytics calls for PostHog instead of adding alongside them, or adds a parallel system without asking.

**Impact.** Existing dashboards, alerts and analytics or logging pipelines stop working, and a large unrelated diff is hard to review.

```diff file=Generic agent
- from prometheus_client import Counter
- REQS = Counter("http_requests_total", ...)
+ posthog.metrics.count("http.requests")
- analytics.track('Signed Up', props)
+ posthog.capture('user_signed_up', props)
- logger.add(file_handler)
+ logger.add(otlp_handler)
```

```text file=Wizard
Add the PostHog call next to the existing one, reusing the same metric name and
attributes. Do NOT remove or restructure existing instrumentation.
When a file has other tools' integration code, place PostHog code below it.
If the project already has StatsD/Prometheus/OTel, ask before adding a parallel system.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Second tracing pipeline beside existing OpenTelemetry</summary>

**Problem.** The agent follows the posthog-node tracing doc without noticing the app already has OTel, or swaps its exporter.

**Impact.** Duplicate spans, broken existing tracing, or data sent to a destination the user did not choose.

```js file=Generic agent
// project already uses OTel exporting to another backend
new PostHog(key, { traces: { serviceName: 'api' } }) // second pipeline beside OTel
// or the existing exporter is replaced
```

```text file=Wizard
// OTel already present: point an OTLP trace exporter at /i/v1/traces on the PostHog host
// (Bearer project token); no posthog-node spans alongside it
// already exporting elsewhere: ask before adding PostHog as a second destination
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Narrow audit check grows into deep dive</summary>

**Problem.** Each expansion feels justified, so a single check like 'is identify called correctly' turns into a person-processing and flag review.

**Impact.** Slow, noisy audits that drift from the seeded checks.

```text file=Generic agent
Check: 'is identify() called correctly?'
-> person-processing deep dive -> flag evaluation analysis
-> reformats report sections
```

```text file=Wizard
# Seeded checks define scope. Ship rough findings;
# polish only if asked; deliver the minimum answer first,
# then offer to go deeper.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Migration keeps going when the source SDK is absent</summary>

**Problem.** Finding nothing feels like failure, so the agent widens the search to other vendors or treats a listed-but-unused package as in use.

**Impact.** The wrong vendor gets migrated, or a false success is reported.

```text file=Generic agent
grep statsig -> 0 call sites (package only in package.json)
-> "found LaunchDarkly instead, migrating that"
```

```ts file=Wizard
[ABORT] No source-SDK calls found
// signal = package in manifest AND at least one call site; do not widen the search
{ match: /^no source-sdk calls found$/i,
  body: '...run `npx @posthog/wizard@latest` to add PostHog from scratch' }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Wraps LLM providers the app does not use</summary>

**Problem.** LLM analytics docs show every supported provider side by side, and the agent copies wrappers for all of them instead of checking which providers the code calls.

**Impact.** Dead dependencies, imports and noisy diffs for providers the app never calls.

```python file=Generic agent
from posthog.ai.openai import OpenAI
from posthog.ai.anthropic import Anthropic
from posthog.ai.gemini import Client   # app only uses OpenAI
```

```python file=Wizard
from posthog.ai.openai import OpenAI   # the only provider the app calls
# Only instrument the provider(s) the user is actually using.
# Provider not listed -> manual-capture.md fallback
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>AI observability invented for an app without LLMs</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to complete every product, the agent installs a vendor SDK or adds a demo call rather than reporting the step not needed.

**Impact.** A new dependency, fake traffic and unrelated code in the user's app.

```js file=Generic agent
npm install openai
// added demo
const r = await openai.chat.completions.create({ model: 'gpt-4o', messages })
posthog.capture({ event: '$ai_generation', ... })
```

```text file=Wizard
Inspect existing call sites, not dependency names.
No LLM calls -> complete as `not needed` with the reason, change nothing.
Never install a vendor SDK or add a demo call.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Linear signal source reads every workspace team</summary>

**Problem.** The source config works with no team filter, so the agent enables it bare, which defaults to every open issue in the whole Linear workspace.

**Impact.** Signals, reports and draft PRs are created for teams the user never meant to hand over.

```json file=Generic agent
inbox-source-configs-create {
  "source_product": "linear", "source_type": "issue", "enabled": true
}   // no config: reads every team
```

```json file=Wizard
// integrations-linear-teams-retrieve, then one multi-select: 'All teams' first (the decline)
inbox-source-configs-create {
  "source_product": "linear", "source_type": "issue", "enabled": true,
  "config": { "linear_team_ids": ["<team id>"] }
}   // [] = all teams; report names the scope and where to change it
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Frontend flag invented for a server-rendered app</summary>

**Problem.** Told to add flags per side, the agent assumes every web app has a frontend and adds a browser SDK to templates that load no JavaScript.

**Impact.** Fabricated structure: unused client code and an example flag nothing evaluates.

```text file=Generic agent
Django templates load no scripts
agent: adds posthog-js snippet to base.html
       + wizard-example-frontend-flag
```

```text file=Wizard
A side counts only when the app already has code there to put an
evaluation in. Never add a side to host a flag. Server-rendered templates
that load no JavaScript of the app's own are not a frontend: such an app
has only a backend (sides: ["backend"]) -> one flag,
wizard-example-backend-flag, one server-side evaluation.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Extra recap files beyond the one report</summary>

**Problem.** Agents habitually finish by writing a SUMMARY.md or 'what was done' file next to the deliverable, unaware the skill's report is the only deliverable.

**Impact.** Orphan files clutter the user's repo and dilute the single requested deliverable.

```text file=Generic agent
Write posthog-events-audit-report.md
Write posthog-audit-3000-report.md   # meta-summary nobody asked for
Write SUMMARY.md                     # what was done
```

```text file=Wizard
**Only one file is produced by this skill:** `posthog-events-audit-report.md`.
**Do not write any additional summary, recap, or "what was done" file**
(e.g. `posthog-audit-report.md`, `audit-summary.md`, `SUMMARY.md`).
Keep the end-of-turn summary in the chat reply only.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Data-source step edits app files</summary>

**Problem.** Connecting a Postgres source looks related to the app's DATABASE_URL, so a capable agent wires it into .env or db code. The per-task harness also forgot to pause file writes while the credential prompt was open, and that gap is silent.

**Impact.** Credentials can land in project files, and Write/Edit can mutate the app for up to the 20-minute ask timeout while the user types a password.

```ts file=Generic agent
// per-task pi path: ask signal and fence reader never connected
createSecurityExtension({ disallowedTools })   // no getWizardAskPending
// ask tool built without onAskPendingChange
// agent meanwhile:
Write .env.local   # DATABASE_URL=postgres://app:<password>@db.acme.io/app
Edit  src/db.ts
```

```ts file=Wizard
# warehouse.md frontmatter
allowedTools: [Read, Glob, Grep, wizard_ask, posthog_exec]
disallowedTools: [Write, Edit, Bash, enqueue_task]
# tenet 6: 'Change no project code. This step connects external data.'
// harness (task.ts): pause Write/Edit while an ask is open
const askState = { pending: false };
createSecurityExtension({ getWizardAskPending: () => askState.pending, ... });
// ask tool: onAskPendingChange: (p) => { askState.pending = p }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Offering every detected data source at once</summary>

**Problem.** Every vendor API key in .env looks like a connectable source, so the obvious move is to offer them all. Long offers fail silently because users just dismiss them.

**Impact.** Users facing a long queue of credential prompts answer none and nothing connects.

```text file=Generic agent
seeded step input: 14 detected sources (every vendor with an API key in .env)
-> 14 credential prompts queued; user dismisses the first, nothing connects
```

```ts file=Wizard
const WAREHOUSE_SEED_LIMIT = 3;  // sources handed to the seeded step
const offered = sources.slice(0, WAREHOUSE_SEED_LIMIT);
// the notice names only those and says where the rest go;
// buildWarehouseNextSteps lists detected sources in the outro (up to
// WAREHOUSE_LINK_LIMIT, then 'And N more') with pre-filled links,
// whether or not the step finished done
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Asks credentials for data sources the app lacks</summary>

**Problem.** An over-eager detector reads a plain project as having Postgres or Stripe, and the agent goes on to ask for credentials instead of stopping.

**Impact.** The user is asked for credentials for systems the app never uses.

```text file=Generic agent
link checker (only dependency: commander)
agent: "Which Postgres database should I connect?"
```

```text file=Wizard
If your prompt lists no detected sources, emit `[ABORT] No data source detected`
and stop.   // zero ask batches, zero creates; WAREHOUSE_ABORT_CASES matches it
// expect.json: forbidKinds lists the 6 most-hallucinated kinds, expectAbort
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Connects a data source after the user declined</summary>

**Problem.** The task brief says to connect the source, and the agent puts finishing the task above the Skip answer it recorded earlier.

**Impact.** The user's explicit decline is overridden and a data source is connected anyway.

```text file=Generic agent
notice: Connect Stripe to the data warehouse?  -> Skip
agent: wizard_ask(Stripe restricted key) ... external-data-sources-create
```

```json file=Wizard
expect.json (next-stripe-declined): notice=decline, created=[], askBatches=[0,0],
  expectSkipReason="user-declined"
// zero credential batches, zero creates in the stub journal, task ends skipped
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Agent turns off autocapture or session replay</summary>

**Problem.** Turning off autocapture or opting users out by default looks like tidy noise reduction or a privacy guess once custom events exist, and the loss of data is silent.

**Impact.** Pageviews, clicks, form submits and replays the user expects stop being collected with no error.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, {
  api_host: host,
  autocapture: false,
  disable_session_recording: true,
  opt_out_capturing_by_default: true,
})
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(token, {
  api_host: host,
  defaults: '2025-05-24',
  // autocapture is ON by default (clicks, form submits, pageviews): leave it
})
// runtime GDPR opt-out stays posthog.opt_out_capturing() for a real consent flow
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Invented wrappers the codebase never uses</summary>

**Problem.** Agents default to 'good architecture': a service class, registry or safe wrapper, even when the project and the PostHog docs call the SDK directly. Some framework guidance (the Laravel commandment) even suggests a service, which pushes toward a lazy-init wrapper.

**Impact.** Large diffs that do not match the codebase, dead helpers, init running per call path, and later steps that cannot reach the client the same way.

```php file=Generic agent
class PostHogService {
  public function __construct() { PostHog::init(config('posthog.api_key'), [...]); }
}
// every controller:
$posthog = app(PostHogService::class);
$posthog->capture((string) $user->getAuthIdentifier(), 'user_logged_in');
```

```php file=Wizard
// init once at the app's init point; call sites use the SDK the way the docs/example do
PostHog::init($token, ['host' => $host]);
PostHog::capture(['distinctId' => (string) $user->id, 'event' => 'user_logged_in']);
// review.md: 'If a change invented a seam the project does not have - a registry, locator,
// wrapper, or app the codebase never uses - replace it with the closest pattern the
// project actually uses.'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Re-run strips hand-tuned config and guards</summary>

**Problem.** On a project that already has PostHog, the agent treats it as greenfield and 'normalizes' init and identify to the doc template. It cannot tell deliberate options, comments and guards from leftovers, and the build still passes.

**Impact.** Silent regressions: ui_host dropped while api_host stays '/ingest', identify fires $set on every navigation, host fallbacks and rationale comments vanish.

```diff file=Generic agent
 posthog.init(token, {
   api_host: '/ingest',
-  ui_host: 'https://us.posthog.com',
+  tracing_headers: [window.location.hostname],
 })
-    if (posthog.get_distinct_id() !== userId) {
       posthog.identify(userId, email ? { email } : undefined);
-  host: env?.X ?? process.env.X ?? "https://us.i.posthog.com",
```

```ts file=Wizard
// universal.md: 'If an installation already exists, do not modify its code in any way.'
posthog.init(token, {
  api_host: '/ingest',
  ui_host: 'https://us.posthog.com',   // kept
})
// commandment: 'never drop existing code, comments, or markup to make room for a change'
// build step: 'Nothing was mangled' - restore anything deleted or reformatted
// intro: 'You can still rerun the installation, but it might overwrite some of your work.'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Already-integrated project re-run without detection</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to add PostHog, an agent does not check whether it is already there and runs the full flow anyway.

**Impact.** About 8 minutes of no-op work (auth, skill install, agent run), and users bail out fearing their setup will be overwritten.

```text file=Generic agent
user: "add PostHog to my app"
agent: (no dependency check) -> installs SDK, edits init, adds captures
```

```text file=Wizard
detectExistingPostHog(): findPackageJsons() scan (monorepo-aware)
  -> session.posthogSdkDetected
Intro: 'It looks like PostHog is already installed. The Wizard has many tricks up its sleeve...'
       'Explore spell book' offered first, 'Continue anyway' second
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Whole-file rewrite drops existing code</summary>

**Problem.** When a targeted edit fails because the match text is not unique, rewriting the full file is the easy fallback. The model regenerates from memory and silently loses parts.

**Impact.** User code, providers, comments and markup disappear from the diff. Astro files lose their leading --- frontmatter fence, often with a syntax error.

```text file=Generic agent
Edit { old_string: "</body>" } -> "not unique"
Write { file_path: "src/pages/index.astro", content: <re-typed, leading --- cut off> }
write src/app/layout.tsx <rewritten file, about half the original length>
// existing ThemeProvider and AuthProvider, comments and markup dropped
```

```ts file=Wizard
// commandment: edit in place, add context until unique; Write only to create a file
export function overwriteShrinkReason(existing, next) {
  // existing >= 400 non-whitespace chars and the write removes > 30%
  return `This write replaces an existing file and removes ~${removed}% of its content. Make targeted edits instead...`;
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Unrelated code changed in touched files</summary>

**Problem.** While adding a provider or a metadata key, capable agents tidy nearby code (restructure props, extract helpers, change response shapes) because it looks like an improvement.

**Impact.** Invalid syntax, broken styling or changed route behavior unrelated to PostHog, which the user must find and undo.

```diff file=Generic agent
// app/_layout.tsx gets PostHogProvider (fine), but the agent also edits app/index.tsx
- <View style={styles.myStyle}>
+ <View style = [styles.myStyle]>   // invalid
// backend/routes/subscriptions.ts, while adding metadata (illustrative)
-  return res.json(sub)
```
