```yaml file=Wizard
  POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID: ${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID }}
# POSTHOG_CLI_* for direct posthog-cli; POSTHOG_* for bundler-plugin uploaders
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Upload credentials never reach the CI or Docker build</summary>

**Problem.** Agents write POSTHOG_CLI_\* to the local .env and edit the bundler config, but never trace where the production build runs. Env does not cross into a Docker build, composite action or SSH session.

**Impact.** Deploys build fine, no symbol set uploads, and Error Tracking keeps showing minified stack traces.

```yaml file=Generic agent
- name: Build and push image
  uses: docker/build-push-action@v6
  with:
    context: .
    push: true
# Dockerfile `RUN npm run build` sees no POSTHOG_CLI_* vars
```

```yaml file=Wizard
- name: Build and push image
  uses: docker/build-push-action@v6
  with:
    context: .
    push: true
    build-args: |
      POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY=${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY }}
      POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID=${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID }}
# multi-stage: ARG/ENV in the build stage before the build RUN; single-stage: secrets: input + BuildKit secret
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Gradle upload task ignores .env</summary>

**Problem.** Agents assume the build tool reads .env like JS bundlers do. Gradle does not, and the PostHog CLI exec task silently gets no credentials.

**Impact.** Android/Flutter symbol upload fails, or works only where posthog-cli login was run, so CI and teammates break.

```kotlin file=Generic agent
// .env holds POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY, POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID
// app/build.gradle.kts: PostHog plugin applied, no .env bridge
// PostHogCliExecTask gets no credentials
```

```kotlin file=Wizard
val postHogEnv = Properties().apply {
    rootProject.file(".env").takeIf { it.exists() }?.inputStream()?.use { load(it) } }
tasks.withType<PostHogCliExecTask>().configureEach {
    postHogEnv.getProperty("POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY")?.let { postHogApiKey.set(it) }
    postHogEnv.getProperty("POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID")?.let { postHogProjectId.set(it) }
    postHogEnv.getProperty("POSTHOG_CLI_HOST")?.let { postHogHost.set(it) }
}  // unset props fall back to POSTHOG_CLI_* env vars in CI
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Go binary tested without re-uploading symbols</summary>

**Problem.** Every Go build has its own identity (GNU build ID on Linux, Mach-O UUID on macOS), so symbols uploaded for an earlier build do not match. The failure looks like broken wiring rather than a stale upload.

**Impact.** Silent: the test exception arrives unsymbolicated.

```bash file=Generic agent
go build -o bin/app . && ./bin/app   # upload skipped, or done for an earlier build
```

```bash file=Wizard
# Linux; on macOS use -ldflags="-compressdwarf=false" instead
go build -ldflags="-B gobuildid" -o bin/<app> . \
  && posthog-cli --dotenv-file .env symbol-sets upload --directory ./bin
./bin/<app>   # the binary whose symbols were just uploaded
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Generic CLI bolted onto framework source maps</summary>

**Problem.** Appending posthog-cli to the build script is the generic recipe, but Next.js, Nuxt and Angular own their build pipelines and have dedicated integrations.

**Impact.** Upload runs against the wrong output or misses framework bundles. Nuxt 3.6 vs 3.7+ differences ignored. Same for React plus Vite, which should use the Vite plugin.

```json file=Generic agent
"build": "next build && posthog-cli sourcemap process --directory .next"
```

```ts file=Wizard
// detect.ts pickJsVariant: react-native, nuxt, next, @angular/core come first,
// before vite/webpack/rollup/react/node/web: they own their build pipeline
// skill: "Use the framework's documented source-map upload integration...
// configure upload there rather than bolting on a separate CLI step"
// (references split Nuxt 3.6- and 3.7+)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>React Native repo gets the iOS dSYM uploader</summary>

**Problem.** A React Native or Flutter repo carries ios/Podfile, .xcodeproj and Gradle files, so it looks exactly like a native app.

**Impact.** Native dSYM or Gradle upload is wired in, JavaScript source maps are never uploaded, and every JS stack trace stays minified.

```text file=Generic agent
repo: package.json (react-native), ios/Podfile, android/build.gradle
agent picks variant: ios  -> Run Script dSYM wiring in ios/
```

```text file=Wizard
first match wins:
1. react-native or expo in package.json deps -> react-native
2. pubspec.yaml -> flutter
3. .xcodeproj / Podfile / Package.swift -> ios
4. Gradle with com.android.application|library -> android
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Composite GitHub action reads secrets directly</summary>

**Problem.** $&#123;&#123; secrets.\* &#125;&#125; works in workflow files, so the agent uses it inside a local composite action.yml, where only inputs.\* is available and the value is silently empty.

**Impact.** The build arg is empty, credentials never reach the Docker build, and source-map upload silently fails.

```yaml file=Generic agent
# .github/actions/build-and-push/action.yml
build-args: |
  POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY=${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY }}
```

```yaml file=Wizard
# action.yml
inputs:
  posthog-cli-api-key: { required: true }
# ...
build-args: |
  POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY=${{ inputs.posthog-cli-api-key }}
# caller: with: posthog-cli-api-key: ${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY }}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Symbol upload wired into a pure Dart package</summary>

**Problem.** A pubspec.yaml looks like a Flutter app, but a package or plugin has no web/, android/ or ios/ target and never produces an app build.

**Impact.** The post-build upload step has nothing to upload, so it fails or does nothing.

```bash file=Generic agent
# flutter_plugin/ has lib/ and pubspec.yaml only
flutter build web --source-maps
posthog-cli sourcemap process --directory build/web   # no web/ target
```

```ts file=Wizard
function projectHasBuildTarget(installDir, projectPath) {
  const root = join(installDir, projectPath);
  return existsSync(join(root, 'web', 'index.html')) ||
         existsSync(join(root, 'android')) || existsSync(join(root, 'ios'));
}
// else blocked: 'Flutter project has no web, android or ios target - nothing to upload symbols for'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Agent invents a CI workflow it cannot trace</summary>

**Problem.** Needing somewhere to put upload secrets, the agent copies a sibling package's workflow or writes one from scratch rather than admitting it could not find the real deploy path.

**Impact.** An invented, unreviewed pipeline that does not match the real deploy, while the real build still has no credentials.

```yaml file=Generic agent
# repo has .github/workflows/deploy-backend.yml only; agent creates:
# .github/workflows/deploy-frontend.yml
- run: npm run build
  env:
    POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY }}
```

```text file=Wizard
Only ever edit CI/deploy files that already exist - never create a new
workflow, pipeline, or deploy file. Untraceable setup: make no CI changes
and tell the user that wherever their production build command runs, it
must have the upload credentials (POSTHOG_CLI_* / POSTHOG_*) available as
environment variables, or maps won't upload on deploy.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Node server errors never reach Error Tracking</summary>

**Problem.** Server SDKs leave exception autocapture off by default, and the analytics quickstart never mentions it. Even with enableExceptionAutocapture on, the Express/Fastify/Koa error middleware catches route errors first, so the global hook never hears of them.

**Impact.** Silent: handled and unhandled server errors returned as 500s never appear in Error Tracking, though events flow normally.

```js file=Generic agent
const posthog = new PostHog(token, { host })
app.use((err, req, res, next) => {
  console.error(err)
  res.status(500).json({ error: 'Internal error' })
})
```

```js file=Wizard
const posthog = new PostHog(token, { host, enableExceptionAutocapture: true })
app.use((err, req, res, next) => {
  posthog.captureException(err, req.user?.id)
  res.status(500).json({ error: 'Internal error' })
})
// Express alternative the wizard also emits: setupExpressErrorHandler(posthog, app)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Nuxt module set up without error tracking</summary>

**Problem.** @posthog/nuxt works with only a key and host. Client capture, server capture and source maps are separate opt-in blocks with different option names (capture_exceptions vs enableExceptionAutocapture), and the personal key must stay out of public runtime config.

**Impact.** Silent: unhandled Vue errors and Nitro request errors never reach Error Tracking, and client stack traces stay minified.

```ts file=Generic agent
export default defineNuxtConfig({
  modules: ['@posthog/nuxt'],
  posthogConfig: { publicKey, host },
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthogConfig: {
  publicKey, host,
  clientConfig: { capture_exceptions: true },          // client side (Vue)
  serverConfig: { enableExceptionAutocapture: true },  // server side (Nitro)
  sourcemaps: { enabled: true, envId: process.env.PROJECT_ID || '',
    personalApiKey: process.env.PERSONAL_API_KEY || '' },
}  // personal key stays out of runtimeConfig.public
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flask error handlers hide exceptions from PostHog</summary>

**Problem.** Agents trust SDK autocapture, but Flask turns exceptions into responses in its own errorhandler first, so the exception is handled and autocapture never fires.

**Impact.** Silent: Flask 500s never reach Error Tracking.

```python file=Generic agent
@app.errorhandler(500)
def internal_server_error(e):
    return render_template('errors/500.html'), 500  # PostHog never sees e
```

```python file=Wizard
@app.errorhandler(Exception)
def handle(e):
    if isinstance(e, HTTPException):
        return e
    posthog.capture_exception(e)
    return render_template('500.html'), 500
# routes.py example adds user context:
#   with new_context(): identify_context(str(current_user.id)); posthog.capture_exception(e)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Exception autocapture left off at init</summary>

**Problem.** The agent treats 'add PostHog' as analytics only and copies an analytics-only init snippet. Capture_exceptions is opt-in, so nothing breaks until a user opens an empty Error Tracking tab.

**Impact.** Silent: uncaught browser errors never reach PostHog. Users conclude Error Tracking does not work. Same for Python, where the option is enable_exception_autocapture.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.init(apiKey, { api_host: '/ingest', defaults: '2026-01-30' })
// no capture_exceptions, no captureException anywhere
// React: <PostHogProvider apiKey={key} options={{ api_host: '/ingest' }}>
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(apiKey, {
  api_host: '/ingest',
  defaults: '2026-01-30',
  capture_exceptions: true, // unhandled exceptions -> Error Tracking
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Manual captures sprinkled across every route</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to 'add error tracking', a thorough agent reads the whole app and wraps each component, route and view in try/catch, which looks diligent but covers only what it touched.

**Impact.** Large noisy diff across unrelated files, a long-running step, partial coverage, and errors outside the wrapped code still go unreported.

```js file=Generic agent
async function loadProfile() {
  try { return await api.get('/profile') }
  catch (e) { posthog.captureException(e); throw e } // x14 files
}
```

```js file=Wizard
// once at init
posthog.init(token, { capture_exceptions: true })
// once in the framework's own handler (Vue shown)
app.config.errorHandler = (err) => {
  posthog.captureException(err)
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Autocapture option copied from the wrong SDK</summary>

**Problem.** posthog-js uses capture_exceptions while posthog-node uses enableExceptionAutocapture. Training data mixes them, and an unknown init option is silently ignored.

**Impact.** Silent: the option does nothing, errors never reach PostHog, and the report still claims error tracking is on.

```js file=Generic agent
// posthog-js client, option from posthog-node docs
posthog.init(token, { enableExceptionAutocapture: true })
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(token, { capture_exceptions: true })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Extra global error listener beside SDK autocapture</summary>

**Problem.** Enabling autocapture looks too passive, so the agent also registers window.onerror, unhandledrejection, uncaughtException or sys.excepthook handlers the SDK already owns.

**Impact.** Silent: every uncaught error is sent twice, doubling issue counts and cost, and the copy drifts from SDK behavior.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, { capture_exceptions: true })
window.addEventListener('error', e => posthog.captureException(e.error))
window.addEventListener('unhandledrejection', e =>
  posthog.captureException(e.reason))
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.init(token, { capture_exceptions: true }) // SDK owns the global handlers
// add only what the SDK cannot see: Express error middleware,
// Vue app.config.errorHandler, Angular ErrorHandler,
// SvelteKit handleError, React error boundary, Next.js global-error
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>One failure captured at several layers</summary>

**Problem.** Told to add tracking 'around critical flows and API boundaries', the agent logs, captures and rethrows at each layer and also captures expected outcomes like validation 4xx.

**Impact.** Silent: one failure becomes several issues, inflating error counts, noise and cost.

```js file=Generic agent
try { await charge(order) } catch (e) {
  logger.error(e); posthog.captureException(e); throw e
}   // global handler captures it again
// also captures validation 4xx
```

```js file=Wizard
// global handler or autocapture is the floor
// capture at a natural boundary only in a single edit,
// not sprinkled across files
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Source maps generated but never uploaded</summary>

**Problem.** The agent turns on exception capture or sourcemap generation and stops, because errors do arrive and the report says 'exceptions are captured'. Nothing errors when maps never reach PostHog.

**Impact.** Silent: production stack traces stay minified (frames like lt / ct in dist/index.js:2:90646), or fail to match because deployed assets were never injected.

```json file=Generic agent
"build": "esbuild index.js --bundle --platform=node --sourcemap=external --outfile=dist/index.js"
// no posthog plugin or CLI step; CI deploys dist/ as is
// frontend: "build": "vite build"   // no upload anywhere
```

```json file=Wizard
"build": "esbuild index.js ... --sourcemap=external --sources-content=true --outfile=dist/index.js && posthog-cli --dotenv-file .env sourcemap process --directory ./dist --release-name express-todo"
// or @posthog/webpack-plugin / rollup-plugin with deleteAfterUpload
// handoff: - [ ] Wire source-map upload into CI so production stack traces de-minify
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Existing PostHog setup skips source map upload</summary>

**Problem.** The agent treats 'PostHog is already integrated' as 'nothing left to do', and assumes a tsc-built Node service has readable stack traces. Those are two separate questions.

**Impact.** Production stack traces stay compiled or minified in an otherwise complete setup. Nothing reports the gap.

```text file=Generic agent
plan: posthog-node present -> integrated -> done
(no upload step queued for a Node service built with tsc)
```

```text file=Wizard
The three facts are independent: settle ALL of them before you enqueue anything.
"PostHog is already integrated" answers fact 1 only; it never decides fact 2 or fact 3,
and an already-integrated project still gets the upload subgraph when a variant matches.
A Node service built with `tsc` ships compiled output, so it is the `node` variant, not "none".
Only two kinds of project skip the upload subgraph: the readable-stack platforms, and Astro.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Public project token used to upload maps</summary>

**Problem.** The phc_ token is already in the app, so the agent reuses it for posthog-cli or the bundler plugin. Uploads need a personal API key with specific scopes.

**Impact.** Uploads fail auth (no write scope for symbol sets), so production errors stay minified.

```bash file=Generic agent
# .env
POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY=phc_xxxxxxxx   # the public project token from posthog.init
```

```bash file=Wizard
# personal API key, "Source map upload" preset
# scopes: organization:read, error_tracking:write
POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY=phx_xxxxxxxx
POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID=12345
POSTHOG_CLI_HOST=https://us.posthog.com
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Upload wired for stacks that cannot use it</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to set up source maps, the agent wires an upload into any project: one with no PostHog SDK, bare React Native that cannot inject chunk IDs, a project with no build command, or a Rust project a stray JS SDK makes look instrumented.

**Impact.** Maps uploaded for an app that never reports exceptions, uploads that never resolve, or an invented build command that breaks the build. None fails visibly.

```text file=Generic agent
# no posthog-js/posthog-node dependency: agent still adds posthog-cli upload
# bare React Native (no expo): adds posthog-cli upload to the Metro build
# no build script: agent invents `npm run build`
# Rust workspace: hasPostHog = true because a stray JS SDK exists
```

```ts file=Wizard
if (!isAutomatableVariant(p.targetId)) return { instrumentable: false,
  reason: "Source-map upload isn't supported for this stack yet" };
if (p.targetId === 'react-native' && !probes.isExpoProject(p.path)) return { instrumentable: false, reason: BARE_REACT_NATIVE_REASON };
if (!hasPostHog) return { instrumentable: false,
  reason: `No PostHog SDK installed yet - ${install}` };
// prompt STEP 6: no build command -> [ABORT] build command not found
// Rust/Go hasPostHog read from Cargo.toml / go.mod, not the agent
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>SDK presence judged by any manifest mention</summary>

**Problem.** An agent asked 'does this project use posthog-go?' substring-matches go.mod. A replace, exclude, comment or module line looks like a dependency.

**Impact.** The wizard treats the project as instrumented and wires symbol upload for an app that never reports exceptions. Same for Rust, where a JS SDK or a comment matched posthog-rs.

```text file=Generic agent
module example.com/svc
// agent: hasPostHog = true (substring match)
```

```ts file=Wizard
// deterministic go.mod read overrides the agent's hasPostHog boolean
function goModRequiresSdk(content: string): boolean {
  // strip // comments; only `require x` lines and `require ( ... )` blocks count
  if (inBlock === 'require' && code.includes(GO_SDK_MODULE)) return true;
  if (code.startsWith('require ') && code.includes(GO_SDK_MODULE)) return true;
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Web source-map recipe applied to mobile apps</summary>

**Problem.** 'Upload source maps' suggests .js.map files everywhere, but native platforms need their own debug symbols, often through a platform plugin rather than a CLI step.

**Impact.** Wrong artifacts are uploaded and native stack traces stay unresolved.

```bash file=Generic agent
posthog-cli sourcemap upload --directory build/  # *.js.map recipe on an Android app
```

```bash file=Wizard
# Android: ProGuard/R8 mappings via the Gradle plugin on the app module
id("com.posthog.android") version "<latest>"   // release needs isMinifyEnabled = true
# iOS: dSYMs via Run Script (upload-symbols.sh with POSTHOG_CLI_DOTENV_FILE)
# React Native (Expo): posthog-react-native/expo plugin, uploadNativeSymbols
# Go/Rust: posthog-cli --dotenv-file .env symbol-sets upload --directory <bin dir>
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>iOS crashes unsymbolicated: no dSYM upload</summary>

**Problem.** Enabling autoCapture looks complete. When asked for symbols the agent hand-rolls a JS-style CLI step and misses the Xcode settings (dSYMs in Release, script sandbox off) and the env prefixes the SDK script needs. The build still succeeds.

**Impact.** Silent: Release builds never upload dSYMs, so crash reports show memory addresses instead of code.

```bash file=Generic agent
config.errorTrackingConfig.autoCapture = true
# hand-rolled Run Script, build settings untouched:
posthog-cli sourcemap process --directory ./build
# DEBUG_INFORMATION_FORMAT = dwarf (Release)
# ENABLE_USER_SCRIPT_SANDBOXING = YES
```

```bash file=Wizard
DEBUG_INFORMATION_FORMAT = dwarf-with-dsym   # Release
ENABLE_USER_SCRIPT_SANDBOXING = NO
# Run Script phase, ordered last (SPM variant):
POSTHOG_INCLUDE_SOURCE=1 POSTHOG_CLI_DOTENV_FILE="${SRCROOT}/.env" \
  "${BUILD_DIR%/Build/*}/SourcePackages/checkouts/posthog-ios/build-tools/upload-symbols.sh"
# CocoaPods: "${PODS_ROOT}/PostHog/build-tools/upload-symbols.sh"
# wizard pre-installs posthog-cli (VARIANTS_REQUIRING_POSTHOG_CLI)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>iOS errors sent as custom events</summary>

**Problem.** The agent copies the custom-event shape still shown in the Swift example app. It never enables errorTrackingConfig.

**Impact.** Nothing appears in Error Tracking, and any crash reports stay unsymbolicated.

```swift file=Generic agent
PostHogSDK.shared.capture("test_error_triggered", properties: [
    "error_type": "test",
    "error_message": error.localizedDescription,
])
```

```swift file=Wizard
config.errorTrackingConfig.autoCapture = true
// and upload dSYM files so crash reports are symbolicated
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Android mapping upload never runs</summary>

**Problem.** The agent treats Android like web and hand-rolls a posthog-cli step, or applies the Gradle plugin at the root with the SDK's version number. It also misses that the plugin only hooks minified variants.

**Impact.** No ProGuard/R8 mapping is uploaded, so Android stack traces stay obfuscated with no error.

```kotlin file=Generic agent
// build.gradle.kts (root project)
plugins { id("com.posthog.android") version "3.9.0" }  // SDK version reused
android { buildTypes { release {
    isMinifyEnabled = false   // plugin hooks nothing
} } }
```

```kotlin file=Wizard
// app/build.gradle.kts (app module, never root)
plugins { id("com.posthog.android") version "<latest plugin>" }  // versioned apart from SDK
android { buildTypes { release {
    isMinifyEnabled = true   // keep existing proguardFiles line
} } }
// no hand-rolled posthog-cli step: the plugin uploads
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>React Native: native crash symbols skipped</summary>

**Problem.** The reference docs badge native symbolication as optional, so the agent wires only JS/Hermes maps. It also misses that native crash capture must be turned on at runtime, and it burns time trying to make bare React Native work.

**Impact.** Native crashes arrive unsymbolicated or are never captured at all. Bare RN attempts made wizard runs extremely slow.

```text file=Generic agent
// app.json
"plugins": [["posthog-react-native/expo", {}]]
// metro.config.js: default config
// SDK init: nativeCrashes not enabled
```

```text file=Wizard
app.json: ["posthog-react-native/expo", { "uploadNativeSymbols": true, "dotenvFile": ".env" }]
  (dotenvFile needs posthog-react-native >= 4.60.0)
metro.config.js: getPostHogExpoConfig from posthog-react-native/metro
SDK init: errorTracking.autocapture.nativeCrashes on + @posthog/react-native-plugin installed
no Expo -> not supported: 'Bare React Native (without Expo) is not supported'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flutter symbols uploaded for one platform only</summary>

**Problem.** Flutter looks like one codebase, so the agent wires one upload or looks for a Dart-level step. But each of the three compilers makes its own artifact, and captureNativeExceptions defaults to false.

**Impact.** Stack traces from the platforms left out stay unsymbolicated, and native SDK crashes are not captured.

```bash file=Generic agent
# only web wired
flutter build web --source-maps
posthog-cli sourcemap process --directory build/web
# android/ and ios/ have no upload step
```

```bash file=Wizard
# one upload path per platform directory present
web:     flutter build web --source-maps && posthog-cli --dotenv-file .env sourcemap process --directory build/web
android: Gradle plugin on android/app/build.gradle.kts; .env via rootProject.file("../.env")
ios:     Runner Run Script ${PODS_ROOT}/PostHog/build-tools/upload-symbols.sh
         with POSTHOG_CLI_DOTENV_FILE="${SRCROOT}/../.env"
# errorTrackingConfig.captureNativeExceptions = true
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flutter exceptions captured without stack trace</summary>

**Problem.** captureException takes named arguments, and stackTrace is optional. So passing only the error compiles fine. Uncaught-error capture also needs its own errorTrackingConfig flags.

**Impact.** Events arrive with no frames to resolve, and uncaught Flutter or platform errors are never captured.

```dart file=Generic agent
} catch (error) {
  await Posthog().captureException(error: error);
}
// no errorTrackingConfig: uncaught errors not captured
```

```dart file=Wizard
config.errorTrackingConfig.captureFlutterErrors = true;
config.errorTrackingConfig.capturePlatformDispatcherErrors = true;
config.errorTrackingConfig.inAppIncludes.add('package:burrito_app');
// handled:
} catch (error, stackTrace) {
  await Posthog().captureException(error: error, stackTrace: stackTrace);
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Readable-stack apps get no release linking</summary>

**Problem.** Python, Ruby and PHP stack traces are already readable, so the agent and the old flow skipped the whole upload step. Release tagging was part of that step, so it went too.

**Impact.** Silent: exceptions carry no $release_id, so Error Tracking cannot say which deploy or commit caused an error.

```python file=Generic agent
# plain Flask app: capture only
posthog = Posthog(os.environ["POSTHOG_API_KEY"], host=os.environ["POSTHOG_HOST"])
posthog.capture_exception(exc)   # no release resolved at deploy
```

```yaml file=Wizard
- id: posthog-release
  uses: PostHog/resolve-release@v1     # production deploy job only
  with:
    api-key: ${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY }}
    project-id: ${{ vars.POSTHOG_PROJECT_ID }}
# app starts with POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID=${{ steps.posthog-release.outputs.release-id }}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Backend release name differs from source maps</summary>

**Problem.** Setting a friendly release-name looks harmless. But the source map upload in the same repo uses repo name plus commit SHA, so the two steps create two releases.

**Impact.** Frontend and backend errors from one deploy land on different releases, and nothing reports the split.

```yaml file=Generic agent
- uses: PostHog/resolve-release@v1
  with:
    api-key: ${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY }}
    project-id: ${{ vars.POSTHOG_PROJECT_ID }}
    release-name: my-service   # upload uses repo name + SHA
```

```yaml file=Wizard
- uses: PostHog/resolve-release@v1
  with:
    api-key: ${{ secrets.POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY }}
    project-id: ${{ vars.POSTHOG_PROJECT_ID }}
    # keep defaults; set release-name/release-version only to match
    # custom values the source map upload already sets
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>CLI catch-all swallows errors before autocapture</summary>

**Problem.** Autocapture only sees unhandled exceptions, but CLI scripts wrap main() in a catch-all that prints and exits, so turning autocapture on reports nothing.

**Impact.** Silent: script failures never reach Error Tracking. A capture with no distinct id becomes a personless random-id event.

```python file=Generic agent
posthog = Posthog(token, enable_exception_autocapture=True)
try:
    main()
except Exception as e:
    print(f"ERROR: {e}")
    sys.exit(1)   # swallowed: nothing is captured
```

```python file=Wizard
except Exception as e:
    print(f"ERROR: {e}")
    # Manually capture handled errors
    if posthog:
        posthog.capture_exception(e, distinct_id=get_user_id())
    sys.exit(1)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PHP autocaptured errors are personless</summary>

**Problem.** The PHP README snippet enables error_tracking without a context_provider, so the agent copies it exactly and nothing looks wrong.

**Impact.** Silent: autocaptured exceptions get a random UUID and $process_person_profile=false, so they cannot be tied to a user.

```php file=Generic agent
PostHog::init($projectToken, [
    'error_tracking' => ['enabled' => true],
]);
```

```php file=Wizard
'error_tracking' => [
    'enabled' => true,
    'context_provider' => static function (array $payload): array {
        return [
            'distinctId' => getUserId(),
            'properties' => ['app' => 'php_todo_cli'],
        ];
    },
],
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>WordPress recovery mode swallows fatals first</summary>

**Problem.** Enabling error_tracking or a PHP set_exception_handler looks universal, but WordPress's WP_Fatal_Error_Handler renders the recovery page first, and WP_DEBUG is off in production.

**Impact.** Silent: fatals can be swallowed before PostHog sees them in production.

```php file=Generic agent
PostHog::init($token, ['error_tracking' => ['enabled' => true]]);
set_exception_handler(function ($e) {
    PostHog::captureException($e);
}); // WP_Fatal_Error_Handler renders recovery mode first
// relies on WP_DEBUG=true to surface errors
```

```php file=Wizard
try {
    run_checkout_sync();
} catch (\Throwable $e) {
    PostHog::captureException($e); // explicit capture
    throw $e;
}
// do not rely on WP_DEBUG being on in production
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>React Router error boundary swallows exceptions</summary>

**Problem.** The agent trusts capture_exceptions to see every error. But the router's ErrorBoundary catches render errors before window.onerror does.

**Impact.** Silent: render errors vanish from Error Tracking even with capture_exceptions on.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, { api_host: host, capture_exceptions: true })
// root.tsx ErrorBoundary renders UI only, no captureException
```

```tsx file=Wizard
// data mode: root.tsx (framework mode takes { error } as a prop instead)
export function RootErrorBoundary() {
  const error = useRouteError();
  const posthog = usePostHog();
  if (error) posthog.captureException(error);
  ...
}
// declarative: <PostHogProvider client={posthog}><PostHogErrorBoundary><BrowserRouter>
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Vue component errors never reach PostHog</summary>

**Problem.** The agent relies on window.onerror autocapture. Vue handles component errors itself and, in production, only logs them unless app.config.errorHandler is set.

**Impact.** Silent: component and handler errors never appear in Error Tracking.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(token, { api_host: host, capture_exceptions: true })
app.mount('#app')   // no app.config.errorHandler
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(import.meta.env.VITE_POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN || '', {
  api_host: import.meta.env.VITE_POSTHOG_HOST || 'https://us.i.posthog.com',
  defaults: '2026-01-30',
})
app.config.errorHandler = (err, instance, info) => {
  posthog.captureException(err)
}
app.mount('#app')
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>SvelteKit handleError hooks not wired</summary>

**Problem.** The agent assumes capture_exceptions in hooks.client.ts covers everything. SvelteKit sends handled and server errors through handleError hooks instead.

**Impact.** Silent: server errors and framework-handled errors never reach Error Tracking.

```ts file=Generic agent
// hooks.client.ts: posthog.init(..., { capture_exceptions: true }) only
// hooks.server.ts: no handleError export
```

```ts file=Wizard
// hooks.client.ts
export const handleError: HandleClientError = async ({ error, status, message }) => {
  posthog.captureException(error);
  return { message, status };
};
// hooks.server.ts: HandleServerError, posthog-node getPostHogClient()
//   posthog.capture({ distinctId, event: 'server_error', properties })
//   await posthog.flush()  // short-lived request
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Upload CLI pointed at the ingestion host</summary>

**Problem.** The agent reuses the SDK host it already wrote. posthog-cli needs the API host, not \*.i.posthog.com, and nothing in the SDK setup says so.

**Impact.** Source map or symbol uploads hit the ingestion endpoint, so they fail authentication or get rejected, and stack traces stay unsymbolicated.

```bash file=Generic agent
POSTHOG_CLI_HOST=https://us.i.posthog.com
POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID=12345
POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY=phx_...
```

```bash file=Wizard
# .env (gitignored)
POSTHOG_CLI_HOST=https://us.posthog.com   # API host; EU: https://eu.posthog.com
# never the *.i.posthog.com ingestion host
POSTHOG_CLI_PROJECT_ID=12345
POSTHOG_CLI_API_KEY=phx_...
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Source maps shipped publicly or bundles not injected</summary>

**Problem.** Generating maps and deploying dist/ looks done. Whether the deployed bundle has the injected chunk ID, and whether the .map files are public, never shows up as an error.

**Impact.** Source code is exposed publicly, or stack traces never match the uploaded maps (silent).

```text file=Generic agent
deploy dist/**   # includes *.js.map
# posthog-cli sourcemap process skipped, or run on a different build
```

```bash file=Wizard
# inject + upload inside the same production build; deploy the injected bundles
tsc && posthog-cli sourcemap process --directory ./dist
# (bundler plugins for Vite/Webpack/Rollup do inject + upload in one pass)
# omit .map files from the deployed artifact (or use hidden source maps)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Release ID never reaches the running container</summary>

**Problem.** Setting POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID in CI or in a build stage looks correct in the diff. Multi-stage Docker drops it at the final stage, and the deploy stays green.

**Impact.** Silent: the running app has no POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID, so every exception arrives without a release.

```dockerfile file=Generic agent
FROM python:3.12 AS build
ARG POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID
# build stage only: the final stage below never sees it
ENV POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID=$POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID

FROM python:3.12-slim
COPY --from=build /app /app
CMD ["gunicorn", "app:app"]
```

```dockerfile file=Wizard
FROM python:3.12-slim
# final (runtime) stage: the running container reads it
ARG POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID
ENV POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID=$POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID
COPY --from=build /app /app
CMD ["gunicorn", "app:app"]
# workflow: build-args: POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID=${{ steps.posthog-release.outputs.release-id }}
# docker run / compose: -e POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID=...
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PHP-FPM clear_env drops the release ID</summary>

**Problem.** Setting the variable in the container env works for CLI PHP. When the repo ships its own FPM pool config, `clear_env` is yes or unset (FPM's default is yes) and strips the variable from the workers that serve requests. Stock official php-fpm images set clear_env = no, so only a repo pool config triggers this.

**Impact.** Silent: FPM workers see no POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID, so every exception has no release.

```text file=Generic agent
; pool.d/www.conf
[www]
clear_env = yes      ; workers never see POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID
```

```text file=Wizard
; pass the release ID from the master process to the workers
; (the master needs it first, e.g. from the container ENV)
env[POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID] = $POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Releases created on every branch build</summary>

**Problem.** The agent adds the resolve step to the first job it finds, often test or a deploy job that also runs on PRs. It does not know that resolving creates the release.

**Impact.** PostHog gets a new release for every branch, preview and PR build.

```yaml file=Generic agent
on: [pull_request, push]
jobs:
  test:
    steps:
      - uses: PostHog/resolve-release@v1   # creates a release per branch
```

```yaml file=Wizard
jobs:
  deploy:                       # production deploy job only
    steps:
      - id: posthog-release
        uses: PostHog/resolve-release@v1
        # deploy job also runs on PRs? gate the step, e.g.
        # if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Release step breaks on slim CI images</summary>

**Problem.** On GitLab, CircleCI or Jenkins, the agent assumes npm or posthog-cli exists in the job image, as it would on a dev machine.

**Impact.** The job fails on images without Node or curl (for example ruby:3.3-slim), or the release ID comes out empty without any error.

```yaml file=Generic agent
deploy:
  image: ruby:3.3-slim
  script:
    - npm install -g @posthog/cli     # no Node in this image
    - export POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID="$(posthog-cli release resolve)"
```

```yaml file=Wizard
deploy:
  script:
    # slim image: apt-get install -y curl first
    - curl --proto '=https' --tlsv1.2 -LsSf https://download.posthog.com/cli | sh
    - export PATH="$HOME/.posthog:$PATH"
    - export POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID="$(posthog-cli release resolve || true)"
    - ./deploy.sh   # must hand POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID to the app
```

</details>

## Feature flags

<details>
<summary>Flag copied into state instead of hook</summary>

**Problem.** Copying a value into state from useEffect is a generic React habit. The agent does not know posthog-js/react hooks track loading and later flag updates.

**Impact.** Silent: the effect can run before flags load, so the UI sticks on the default and never follows later flag changes.

```tsx file=Generic agent
const [on, setOn] = useState(false)
useEffect(() => {
  setOn(!!posthog.isFeatureEnabled('new-nav'))   // may run before flags load
}, [])
```

```tsx file=Wizard
import { useFeatureFlagEnabled } from 'posthog-js/react'

const on = useFeatureFlagEnabled('new-nav')   // handles loading and updates
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>All-flags result indexed like a map</summary>

**Problem.** Training data shows a name-keyed object, but the pinned SDK returns an array of results. Indexing it by name just yields undefined.

**Impact.** Healthy true assignments are rejected and exposures skipped, so the feature stays off for users who should have it.

```js file=Generic agent
const values = posthog.getAllFeatureFlags()   // FeatureFlagResult[] in the pinned SDK
if (typeof values['kestrel-help-pane'] === 'boolean') enablePane()   // never true
```

```js file=Wizard
const v = posthog.getFeatureFlag('kestrel-help-pane')   // per-key getter, reports exposure
if (v === true) enablePane()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flag value or payload used with no default</summary>

**Problem.** Examples show the happy path. The agent does not plan for undefined during loading, an unreachable PostHog, quota limits or an unset payload.

**Impact.** Broken or empty UI, a TypeError on payload fields, or the literal word 'undefined' rendered to users.

```tsx file=Generic agent
<Banner variant={posthog.getFeatureFlag('banner-variant')} />
const limit = posthog.getFeatureFlagPayload('limits').max   // TypeError on undefined
`Tip: ${posthog.getFeatureFlagPayload('quiz-difficulty')?.tip}`   // prints undefined
```

```tsx file=Wizard
<Banner variant={posthog.getFeatureFlag('banner-variant') ?? 'control'} />
const isBeta = posthog.isFeatureEnabled('beta') ?? false
`Tip: ${posthog.getFeatureFlagPayload('quiz-difficulty')?.tip ?? ''}`
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Local evaluation inside edge or Lambda handlers</summary>

**Problem.** Local evaluation is documented as the fast option, so the agent adds a personal API key without noticing the client is rebuilt per invocation.

**Impact.** Every invocation re-downloads flag definitions, defeating the polling cache and inflating flag cost drastically.

```ts file=Generic agent
export const runtime = 'edge'
export async function GET() {
  const ph = new PostHog(KEY, { personalApiKey: PERSONAL_KEY })
  return Response.json(await ph.getAllFlags(userId))
}
```

```ts file=Wizard
const ph = new PostHog(KEY, { host })   // no personalApiKey: remote evaluation
await ph.getAllFlags(userId)
// or share definitions via an external cache
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Local evaluation left on 30-second polling</summary>

**Problem.** The default polling interval is invisible in the init call, and its credit cost is not on the page the agent copies from.

**Impact.** Silent cost: a long-running server spends about 864,000 flag-definition credits a month.

```ts file=Generic agent
const posthog = new PostHog(key, { personalApiKey })   // polls every 30s, 10 credits each
```

```ts file=Wizard
const posthog = new PostHog(key, {
  personalApiKey,
  featureFlagsPollingInterval: 300_000,   // 5 min unless real-time changes are needed
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Bootstrap uses a different or random distinct id</summary>

**Problem.** bootstrap.distinctID looks like any id field, so the agent fills it with a per-request UUID. The SDK then believes it is already identified.

**Impact.** Silent: flags re-evaluate for a different person, users flip variants, and the later identify() cannot merge anonymous activity, leaving orphan profiles.

```ts file=Generic agent
posthog.init(KEY, {
  bootstrap: { distinctID: crypto.randomUUID(), featureFlags },
})
posthog.identify(user.id)   // different id
```

```ts file=Wizard
posthog.init(KEY, {
  bootstrap: { distinctID: user.id, featureFlags },  // stable, equals identify id
})
// audit: volatile id = warning; different id then identify = error
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Deprecated payload getter evaluates the flag twice</summary>

**Problem.** isFeatureEnabled plus getFeatureFlagPayload is the shape in older docs and training data. GetFeatureFlagResult is newer.

**Impact.** A deprecated client API and two evaluations per read. Skill examples drift from posthog.com docs.

```ts file=Generic agent
if (!posthog.isFeatureEnabled(flagKey)) return;
const payload = posthog.getFeatureFlagPayload(flagKey) as {
  steps?: TourStep[];
} | null;
```

```ts file=Wizard
const result = posthog.getFeatureFlagResult(flagKey);
if (!result?.enabled) return;
const payload = result.payload as { steps?: TourStep[] } | null;
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Multivariate flag read as a boolean</summary>

**Problem.** isFeatureEnabled is the first flag API in most examples, and any matched variant counts as true.

**Impact.** Every variant takes the same branch, for example a hard-only banner also shows for the easy variant.

```js file=Generic agent
const proTip = Boolean(posthog.isFeatureEnabled('quiz-difficulty'))   // easy and hard both true
```

```js file=Wizard
const proTip = posthog.getFeatureFlag('quiz-difficulty') === 'hard'   // compare the variant
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Code checks a flag that does not exist</summary>

**Problem.** The SDK returns false for unknown keys with no error or log, so a typo or deleted flag looks like a flag that is just off.

**Impact.** Silent: any branch gated on the missing key never runs for anyone.

```js file=Generic agent
posthog.isFeatureEnabled('quiz-hint-enabled')   // no such flag: false for everyone
```

```text file=Wizard
// drift audit: every key in code must exist in the live flag roster
// unknown key -> report it, do not assume the flag is just off
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flag evaluation failure takes the page down</summary>

**Problem.** evaluateFlags reads like a local lookup, so the agent calls it inline, unwraps the result or pattern-matches only success. It is a network call that throws when PostHog is slow or down.

**Impact.** A PostHog outage turns the page into a 500, a Rust panic or an Elixir MatchError instead of falling back to the flag being off.

```java file=Generic agent
PostHogFeatureFlagEvaluations flags = posthog.evaluateFlags(userId);
model.addAttribute("showNewFeature", flags.isEnabled("new-dashboard-feature"));
// Rust: .await.unwrap()   Elixir: {:ok, snapshot} = evaluate_flags(id)
```

```java file=Wizard
boolean show = false;
try {
    show = posthog.evaluateFlags(userId).isEnabled("new-dashboard-feature");
} catch (RuntimeException e) {
    log.warn("feature flag evaluation failed", e);   // fall back to off
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Feature Management without a PostHog user context</summary>

**Problem.** AddFeatureManagement() is the standard Microsoft setup and compiles fine. Nothing tells PostHog who the current user is.

**Impact.** Silent: flags evaluate without a distinct id, person properties or groups, so targeting returns defaults for everyone.

```csharp file=Generic agent
builder.Services.AddFeatureManagement();
// [FeatureGate("beta")] evaluated with no distinct id
```

```csharp file=Wizard
builder.AddPostHog(options => {
    options.UseFeatureManagement<AppFlagContext>();
});

public class AppFlagContext(IHttpContextAccessor http) : PostHogFeatureFlagContextProvider
{
    protected override string? GetDistinctId() => http.HttpContext?.User.Identity?.Name;
    // override GetFeatureFlagOptionsAsync for person properties and groups
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flag polling keeps Durable Objects awake</summary>

**Problem.** The generic posthog-node local-evaluation setup works in a Durable Object, and the timer cost only shows up on the bill.

**Impact.** Silent cost: polling timers stop the object hibernating or being evicted, extending billable duration.

```js file=Generic agent
const posthog = new PostHog(env.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, {
  host: env.POSTHOG_HOST,
  secretKey: env.POSTHOG_FEATURE_FLAGS_SECURE_API_KEY,   // default polling timer
})
```

```js file=Wizard
const posthog = new PostHog(env.POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN, {
  host: env.POSTHOG_HOST,
  secretKey: env.POSTHOG_FEATURE_FLAGS_SECURE_API_KEY,
  featureFlagsPollingInterval: null,   // posthog-node >= 5.55.0
})
// refresh deliberately: await posthog.reloadFeatureFlags()
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Migrated server flag checks lose the user id</summary>

**Problem.** Statsig server calls take a user object while PostHog takes a distinct_id string, so a mechanical rewrite drops the user or passes the whole object.

**Impact.** Flags evaluate for no user or the wrong one, so targeting silently returns wrong results.

```diff file=Generic agent
- Statsig.checkGate(user, 'my-gate')
+ posthog.isFeatureEnabled('my-gate')         // no distinct id
+ posthog.isFeatureEnabled('my-gate', user)   // object, not a string
```

```js file=Wizard
posthog.isFeatureEnabled('my-gate', user.userID)   // Statsig userID -> distinct_id
// per-call custom props -> personProperties; set only at init -> mark warning
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flag guard placed in a shared function</summary>

**Problem.** The agent puts the flag check where it is easiest, in an existing controller method that serves an unrelated route, without tracing what else reaches it.

**Impact.** False, missing or unavailable flags break an existing view: in Kestrel the Completed filter stopped rendering its list (9 route assertions failed across false, missing and outage modes). Tasks stayed saved. The route contents were wrong.

```js file=Generic agent
Controller.prototype.showCompleted = function () {
  // guard meant only for the Clear completed button
  if (!self.clearCompletedGate || !self.clearCompletedGate.enabled) return;
  self.model.read({ completed: true }, function (data) {
    self.view.render('showEntries', data);
  });
};
```

```js file=Wizard
// guard only the new control; existing routes render in every flag state
clearCompletedButton.hidden = !(posthog.isFeatureEnabled('clear-completed') ?? false);
// showCompleted stays unguarded and always renders
```

</details>

## Verification

<details>
<summary>LLM tracing judged by the diff</summary>

**Problem.** Swapping in the PostHog wrapper client looks complete, but trace grouping (session, trace, spans, person) only shows up in what reaches PostHog.

**Impact.** Silent: a run producing one trace per call passes, and the user is told it works.

```markdown file=Generic agent
Agent report: "Replaced OpenAI client with PostHog wrapper, added posthog_distinct_id.
AI Observability integration complete."
```

```markdown file=Wizard
Grade what reaches PostHog, not what the diff contains. Open the newest trace:
- one session holds the turns of one conversation
- one trace holds every generation of one turn
- a span per tool run; person matches the app's user id
- $ai_provider names the real provider on a gateway
Report "wired, unverified" until the user confirms.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Audit passes while PostHog shows open warnings</summary>

**Problem.** Audits read only the source tree, but some problems (unresolved stack frames, health issues) are only visible in PostHog's own server-side findings.

**Impact.** Silent: the source_maps recommendation (over 30% of JS frames unresolved) stays open while the audit reports clean.

```text file=Generic agent
wizard audit all, steps 1-4 only:
  installation, init, identification, event capture -> all pass
  (PostHog UI shows an open missing-source-maps warning)
```

```text file=Wizard
call error-tracking-recommendations-list {"limit": 50}
call health-issues-list {"status": "active", "dismissed": false, "limit": 50}
# audit_add_checks: one row per open finding, area "Live Data"
# id: live-data-<type>, e.g. live-data-source-maps
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Mobile symbol upload tested on a debug build</summary>

**Problem.** Default run commands (react-native run-android, flutter run, Android Studio Run) build the debug variant, but mapping upload runs only on minified Release builds.

**Impact.** The test exception shows an unsymbolicated trace and the setup looks broken. Same for iOS dSYMs, which upload only from the Release configuration.

```bash file=Generic agent
npx react-native run-android          # debug variant
./gradlew installDebug
# tap the test button; the mapping was never uploaded
```

```bash file=Wizard
./gradlew installRelease              # or Build Variants > release, then Run
npx expo run:ios --configuration Release
npx expo run:android --variant release
# launch, tap the button: an event, not a crash
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Source map test run on the dev server</summary>

**Problem.** The dev server is the natural place to try things, but source map upload runs only in the production build, so a dev-server test proves nothing either way.

**Impact.** The test exception shows minified or unresolved frames and the setup is wrongly judged broken or working. Same for Flutter web, which needs flutter build web, not flutter run.

```bash file=Generic agent
npm run dev   # trigger the test error, then check Error Tracking
```

```bash file=Wizard
npm run build   # uploads maps
npm run start   # or preview; trigger the error
# confirm a new symbol set on the Symbol sets page and resolved source frames
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Audit counts captureException as a product event</summary>

**Problem.** A \.capture( regex also matches .captureException(, and the agent does not notice the prefix match.

**Impact.** Silent. Exception sites pollute the product event inventory.

```bash file=Generic agent
grep -rn '\.capture(' src/   # also matches posthog.captureException(err)
```

```text file=Wizard
# drop slices whose trigger line contains .captureException(
# list them separately as exception_sites in the report appendix
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>iOS test error crashes instead of capturing</summary>

**Problem.** A test error reads as 'make it crash', and capture(error) looks like it should work, but capture() takes an event-name String.

**Impact.** Needs debugger detach and relaunch, or does not compile, so the symbolication check is not deterministic.

```swift file=Generic agent
Button("Trigger test error") {
    fatalError("Source map upload test") // detach debugger, relaunch
}
// or: PostHogSDK.shared.capture(error)  // capture() takes a String
```

```swift file=Wizard
do {
    throw NSError(domain: "PostHogSourceMapTest", code: 1,
                  userInfo: [NSLocalizedDescriptionKey: "Source map upload test error"])
} catch {
    PostHogSDK.shared.captureException(error)
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Success screen names a report nobody writes</summary>

**Problem.** The agent naturally summarizes in chat. Unless the prompt names the file and location, it never writes the report the outro points to.

**Impact.** Every upload-source-maps success screen (iOS, Android, Flutter, JS) pointed at posthog-source-maps-report.md, which no run had written.

```text file=Generic agent
Outro: "Details in <installDir>/posthog-source-maps-report.md"
STEP 9 - Summarise and hand off. (skill: "Verify and hand off")
   Follow the skill's "Verify and hand off" step.
# agent summarises in chat; no file is written
```

```text file=Wizard
STEP 9 - Summarise and hand off. (skill: "Verify and hand off")
   Write the hand-off to `${reportFile}` at the WIZARD'S WORKING DIRECTORY,
   never prefixed with the selected project directory. Cover: the files
   you changed (paths only), the exact build and upload commands, every CI
   secret the user still has to create, and how to verify the upload.
   Never write secret values into the report, only variable names.
   The success screen points the user at this file, so do not skip it.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Automated browser checks see zero events</summary>

**Problem.** posthog-js silently drops every capture from Playwright, Puppeteer or Selenium as a likely bot while /flags and asset loads still return 200, so the network tab looks healthy. Nothing in the integration docs warns about this, so the agent blames its own init code.

**Impact.** False negative: a working integration is rewritten or reported broken, or the agent ships opt_out_useragent_filter: true in production init to make the test pass.

```ts file=Generic agent
await page.goto('http://localhost:3000')
await page.click('#signup')
// /flags and array.js 200, no /e capture requests
// agent: 'events are not sending' -> rewrites init,
// or ships opt_out_useragent_filter: true
```

```ts file=Wizard
// test harness only, never app code
await context.addInitScript(() => {
  Object.defineProperty(navigator, 'webdriver', { get: () => false })
  // also override navigator.userAgent AND navigator.userAgentData
})
// diagnose with ?__posthog_debug=true and look for "likely bot" in the console
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Green build reported as events flowing</summary>

**Problem.** A passing build and a correct-looking diff feel like proof, and the agent never sees the running app.

**Impact.** Users merge believing events and identify work when the app sends nothing or only personless events.

```markdown file=Generic agent
## Summary
Build passes. Events are now flowing to PostHog:
- user_signed_up, note_created, check_created
- Identify wired for all users
(no event was observed arriving)
```

```markdown file=Wizard
## Verified
- Build and typecheck pass
## Not verified
- Event delivery: nothing in this run observed an event arrive
# rule: 'A passing build proves the code compiles, not that events flow -
# never write that an event was captured unless the run observed it arrive.'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Report claims done without a pre-merge checklist</summary>

**Problem.** Agents end with 'PostHog is integrated and working' or 'test it and check PostHog' because they built the files they touched, not knowing which failures are invisible from code, such as a CSP silently blocking the SDK, unset deploy env vars, or stale mocks.

**Impact.** A blocked SDK queues events silently and never sends. Users merge with unrun tests, unset deploy env vars and no CI source-map upload.

```markdown file=Generic agent
## Next steps
Test that everything works and check PostHog.
# or:
Setup complete. PostHog is integrated and working. No further action needed.
```

```markdown file=Wizard
## Before you merge
- [ ] Load the app and check the console for CSP violations
      (src/middleware.ts:12): a blocked SDK queues events silently
- [ ] Run a full production build (the wizard only verified the files it touched)
- [ ] Run the test suite: call sites may need updated mocks
- [ ] Env vars set in deploy environments, not just locally
- [ ] Wire source-map upload into CI if bundles are minified
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Full build never run after generated code</summary>

**Problem.** The agent checks only the files it touched and trusts that a lint directive or cast it wrote is right. The misplaced eslint-disable only fails under a full production build.

**Impact.** First `next build` fails on an unused disable directive plus an unsuppressed `any`, breaking CI right after the integration reported success.

```ts file=Generic agent
const client = new PostHogAnthropic({
  apiKey: process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY,
  // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
  posthog: phClient,
} as any)  // the `any` is here, the directive is not
// report: "Integration complete." (no build, no tests)
```

```markdown file=Wizard
## Verify before merging
- [ ] Run a full production build (the wizard only verified the files it touched)
      and fix any lint or type errors introduced by the generated code.
- [ ] Run the test suite - call sites that were rewritten or instrumented
      may need updated mocks or fixtures.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Skipped or failed products reported as configured</summary>

**Problem.** The agent summarizes intent rather than outcome and rounds partial results up to success. The outro renders every planned change under a success banner regardless of task status.

**Impact.** Users believe AI Observability, Logs or source-map upload is live when the task was skipped or failed.

```markdown file=Generic agent
Successfully installed PostHog!
- AI Observability: configured, events flowing
- Source-map upload is wired into every production build
(AIO task skipped; credentials and CI step skipped)
```

```markdown file=Wizard
- AI Observability: skipped (no LLM call sites found)
- Logs: configured, delivery unobserved; trigger X to confirm
- Source maps: failed, credentials not provided
# 'A skipped product is not a successful setup.'
# 'Do not make paid LLM calls or claim delivery based on code changes.'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>LLM or log delivery claimed without seeing it</summary>

**Problem.** After wiring AI Observability or Logs the agent either assumes events flow or proves it by making a model call itself, writing an API key into a file to make it work, though it holds no credentials.

**Impact.** Reports assert delivery nobody observed. Model costs land on the user and a secret may land in committed code.

```text file=Generic agent
[STATUS] AI Observability complete: $ai_generation events are flowing
# agent ran a live OpenAI call to check, or never checked at all:
echo "OPENAI_API_KEY=<real key>" >> smoke.env
python smoke_test.py   # agent calls the model itself
```

```text file=Wizard
Do not call the model yourself. You hold no credentials.
Do not put an API key in any file.
Name the smallest existing path (script, route, test) that runs one turn.
If no path exists, write one in the report as suggested code.
Report "wired, unverified" until the user confirms what landed.
If nothing arrives: vendor client still called, key or host unset, short script exits before flush.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>LLM wrapper import never executed</summary>

**Problem.** Wrapper module paths differ by provider and language, and some providers (Bedrock, Go) have no wrapper at all. The agent writes the import from memory without running it.

**Impact.** The app crashes at startup with ModuleNotFoundError when the wrapper does not exist for that provider or language.

```python file=Generic agent
from posthog.ai.bedrock import BedrockClient   # no such wrapper; Bedrock is OTel-only
# written, never executed; report says done
```

```bash file=Wizard
# Before you hand over, run the import the code depends on:
python3 -c "from posthog.ai.openai import OpenAI"
# If it fails, go back to 1-begin.md and pick another variant.
# Do not report a run as done while an import fails.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>One LLM call site left uninstrumented</summary>

**Problem.** The agent finds call sites with one narrow grep for a single constructor shape and reports the hit count as the whole set. A differently structured import is invisible and the miss is silent.

**Impact.** One agent loop keeps the raw vendor client, so its generations never produce $ai_generation events while the report claims full coverage.

```bash file=Generic agent
grep -rn "new Anthropic(" src/     # finds N sites
# site N+1 lives in another module with a different import shape
# report: "instrumented N Anthropic call sites"
```

```markdown file=Wizard
# integration 4-conclude.md, 'Verify before merging' checklist:
- [ ] Trigger the LLM call path(s) you instrumented and confirm
      `$ai_generation` events appear in PostHog AI Observability.
# ai-observability 4-verify.md, 'Nothing arrives' cause:
#   the code still calls the vendor client (a missed call site)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Outro hides sources left unconnected</summary>

**Problem.** It is natural to assume a step marked done handled every input it was given, so the follow-up links get filtered out.

**Impact.** Sources whose credential prompts were dismissed or timed out get no next-step link at all.

```ts file=Generic agent
buildWarehouseNextSteps(sess, host, projectId, completedSeededTypes)
if (completedSeededTypes.includes(WAREHOUSE_SEED_TASK_TYPE)) return undefined;
// drops every source when the seeded step finished 'done'
```

```ts file=Wizard
buildWarehouseNextSteps(sess, host, projectId)
const listed = sources.slice(0, WAREHOUSE_LINK_LIMIT);
// lists every detected source; the limit only caps how many get a bullet
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Elixir verification limited to mix compile</summary>

**Problem.** `mix test`, `mix run` and `mix phx.server` are the obvious Elixir checks, but they run project-defined code (app code, tests, servers). The fence admits only dependency and build tasks, and a review bot noted that Mix aliases in mix.exs can still hide arbitrary code behind task names.

**Impact.** The fence refuses them, so Elixir verification is compile and format only and cannot prove events arrive.

```bash file=Generic agent
mix test
mix run -e 'PostHog.capture("smoke", %{distinct_id: "x"})'
mix phx.server
```

```ts file=Wizard
// mix runs arbitrary project-defined tasks, so only the dependency + build
// tasks are listed. run/test/phx.server are denied.
mix: ['deps.get', 'deps.update', 'deps.tree', 'compile', 'format', 'hex.info'],
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Native analyzer skipped when no manifest scripts</summary>

**Problem.** Verification prose assumes a JS app and reads package.json scripts. Flutter, Go, Rails or Gradle projects have none, so the agent concludes there is nothing to run, though the toolchain ships its own analyzer the project enforces.

**Impact.** Analyzer and lint errors the project enforces ship unchecked while the run says 'Build verified'.

```text file=Generic agent
# pubspec.yaml has no scripts block
# agent: 'no build/lint scripts found, nothing to run'
# flutter analyze / go vet never run
# agent: 'Build verified.'
```

```text file=Wizard
Run the project's build (or typecheck) and lint scripts if they exist
(check the manifest's scripts). Where the toolchain ships its own analyzer rather
than manifest scripts, run that instead.
// fence admits it: FLUTTER_DART_SUBCOMMANDS = ['analyze','build','clean','doctor']
//                  GO_SUBCOMMANDS = ['get','build','vet','fmt','version','list']
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Command fence blocks the project's own checks</summary>

**Problem.** A short command allowlist misses the linters and project generators real repos use (biome, ruff, xcodegen, python -m compileall), so the agent is denied and either burns turns or skips verification.

**Impact.** Edited files are never proven to parse, build or import the SDK, and broken integrations ship as done.

```bash file=Generic agent
npx biome check .
→ Bash command not allowed: `npx biome check .`
xcodegen generate
→ `xcodegen` is not an allowed tool.
python3 -m compileall app/
→ `python3` is not an allowed tool.
```

```ts file=Wizard
export const LINTING_TOOLS: string[] = ['biome', 'prettier', 'eslint', 'ruff', /* ~130 */]
xcodegen: ['generate'],  // materialise project.yml so xcodebuild can verify
// python3: -m compileall <files> (byte-compiles, never imports project code)
// xcodebuild: every action except test / test-without-building
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Python edits verified by running the app</summary>

**Problem.** Python has no typecheck script, so agents prove edits by importing or running the app, which executes project code and is denied by the fence.

**Impact.** The verify step either runs untrusted project code or is skipped. The agent thrashed between pip, venv and compileall attempts.

```bash file=Generic agent
python3 -c "import app"
python3 app/server.py
```

```bash file=Wizard
python3 -m compileall app config.py   # byte-compiles without importing
.venv/bin/python -m compileall server.py
.venv/bin/ruff check app
# still denied: python -c, python script.py, .venv/bin/pytest
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Swift build fails without generating the Xcode project</summary>

**Problem.** Projects defined by project.yml do not commit the .xcodeproj, so xcodebuild has nothing to build until xcodegen generate runs, and a strict toolchain allowlist denies it.

**Impact.** The Swift run cannot build-verify its edits, so a broken integration ships unverified.

```bash file=Generic agent
$ xcodegen generate
→ Bash command not allowed: `xcodegen generate`. `xcodegen` is not an allowed tool.
$ xcodebuild -scheme BurritoStand build   # no .xcodeproj yet
```

```ts file=Wizard
// bash-fence.ts
// Materializes the Xcode project from project.yml so xcodebuild can verify -
// build-equivalent risk (runs on the project's own spec).
xcodegen: ['generate'],  // other subcommands (e.g. dump) are not listed, so denied
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Never-compiled change presented as done</summary>

**Problem.** When the runner lacks the toolchain (no JRE), the agent cannot build or run the project's format gate, and a generic agent simply reports success instead of saying it could not check.

**Impact.** The change fails the repo's formatter or build in CI. The user is never told it was not compiled.

```text file=Generic agent
// No JRE on runner: mvn compile never ran
// spring-javaformat check fails on PetController.java
setup report: "PostHog integration complete"
```

```ts file=Wizard
conflict: 'A one-line summary of any conflict you could not cleanly resolve (e.g. a dependency or build conflict)...'
// orchestrator-runner.ts outro:
const message = conflict
  ? 'PostHog set up, with one conflict to review.'
  : /* normal success message */
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Framework rules never checked against the diff</summary>

**Problem.** Rules framed as 'follow these when integrating' read as background advice, and review only checks that the code compiles. A missing wiring like Flutter Web init compiles fine, because Setup() is a silent no-op on web.

**Impact.** Required wiring such as screen tracking or web init is missing while the build passes. Web builds capture nothing.

```dart file=Generic agent
// diff: lib/main.dart
await Posthog().setup(config);
// project ships web/index.html; the rule says Flutter Web
// needs the posthog-js snippet -> nothing in the diff answers it
// COMMANDMENTS.md: 'Follow these when integrating PostHog into this framework.'
```

```text file=Wizard
## Build and verify
Before building, re-read the framework rules you were given and check each one
that applies to this project against the diff you are about to verify. A rule
naming a file or a wiring this project has, with nothing in the diff answering
it, is unfinished work - do it now. This is the last step that can catch it.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Created provider never imported or mounted</summary>

**Problem.** Creating the file feels like the work. Nothing fails when it is never imported, so the build stays green.

**Impact.** PostHog never initializes while the file exists and the build passes.

```tsx file=Generic agent
// components/PostHogProvider.tsx  created
// app/layout.tsx: never imports or renders it
export default function RootLayout({ children }) {
  return <html><body>{children}</body></html>
}
```

```tsx file=Wizard
// 3-revise.md: "Ensure that any components created were actually used."
import { PostHogProvider } from '@/components/PostHogProvider'
export default function RootLayout({ children }) {
  return <html><body>
    <PostHogProvider>{children}</PostHogProvider>
  </body></html>
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Denied SDK install reported as success</summary>

**Problem.** The fence allows the install command but the sandbox denies the write, often at a monorepo root. The agent hand-edits the manifest and moves on.

**Impact.** Report and outro claim PostHog was added when the package was never installed.

```bash file=Generic agent
pnpm add posthog-js   # denied by sandbox at workspace root
# agent edits package.json by hand, reports done, no conflict
```

```text file=Wizard
a failed install is a conflict to report (below), not a problem to fight.
...
A build or install you can't cleanly finish is a perfectly acceptable outcome -
as long as you report it. ... put a one-line summary in your handoff `conflict`
field and the full detail in `did`, then complete the task.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Pre-existing build errors fail the review step</summary>

**Problem.** A red build looks like the agent's failure, so it marks the task failed even when the errors are in files it never touched.

**Impact.** Dashboard and report depend on review, so they never run and the user gets no report.

```ts file=Generic agent
complete_task({ status: 'failed' })
// npm run lint fails in files the run never touched
// dashboard and report depend on review -> stranded
```

```text file=Wizard
If the build or lint fails only on pre-existing errors you did not
introduce, that still counts - put a one-line summary in the `conflict`
field and the detail in what you did. Reserve a failed status for when
your own changes break the build.
(build: A `failed` status stops later steps that depend on this one.)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Review flags unverifiable delivery as a finding</summary>

**Problem.** A reviewer that cannot run the app records 'could not verify events arrive', which feels diligent but is not actionable.

**Impact.** Noise in the handoff while real defects like dead captures are under-weighted.

```text file=Generic agent
review handoff: 'Finding: unable to verify events reach PostHog
in production; recommend running the app and checking live events.'
```

```text file=Wizard
a capture wired to a place it will never fire, an uninitialized SDK,
or a call the runtime silently drops is squarely yours to catch and fix
- it is a code defect. What you cannot do is confirm delivery: you
cannot exercise the app, and you never treat "I could not verify events
arrive" as a finding.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Task handoff claims success without evidence</summary>

**Problem.** Agents summarize what they expect to work, not what they ran, so later stages trust unverified claims as facts.

**Impact.** The review stage cannot audit. Assumptions about the app ship as facts.

```ts file=Generic agent
complete_task({ status: 'done', handoff: {
  goals: 'Add PostHog',
  did: 'Added provider, everything should work',
  forNextAgent: 'Capture events next' } })
```

```ts file=Wizard
handoff: {
  did: 'for each file you edited: the change, the intention behind it, and the analytics it should feed',
  filesTouched: ['app/layout.tsx'],
  evidence: 'what you ran or observed, not what you expect',
  assumptions: 'what you assumed about the app and could not verify' }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Partial edits from a failed optional task</summary>

**Problem.** Review trusts handoffs to describe every edit, but an optional task that failed mid-run wrote no handoff for what it left behind.

**Impact.** Dangling imports or half-wired instrumentation ship, or the build fails on code no handoff mentions.

```js file=Generic agent
// index.js
import { posthogLogs } from './posthog-logs'  // logs task failed; file never written
// no handoff mentions index.js, so review never opens it
```

```text file=Wizard
An optional upstream task (AI Observability, Logs) may have failed and
left partial edits no handoff describes - install and build either way,
and let the build surface stray imports; fix or revert them like any
other defect.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Report written before review finishes</summary>

**Problem.** Report and dashboard look independent of review, so they get queued in parallel and describe code the reviewer then changes.

**Impact.** The report documents the first draft, including files and behavior that no longer exist.

```yaml file=Generic agent
# report.md frontmatter
dependsOn: [dashboard]   # review still running, fixes land later
```

```yaml file=Wizard
# report.md frontmatter
sink: true
dependsOn: [dashboard, review]   # describes the reviewed integration
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Stale 'deferred' claims copied into the report</summary>

**Problem.** The report agent copies early handoffs verbatim in order and does not notice a later task already resolved what they deferred, so a failed product can also read as set up.

**Impact.** The report tells the user to do work the run already did, or hides work never done.

```markdown file=Generic agent
Dependencies: posthog-js added to package.json, run npm install
(install handoff said 'deferred'; review step installed it)
Report: "Logs configured."                   # task skipped or failed
```

```text file=Wizard
Handoffs are written mid-run, in task order, so a later handoff
supersedes an earlier one's forward-looking claims. Report the final
state, and let no "deferred" or "pending" claim survive into the report.
Do not treat a skipped or failed task as a successful setup. A product
whose task was never queued is left out of the report entirely.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Manual follow-ups buried in report prose</summary>

**Problem.** Agents mention what the user still owes (load .env, CI secrets) as a passing remark near the bottom, so the run reads as finished.

**Impact.** The user closes the run believing setup is done. The app then crashes at boot or never reports.

```markdown file=Generic agent
# PostHog error tracking report
...paragraphs about capture, build and CI...
(near the bottom) you may also want to load .env
```

```markdown file=Wizard
# PostHog error tracking report
> ⚠️ **Needs your attention**
> - Load `.env` in the app, or set `POSTHOG_API_KEY` in its environment.
# report.md: summary in chat, warning block first.
# exit-line.ts prints "⚠ Needs your attention:" + the bullets again on exit
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Outro points to a report file never written</summary>

**Problem.** The prompt says to write a report file while the handoff tool says not to, so which one happens depends on the model, and the outro prints the path without checking it exists.

**Impact.** The outro says 'Report saved to:' a file that does not exist, and the grader fails correct runs.

```text file=Generic agent
skillPrompt: "write a brief markdown report to ./${reportFile}"
publish_handoff: "never write the report to a file yourself"
Outro: Report saved to: <installDir>/posthog-audit-report.md
```

```ts file=Wizard
// src/agent/tools/handoff.ts: one channel, the tool
'...call it only when your own instructions ask you to write that report,
 passing the complete report as `content`, and never write the report
 to a file yourself.'
// and the outros never check the file exists.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Handoff checklist lists steps that do not apply</summary>

**Problem.** The agent pads the verify checklist with every generic item it knows, such as LLM event checks, regardless of what this run set up.

**Impact.** Developers chase checks for products that were never installed.

```markdown file=Generic agent
## Verify before merging
- [ ] Confirm $ai_generation events appear   # run set up no LLM analytics
```

```markdown file=Wizard
Include ONLY the items that actually apply to the integration you just performed - judge each against the code you changed in this run, and drop any that don't fit.
- If LLM analytics was set up in this run: "Trigger the LLM call path(s) you instrumented and confirm `$ai_generation` events appear..."
Do not invent items beyond what applies. If only the two "Always" items apply, the checklist is just those two.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Run that changed no code reported as success</summary>

**Problem.** Reaching the end of the flow, adding the dependency or writing a report looks like success. When the install is blocked or the model returns nothing, the agent writes an event plan and stops, and a runner that treats any stop as completion cannot tell.

**Impact.** Silent: the outro says PostHog is set up, nothing is instrumented, no events arrive, and loose E2E checks pass.

```text file=Generic agent
setup report: full event plan; 'composer require posthog/posthog-php was rejected'
$ git diff --stat
 package.json | 3 +++
 .env.local   | 2 ++   # no init file, no capture() calls
wizard outro: success
// E2E: assert(hasPosthogDep && skillsComplete) -> PASS
```

```ts file=Wizard
export function completionFailure(args: { toolCalls: number; openTasks: boolean }) {
  // No tool call at all - the project was never touched.
  if (args.toolCalls === 0) return AgentErrorType.NO_PROGRESS;
  // Acted but left tasks in its own plan unfinished.
  if (args.openTasks) return AgentErrorType.INCOMPLETE_TASKS;
  return undefined;
}
// pi/index.ts: NO_PROGRESS -> spinner.stop('Agent made no changes'); linear.ts: 'The Wizard exited without changing your project'
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Agent ends the run with tasks still open</summary>

**Problem.** Literal models end a turn with only a status line and no tool call. The pi harness treats that as the end of the run.

**Impact.** The integration finishes half done while looking complete.

```text file=Generic agent
assistant: "[STATUS] Wiring up the login capture"   // text only, no tool call
// prompt() resolves, run ends; Instrument/Verify/Dashboard still pending
```

```ts file=Wizard
while (nudges < MAX_CONTINUE_NUDGES && hasOpenTasks(store)) {
  await agentSession.prompt('You still have open tasks in your list... ' +
    'pick up the next in_progress or pending task. Continue now.')
  nudges++
}
// MAX_CONTINUE_NUDGES = 20; runtime note: always pair [STATUS] with a tool call
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Failed or empty task queue shows success</summary>
