```text file=Generic agent
# Next.js app -> install_skill metrics-javascript (posthog-js)
posthog.metrics.count("button.clicks")   // runs in the browser
```

```text file=Wizard
# context/agents/metrics/verify-sdk.md
install_skill for the variant matching the project's platform -- the menu
is the source of truth, one variant per platform, and a full-stack app
takes the server variant because metrics measure service work.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Server log exporter added to browser app</summary>

**Problem.** Asked for logs, the agent installs the OTLP exporter wherever code runs, without checking the app is client-only.

**Impact.** Server exporter packages and credentials ship to the browser or break the bundle.

```ts file=Generic agent
// Vite SPA, src/main.tsx (no server)
import { NodeSDK } from '@opentelemetry/sdk-node'
import { OTLPLogExporter } from '@opentelemetry/exporter-logs-otlp-http'
// server exporter + project token in browser-only code
```

```text file=Wizard
A browser-only app must not receive a server exporter. If no documented
setup applies, complete as `not needed` and explain why.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Hand-rolled captures in an MCP server's tool handlers</summary>

**Problem.** Without knowing @posthog/mcp exists, the agent adds posthog.capture to each tool handler, and doesn't stop when there is no MCP server.

**Impact.** No $mcp_\* events or agent intent, behavior changes inside tool handlers, and edits to projects that have no MCP server.

```ts file=Generic agent
server.registerTool("search", schema, async (args) => {
  posthog.capture({ distinctId: "mcp", event: "search_called", properties: args });
  return runSearch(args);
});
```

```ts file=Wizard
const server = new McpServer({ name: "my-mcp-server", version: "1.0.0" })
// wrap once, right after constructing it; do not restructure tool handlers
const analytics = instrument(server, posthog, { captureModel: true })
// no MCP server found -> emit `[ABORT] no mcp server found` and stop
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>MCP analytics wrapped around unsupported servers</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to add MCP analytics, the agent wraps any MCP server, even one on a third-party library or an old runtime the PostHog MCP SDK cannot wrap.

**Impact.** Broken or non-compiling instrumentation, and the wrong install package per language.

```go file=Generic agent
import (
)
// posthogmcpsdk.Instrument expects the official go-sdk *mcp.Server
posthogmcpsdk.Instrument(server, posthogmcp.New(client))
```

```ts file=Wizard
match: /^go mcp server is not built on the official go-sdk$/i
match: /^go toolchain older than 1\.25$/i
match: /^ruby mcp server is not built on the official mcp gem$/i
match: /^ruby older than 3\.0$/i
match: /^ruby mcp gem older than 1\.4$/i
// abort with a clear message; the Ruby prompt says the SDK is experimental
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Debug logging corrupts a stdio MCP server</summary>

**Problem.** console.log or print() is the reflex for confirming setup, but on a stdio MCP server stdout is the protocol channel.

**Impact.** Stray output corrupts the MCP stream and breaks the client connection.

```python file=Generic agent
analytics = instrument(server, posthog)
print("PostHog MCP analytics enabled")   # stdout = the MCP channel
```

```python file=Wizard
# never print() to stdout on STDIO; route SDK warnings to stderr or a file
MCPAnalyticsOptions(logger=lambda m: print(m, file=sys.stderr))
# JS: no console.log; pass a `logger` to instrument() that writes to stderr
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>NestJS MCP server has no constructor to wrap</summary>

**Problem.** The agent looks for new McpServer(...), but @rekog/mcp-nest builds the server inside McpModule, and returning instrument()'s handle from serverMutator replaces the server.

**Impact.** No insertion point is found, or the module receives the analytics handle instead of the server and breaks.

```ts file=Generic agent
// no `new McpServer(...)` in user code: McpModule builds it
McpModule.forRoot({
  serverMutator: (server) => instrument(server, posthog), // returns the handle
})
```

```ts file=Wizard
import { PostHog, instrumentMutator } from "@posthog/mcp"

McpModule.forRoot({
  name: "my-mcp-server",
  version: "1.0.0",
  serverMutator: instrumentMutator(posthog),  // instruments and returns the server
})
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>go.mod alone routes a polyglot repo to Go</summary>

**Problem.** The agent treats any go.mod as proof the LLM calls are made from Go, without finding the call sites.

**Impact.** A Node or Python LLM app beside a Go service gets the Go variant and no instrumentation on the real calls.

```text file=Generic agent
repo: go.mod (infra service) + package.json (openai calls)
agent picks: opentelemetry-go
```

```text file=Wizard
1. Model calls made from Go take OpenTelemetry (opentelemetry-go).
A go.mod on its own is not the signal.
Also a package.json / pyproject.toml / requirements.txt? Find the call sites before you route.
A Go service beside a Node or Python LLM app takes the rules below. Still unclear -> wizard_ask.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Provider instrumented instead of the agent framework</summary>

**Problem.** The agent matches the first familiar provider package like openai or @google/genai and instruments it, missing the agent framework built on top, especially when the framework table never mentioned it (ADK).

**Impact.** Model calls are kept but agent, tool and handoff structure is lost. The framework's own tracing hook is never used.

```python file=Generic agent
# manifest has openai-agents, which wraps openai
from opentelemetry.instrumentation.openai_v2 import OpenAIInstrumentor
OpenAIInstrumentor().instrument()
# package.json: "@google/adk", "@google/genai" -> wraps only the genai client
```

```text file=Wizard
Rule 2: a framework wins over the provider under it.
| openai-agents | variant `openai-agents` |
| langgraph     | variant `langgraph-{python,node}` |
| `@google/adk` | variant `google-adk` |
Instrument the framework, not the provider below it.
Step 3: agent frameworks use their own tracing hook from the install doc.
Do not substitute an OTel instrumentor.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>OpenAI-compatible gateway routed as plain OpenAI</summary>

**Problem.** The manifest shows only the openai package. The real provider is visible only in the client's base_url or OPENAI_BASE_URL.

**Impact.** Install shape matches but the provider is wrong, so events are labelled and priced as OpenAI.

```python file=Generic agent
client = OpenAI(base_url='https://api.groq.com/openai/v1',
                api_key=os.environ['GROQ_API_KEY'])
# agent: manifest has `openai` -> install ai-observability-openai-python
```

```text file=Wizard
Rule 3: an `openai` client with a base URL override means a gateway.
Check the client constructor and OPENAI_BASE_URL (api.groq.com, openrouter.ai,
api.together.xyz, localhost:11434). Pick the variant that names the provider.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>React Native provider wraps NavigationContainer</summary>

**Problem.** Wrapping the whole app in the provider is the normal React pattern, but the SDK's screen hook needs react-navigation hooks, which fail outside the container with only a console error.

**Impact.** Screen views are never captured on React Navigation v7.

```tsx file=Generic agent
<PostHogProvider client={posthog} autocapture={{ captureScreens: true }}>
  <NavigationContainer>
    <RootNavigator />
  </NavigationContainer>
</PostHogProvider>
```

```tsx file=Wizard
<NavigationContainer ref={navigationRef} onStateChange={() => {
  const current = navigationRef.current?.getCurrentRoute()?.name
  if (current && current !== routeNameRef.current)
    posthog.screen(current, { previous_screen: routeNameRef.current })
  routeNameRef.current = current }}>
  <PostHogProvider client={posthog} autocapture={{ captureScreens: false, captureTouches: true }}>
    <RootNavigator />
  </PostHogProvider>
</NavigationContainer>
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Expo Router screens never tracked</summary>

**Problem.** The agent expects PostHogProvider autocapture to emit $screen events, but Expo Router has no NavigationContainer for the SDK to observe.

**Impact.** $screen events are silently missing for route changes. The wizard's own example spreads route params into the event, with a comment to filter sensitive values.

```tsx file=Generic agent
// app/_layout.tsx
<PostHogProvider client={posthog} autocapture>
  <Stack />
</PostHogProvider>
// no posthog.screen() anywhere
```

```tsx file=Wizard
const pathname = usePathname()
const params = useGlobalSearchParams()
useEffect(() => {
  if (previousPathname.current !== pathname) {
    posthog.screen(pathname, { previous_screen: previousPathname.current ?? null, ...params })
    previousPathname.current = pathname
  }
}, [pathname, params]) // provider autocapture: { captureScreens: false }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Replay vision run on backend-only apps</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to set up or scan session replay, the agent complies on an API that serves no pages, installing posthog-js into it, or on a KMP project with no replay support, instead of recognizing there are no recordings.

**Impact.** A wasted agent run and a false 'configured' report.

```ts file=Generic agent
// Express JSON API, no pages served
import posthog from 'posthog-js'
posthog.init(token, { session_recording: { maskAllInputs: true } })
```

```ts file=Wizard
// src/programs/replay-vision/index.ts: platform gate before any agent work
if (!REPLAY_VISION_SUPPORTED.has(integration)) {
  abortUnsupportedPlatform(integration)
}
// "Session replay isn't available for Node.js projects..."
// zero diff, analytics.wizardCapture event, not captureException
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Server-rendered app gets no browser snippet</summary>

**Problem.** In a Rails or Laravel app the server SDK feels like the full integration, since there is no separate frontend package to install, and the agent forgets the views render a browser app.

**Impact.** No pageviews, autocapture, session replay or client-side errors. Only backend events arrive.

```text file=Generic agent
# Gemfile: posthog-ruby, posthog-rails
# diff touches only config/initializers/posthog.rb and controllers
# app/views/layouts/application.html.erb unchanged
```

```html file=Wizard
<%# app/views/layouts/application.html.erb, in <head> %>
<script>
  !function(t,e){/* posthog-js snippet */}(document,window.posthog||[]);
  posthog.init('<%= ENV["POSTHOG_PROJECT_TOKEN"] %>', {
    api_host: '<%= ENV.fetch("POSTHOG_HOST", "https://us.i.posthog.com") %>',
  })
</script>
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PostHogProvider assumed in non-React frameworks</summary>

**Problem.** Most PostHog examples the agent has seen are React, so it plans around a provider wrapper even in Vue, Svelte, Astro or Django.

**Impact.** Non-React integrations get a made-up provider concept in code, todo labels and status messages.

```text file=Generic agent
- [ ] Add PostHogProvider to the root layout   // Vue, Svelte, Astro or Django app
status: "Wrapping the app in PostHogProvider"
```

```text file=Wizard
Do not assume "PostHog provider" or "PostHogProvider" in task labels,
status messages, or code. Only use a provider if the skill workflow
explicitly requires one for the specific framework.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Workers runtime hidden behind a framework adapter</summary>

**Problem.** An Astro, SvelteKit or React Router app looks like a Node SSR app. Only the adapter package or wrangler config reveals it runs on Workers.

**Impact.** Node-shaped code (process.env, shared batching client, no waitUntil) runs on Workers, so env values are missing and events are dropped.

```ts file=Generic agent
// astro.config.mjs: adapter: cloudflare()  (package.json: @astrojs/cloudflare, wrangler)
// src/lib/posthog.ts: shared batching client reading process.env
// no waitUntil: treated as a plain Node SSR Astro app
```

```ts file=Wizard
const CLOUDFLARE_PACKAGES = ['@react-router/cloudflare', '@astrojs/cloudflare',
  '@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare', '@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare-workers',
  '@cloudflare/workers-types', 'wrangler']
// detectCloudflareTarget(installDir): true if wrangler.@(toml|jsonc|json)
// exists in the root or any package above is in dependencies/devDependencies
// fetchCloudflareReference(skillsBaseUrl) -> GET <skillsBaseUrl>/cloudflare-workers.md
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>inject() rule refactors existing Angular classes</summary>

**Problem.** The commandment 'use inject() instead of constructor injection' reads as a codebase-wide rule, so the agent converts existing constructors it only needed to add a call to.

**Impact.** Unrelated diff noise and merge risk in files the integration should barely touch.

```diff file=Generic agent
- constructor(private readonly _credentialsService: CredentialsService) {}
+ private readonly _credentialsService = inject(CredentialsService);
+ private readonly posthogService = inject(PostHogService);
```

```ts file=Wizard
// new PostHog code uses inject(); existing constructor injection stays
// (kept by the minimal-edits rule, not by the angular rule itself)
constructor(private readonly _credentialsService: CredentialsService) {}
private readonly posthogService = inject(PostHogService);
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Unused server client scaffolded from the example</summary>

**Problem.** The agent copies the SSR example's file layout and creates a server client even when no API route captures anything. The astro-ssr and astro-hybrid commandments say to create the singleton and do not say to skip it when nothing uses it.

**Impact.** Dead code, an empty committed file and an unused posthog-node dependency.

```text file=Generic agent
// src/lib/posthog-server.ts  -> 0 bytes (empty)
// package.json: "posthog-node" added, imported by no page or route
```

```ts file=Wizard
// Current rule: server client lives in an API route under src/pages/api/
// (no rule yet says: skip it when no route captures)
// src/lib/posthog-server.ts
export function getPostHogServer(): PostHog { ... }  // flushAt: 1, flushInterval: 0
// route: capture(...); await posthog.flush(); return new Response(...)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>TanStack Router pageviews tracked with useEffect</summary>

**Problem.** The generic React pattern is a useEffect on location. The agent does not know TanStack Router has navigation events built for this.

**Impact.** Duplicate or missed pageviews, because effects fire on re-renders and mounts, not navigations.

```tsx file=Generic agent
const location = useLocation()
useEffect(() => {
  posthog.capture('$pageview')
}, [location.pathname])
```

```ts file=Wizard
// Rule: use TanStack Router's built-in navigation events for pageviews,
// never a useEffect. Example app init (defaults date enables history_change):
posthog.init(key, { api_host: host, defaults: '2026-01-30' })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>SwiftUI screens named by internal view identifiers</summary>

**Problem.** Turning on captureScreenViews works for UIKit and produces $screen events, so the agent assumes SwiftUI is covered. The names it produces are internal view identifiers and nothing flags it.

**Impact.** Silent: $screen events carry internal SwiftUI view names or are missing, so screen analytics are unreadable.

```swift file=Generic agent
config.captureScreenViews = true   // SwiftUI names come out as internal view identifiers
PostHogSDK.shared.setup(config)
// or:
.onAppear { PostHogSDK.shared.capture("dashboard_viewed") }
```

```swift file=Wizard
VStack { /* ... */ }
    .padding()
    .postHogScreenView("BurritoStand")   // meaningful name
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Flutter platform minimums left below the SDK floor</summary>

**Problem.** Adding posthog_flutter to pubspec looks complete. The native iOS, macOS and Android minimums live in separate files the agent does not check.

**Impact.** Native builds fail to resolve pods or Gradle dependencies. The SDK needs iOS 13.0, macOS 10.15 and minSdk 23.

```ruby file=Generic agent
# ios/Podfile
platform :ios, '12.0'   # below the SDK floor
# android/app/build.gradle.kts: minSdk = 21
```

```ruby file=Wizard
# ios/Podfile: posthog_flutter requires iOS 13.0+ (raise only when lower or missing)
platform :ios, '13.0'
# macos/Podfile (example app only, not in the commandment)
platform :osx, '10.15'
# android: minSdk >= 23 (example: minSdk = flutter.minSdkVersion, default 24)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Laravel config:cache ignores the server .env</summary>

**Problem.** Writing to .env is the normal Laravel habit. After `config:cache`, .env is not loaded at runtime, and the SDK reads getenv(), not config().

**Impact.** Silent: the release ID never arrives at runtime.

```bash file=Generic agent
# deploy step
echo "POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID=$ID" >> /srv/app/.env
php artisan config:cache     # .env is no longer read at runtime
```

```dockerfile file=Wizard
# must be a real process env var: container ENV, FPM pool env[...], or service env
ARG POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID
ENV POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID=$POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID
# plus env[POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID] = $POSTHOG_RELEASE_ID in the FPM pool
```

</details>

## MCP, dashboards, and insights

<details>
<summary>Agent recreates a retired replay signal source</summary>

**Problem.** Stale training data and an old prompt both say to enable the session_replay signal source, which PostHog has since deleted.

**Impact.** Silent: a Session Replay source is on but never emits. The run recreates the row the server migration deleted.

```text file=Generic agent
inbox-source-configs-create
  { source_product: "session_replay",
    source_type: "session_analysis_cluster", enabled: true }
```

```text file=Wizard
Session replay: no source to enable. Coverage = Replay Vision scanners
(step 6c). The pair is retired; listed under "Nothing to create here"
so a future run does not add it back.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Inbox responder enabled with no data source</summary>

**Problem.** Flipping the responder toggle looks like the whole setup. The backing warehouse source is a separate step the agent defers.

**Impact.** Silent: the responder emits nothing until the user connects the source, which many never do.

```text file=Generic agent
inbox-source-configs-create { source_type: "zendesk", enabled: true }
# no external-data-sources-create; follow-up: "connect Zendesk later"
```

```text file=Wizard
external-data-sources-create {
  "source_type": "Zendesk",
  "payload": { "credential_id": "<stored id>", "schemas": [...] }
}
# dormant responder + follow-up only when the user skips or creds missing
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>PostHog MCP configured on retired /sse</summary>

**Problem.** Older docs and training data show the /sse endpoint, which the PostHog MCP no longer serves.

**Impact.** The configured MCP server never connects.

```ts file=Generic agent
const baseUrl = `${host}/${type === 'sse' ? 'sse' : 'mcp'}`
// https://mcp.posthog.com/sse
```

```ts file=Wizard
const baseUrl = `${host}/mcp`   // https://mcp.posthog.com/mcp, no transport arg
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Scanner prompt invents product details</summary>

**Problem.** Given a template with blanks like 'In this product that especially means: ...', the agent fills them with plausible flows even when the repo shows none.

**Impact.** A persisted scanner prompt asserts false specifics and skews what it reports.

```text file=Generic agent
In this product that especially means: the slot picker showing no available
courts, the payment step rejecting a valid card, ...   // no such flows in repo
```

```text file=Wizard
If the repo gives you nothing honest for a prompt blank, drop the scaffold
sentence that carries it rather than inventing content, and record that in
your handoff. A generic-but-true prompt beats a specific-but-fabricated one.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Insights created but not on the dashboard</summary>

**Problem.** insight-create succeeds without a `dashboards` field, so the agent never captures the dashboard id or links the two. Nothing errors.

**Impact.** Silent: the insights exist, but the dashboard link given to the user shows nothing.

```bash file=Generic agent
call dashboard-create {"name": "Analytics basics"}
call insight-create {"name": "Signup funnel", "query": {...}}
# no dashboards field, dashboard id never captured
```

```bash file=Wizard
call dashboard-create {"name": "Analytics basics (wizard)", "description": "...", "tags": ["wizard"]}
# capture the returned id, then for every insight:
call insight-create {"name": "Signup funnel (wizard)", "dashboards": [<dashboard id>], "query": {...}}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Insight SQL saved with unfilled placeholders</summary>

**Problem.** The agent writes parameterized HogQL like `{event_list}` as if a template engine will fill it. insight-create saves the query exactly as written, and the agent also stops the whole dashboard when one insight fails.

**Impact.** Insights store the literal placeholder and show no data, or the user gets no dashboard at all.

```text file=Generic agent
insight-create { "query": "... WHERE event IN ({event_list}) ..." }  // persisted verbatim
// one failed insight -> abort the dashboard step
```

```text file=Wizard
dashboard-create -> emit "[DASHBOARD_URL] <url>" immediately
insight-create { dashboards: [DASHBOARD_ID],
  query: { kind: 'DataVisualizationNode', source: { kind: 'HogQLQuery',
    query: "... event IN ('a','b','c') ..." } } }   // IN-list inlined
// continue on failure: a partial dashboard beats none
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Named MCP tools called on an exec-only server</summary>

**Problem.** Skills, docs and training data name tools like mcp\_\_posthog-wizard\_\_insight-create. The server now exposes one `exec` tool by default, so the agent hunts for names that no longer exist.

**Impact.** Tool-not-found errors: dashboard, audit, warehouse and self-driving calls all fail, and integration runs stopped completing the dashboard step. Post-install follow-ups stopped firing silently because every call showed up as 'exec'.

```text file=Generic agent
ToolSearch select:mcp__posthog-wizard__dashboard-create
mcp__posthog-wizard__dashboard-create {"name": "Analytics basics"}
mcp__posthog-wizard__insight-create({ "name": "Signup funnel (wizard)", ... })
mcp__posthog-wizard__execute-sql { "query": "SELECT ..." }
# server now exposes one `exec` tool; these names do not exist
```

```text file=Wizard
exec({ "command": "search dashboard-create|insight-create" })
exec({ "command": "info dashboard-create" })
exec({ "command": "call dashboard-create {\"name\": \"Analytics basics (wizard)\"}" })
# posthog_exec on the pi harness; every PostHog tool goes through exec
# skills use bare inner names via the {{> mcp-tool-calling}} partial;
# harness injects server instructions and recovers inner tool names for follow-ups
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Long skill resource names break tool calls</summary>

**Problem.** The skill builder used the full description sentence as the MCP resource name. Clients that make one tool per resource then derive names far over 128 characters, and nothing warns at build time.

**Impact.** One invalid tool name fails the whole request with a 400, and the agent makes zero tool calls.

```yaml file=Generic agent
name: "Add PostHog MCP analytics to a TypeScript, JavaScript, or Python MCP server. Instruments …"
# -> posthog_get_add_posthog_mcp_analytics_to_a_typescript_… (493 chars)
```

```yaml file=Wizard
name: "any TypeScript or JavaScript MCP server"
# -> posthog_get_any_typescript_or_javascript_mcp_server (51 chars)
# build-phases.js: name: skill.displayName ?? skill.name
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Audit repeats duplicate and dismissed findings</summary>

**Problem.** The agent copies every recommendation and health issue it reads. It does not know one is computed from the other, or that dismissed rows are the user's answer.

**Impact.** The report counts one problem twice and keeps raising findings the user already chose to ignore.

```text file=Generic agent
rows appended:
  live-data-source-maps                         (recommendation: source_maps)
  live-data-error-tracking-missing-source-maps (health kind restating it)
  + rows with completed: true or dismissed_at set
```

```text file=Wizard
Keep a recommendation only when completed is false AND dismissed_at is null.
Drop dismissed rows silently: dismissing is an answer.
Pairing: health error_tracking_missing_source_maps restates recommendation
  source_maps -> keep the recommendation, drop the health issue.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>HogQL written as raw SQL with no time range</summary>

**Problem.** Training data has far more ClickHouse and generic SQL than HogQL, so the agent uses JSONExtract and count(DISTINCT) and scans the full table with no time range. On ClickHouse-backed HogQL that is slow and costly, and nothing errors.

**Impact.** Queries are slow or time out, dashboards are expensive, and results do not cover the window the user wanted.

```sql file=Generic agent
SELECT JSONExtractString(properties, '$browser') AS b, count(DISTINCT person_id)
FROM events
WHERE event = '$pageview'
GROUP BY b
```

```sql file=Wizard
SELECT properties.$browser AS browser, uniq(person_id)
FROM events
WHERE event = '$pageview'
  AND timestamp >= now() - INTERVAL 7 DAY AND {filters}
GROUP BY browser
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Dashboard SQL ignores dashboard filters and dates</summary>

**Problem.** The agent hardcodes a date in the SQL tile as it would in any SQL editor. It does not know about the &#123;filters&#125; placeholders that connect the tile to the dashboard controls.

**Impact.** Silent: the dashboard's filter and date-range controls do nothing for that tile.

```sql file=Generic agent
SELECT toStartOfDay(timestamp) AS d, count()
FROM events
WHERE timestamp >= '2026-01-01'
GROUP BY d
```

```sql file=Wizard
SELECT dateTrunc('day', timestamp) AS d, count()
FROM events
WHERE {filters}
  AND timestamp >= {filters.dateRange.from}
  AND timestamp <= {filters.dateRange.to}
GROUP BY d
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Missing property compared to empty string</summary>

**Problem.** In standard SQL, NULL compared to '' is never true, so an agent assumes missing rows drop out. In HogQL `NULL != ''` is TRUE, and nothing errors.

**Impact.** Silent: every row missing the property counts as a match, so proxied vs direct counts are skewed.

```sql file=Generic agent
countIf(properties.$lib_custom_api_host != '') AS proxied
```

```sql file=Wizard
countIf(coalesce(properties.$lib_custom_api_host, '') != '') AS proxied
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Overlapping replay scanners corroborate themselves</summary>

**Problem.** Narrowing several scanners to the important URL feels like better targeting, but findings emit at half weight and are grouped by meaning, so overlapping scanners corroborate each other.

**Impact.** Two scanners on one session sum to the 1.0 threshold, so a single recording promotes itself into a report with no independent evidence.

```json file=Generic agent
{ "name": "Broken experiences", "emits_signals": true, "query": { "properties": [{ "key": "$current_url", "value": "/checkout" }] } }
{ "name": "Blocked conversion", "emits_signals": true, "query": { "properties": [{ "key": "$current_url", "value": "/checkout" }] } }
```

```json file=Wizard
// scanner 1 (breakage) owns WHERE: URL-scoped on the completion flow
"query": { "kind": "RecordingsQuery", "properties": [{ "key": "$current_url", "value": "/checkout", "operator": "icontains", "type": "event" }] }
// scanner 2 (frustration) owns WHAT: $rageclick as its only filter
"query": { "kind": "RecordingsQuery", "events": [{ "id": "$rageclick", "type": "events" }] }
// never URL-scope frustration; never event-gate breakage; summarizer stays out of self-driving
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Bug scanner limited to exception sessions</summary>

**Problem.** Filtering to $exception sessions feels like the obvious place breakage lives.

**Impact.** The scanner only sees what error tracking already catches and misses blank screens, dead buttons and wrong data.

```json file=Generic agent
"query": { "kind": "RecordingsQuery", "events": [{ "id": "$exception", "type": "events" }] }
```

```json file=Wizard
"query": { "kind": "RecordingsQuery",
  "properties": [{ "key": "$current_url", "value": "/checkout", "operator": "icontains", "type": "event" }] },
"sampling_rate": 0.5
// scope by WHERE plus sampling_rate, never by an outcome event
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Replay scanner scoped to guessed URL paths</summary>

**Problem.** /checkout is the generic default in the agent's head. The real routes live in router files it did not read.

**Impact.** The scanner watches sessions that never occur, or noise, at real credit cost.

```json file=Generic agent
"query": {"kind": "RecordingsQuery", "properties": [
  {"key": "$current_url", "value": "/checkout",
   "operator": "icontains", "type": "event"}]}
// app's flow is actually /booking
```

```text file=Wizard
Read router files and page/route directories to find the real paths;
never guess at `/checkout` if this app calls it `/booking`.
No identifiable completion flow? Fall back to the handful of
highest-traffic paths and record in your handoff that you couldn't
identify one. No web surface: skip creation and say why.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Scanner spend compared in wrong units</summary>

**Problem.** The estimate returns observations while quota is in credits, and both are plain numbers. The agent also infers quota from scanner count.

**Impact.** Spend is understated by the per-observation credit price, pushing the org over its monthly credit budget so observations are silently skipped, or the user is nagged about a cheap create.

```text file=Generic agent
estimate -> ~3000 observations per month
vision-quota-retrieve -> remaining = 5000 credits
3000 < 5000 -> create without asking
# or: project has no scanners -> assume full quota, skip the check
```

```text file=Wizard
skill-get {"skill_name": "creating-replay-vision-scanners"}
# estimate monthly CREDIT spend, read the org's remaining credit budget,
# compare credit to credit; quota is org-wide, never inferred from scanner count
# clearly cheap: just create. Large fraction of what's left (or exhausted):
# ONE wizard_ask, decline option first (create anyway vs skip)
# tool missing: create as-is, note 'spend not verified' in the report
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Replay scanners keep generic template names</summary>

**Problem.** The agent customizes only the query and keeps the template's fixed name and prompt, since that looks like a safe default.

**Impact.** A booking product gets a scanner literally named 'Broken experiences' with a generic prompt, not tailored to its codebase.

```json file=Generic agent
{"name": "Broken experiences", "scanner_type": "monitor",
 "scanner_config": {"prompt": "Watch this session for moments where the
   product visibly broke for the user: an error message, a blank screen..."},
 "query": {"kind": "RecordingsQuery", "properties": [/* /booking */]}}
```

```json file=Wizard
{"name": "Booking failures",   // short, sentence case, product's words
 "scanner_config": {"prompt": "...In this product that especially means:
   {{WATCH_FOR}}. Only flag issues that are unambiguous on screen..."}}
// {{WATCH_FOR}} = noun phrases from the repo, never instructions
// never reuse: Broken experiences / User frustration / Session summaries
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Source config created for self-authorizing scanner</summary>

**Problem.** replay_vision appears in the signal-source enum, so creating a config row for it looks required.

**Impact.** A redundant or wrong config row. The scanner's emits_signals flag is the per-source config.

```text file=Generic agent
call inbox-source-configs-create {"source_product": "replay_vision",
  "source_type": "scanner_finding", "enabled": true}
```

```text file=Wizard
# 4-sources.md: replay_vision is self-authorizing, no row to write
call vision-scanners-create {"name": "Booking failures", "scanner_type": "monitor",
  "emits_signals": true, ...}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Scout proposals ignore the repo's agent docs</summary>

**Problem.** The agent scans source and the event list cold, skipping AGENTS.md and architecture docs that map jobs, billing and out-of-scope areas.

**Impact.** Scouts miss surfaces, use the wrong vocabulary or target out-of-scope areas, adding recurring LLM spend for noise.

```text file=Generic agent
Gap analysis: scan source and the event taxonomy.
Proposed scout: "web app health" (generic wording)
(AGENTS.md / ARCHITECTURE.md never read)
```

```text file=Wizard
Start from AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, .cursor/rules/,
.github/copilot-instructions.md before scanning source. Name scouts in the
product's own vocabulary; check a gap isn't marked out of scope.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>GitHub repo researched via git remote when only one is connected</summary>

**Problem.** Parsing git remote is the reflex for 'which repo', but the integration only sees the repos the user connected. With exactly one connected the choice is forced.

**Impact.** Wasted calls, and a fork or renamed remote can pick a repo the integration cannot see.

```text file=Generic agent
git remote get-url origin   # parse owner/repo
integrations-github-repos-retrieve { integration_id, search: "<repo name>" }
```

```text file=Wizard
integrations-github-repos-retrieve { integration_id }   # no search
# exactly one repo connected -> use it, no git remote inference, confirm
# several -> research (git remote then validate); none -> dormant fallback
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Ticket source syncs every table incrementally</summary>

**Problem.** Incremental sync of everything is the usual default, but tickets are edited and closed after creation.

**Impact.** Needless sync load, and incremental append misses edits and closures, so the inbox acts on stale ticket state.

```json file=Generic agent
{ "source_type": "Zendesk", "payload": { "api_token": "...",
  "schemas": [{"name":"tickets","sync_type":"incremental"},
              {"name":"users","sync_type":"incremental"}] } }
```

```json file=Wizard
{ "source_type": "<Type>",
  "payload": { "credential_id": "<credential id>",
    "schemas": [{ "name": "<table>", "should_sync": true,
                  "sync_type": "full_refresh" }] } }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Re-run clobbers or re-arms the user's scanners</summary>

**Problem.** On a name collision the agent assumes the scanner is its own and overwrites it, or sets enabled: true as part of 'setup'.

**Impact.** User-owned scanners are overwritten, duplicated, or re-armed after a person paused them, burning credits.

```text file=Generic agent
vision-scanners-create {"name": "Broken experiences"} -> 400 name exists
// assume it's ours
vision-scanners-update {"id": "...", "prompt": "<skeleton>", "enabled": true}
```

```text file=Wizard
Ours only if ALL three hold: same scanner_type, prompt contains the brief's
literal match phrase, emits_signals matches the flow. Then update in place
(fresh blanks incl. name); leave `enabled` as it is. Otherwise it is theirs:
leave it untouched and report it. On a 400 for a unique name: fetch that one
scanner, apply the same test; if not ours, ONE rename attempt (append the
product name), then record a follow-up.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Wizard dashboards and notebooks not tagged</summary>

**Problem.** The agent gives created dashboards generic names like 'Analytics basics'. Nothing asks it to mark them as wizard-made.

**Impact.** Users cannot tell wizard-created dashboards and notebooks from hand-built ones, and one search for '(wizard)' cannot list them all.

```text file=Generic agent
dashboard-create { name: "Analytics basics" }
```

```text file=Wizard
dashboard-create { name: "Analytics basics (wizard)", tags: ["wizard"] }
notebooks-create-markdown { title: "PostHog audit (wizard) - <repo> - <timestamp>", markdown: "..." }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Insights skipped because events not ingested yet</summary>

**Problem.** A careful agent checks read-data-schema and sees that the events it just instrumented are not there yet. It treats an insight as a snapshot of data, not a definition over event names, so holding back looks cautious, and the run still reports success.

**Impact.** Silent: the run reports success but hands off a dashboard with zero insights, or no dashboard at all, and it never fills in.

```text file=Generic agent
read-data-schema -> no event named user_signed_up yet
"Events are not ingested yet, so I will skip the insights for now."
call dashboard-create {"name": "Analytics basics (wizard)"}
# no insight-create call follows; handoff: dashboard created, 0 insights
```

```text file=Wizard
Always create these insights based on the intended captures, regardless of
whether those events have been observed yet. An insight is a definition over
event names, not a snapshot of current data ... "No data ingested yet" ... are
never reasons to skip or defer insights - a dashboard handed off without them
is an incomplete integration, not a cautious one.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Trends breakdown written at the wrong level</summary>

**Problem.** Older PostHog filter formats, docs and training data show a top-level `breakdown` on TrendsQuery. The current schema wants a `breakdowns` array inside breakdownFilter, and the validation error does not name the supported shape.

**Impact.** insight-create rejects the payload with a vague schema error, which costs a failed call and a retry turn on the first dashboard. The dashboard may end up empty.

```json file=Generic agent
"source": {
  "kind": "TrendsQuery",
  "series": [{"kind": "EventsNode", "event": "user_signed_up"}],
  "breakdown": {"property": "plan", "type": "event"}
}
```

```json file=Wizard
"source": {
  "kind": "TrendsQuery",
  "series": [{ "kind": "EventsNode", "event": "user_signed_up", "math": "total" }],
  "interval": "day", "dateRange": { "date_from": "-30d" },
  "breakdownFilter": { "breakdowns": [{ "type": "event", "property": "plan" }] },
  "trendsFilter": { "display": "ActionsBar" }
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Funnel window fields misplaced or snake_case</summary>

**Problem.** The docs suggest the window fields go at the top level of FunnelsQuery, and the agent's fallback guess is the snake_case names from the legacy filters API. They must be camelCase inside funnelsFilter.

**Impact.** insight-create rejects the funnel, with about two failed funnel-create calls per run when hit.

```json file=Generic agent
"source": {
  "kind": "FunnelsQuery",
  "series": [ ... ],
  "funnelWindowInterval": 14,
  "funnelWindowIntervalUnit": "day",
  "funnelsFilter": { "funnel_window_interval": 14 }
}
```

```json file=Wizard
"source": {
  "kind": "FunnelsQuery",
  "series": [
    { "kind": "EventsNode", "event": "page_viewed" },
    { "kind": "EventsNode", "event": "user_signed_up" }
  ],
  "funnelsFilter": { "funnelVizType": "steps", "funnelOrderType": "ordered",
    "funnelWindowInterval": 14, "funnelWindowIntervalUnit": "day" }
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Guessed chart display names and retry loops</summary>

**Problem.** 'ActionsBarChart' sounds right, and the valid trendsFilter.display values were not in `info insight-create`, only in the error response. The agent guesses, then retries small variations instead of reading the error.

**Impact.** Every run that guesses loses at least one call and one retry turn, and some loop or skip the insight.

```json file=Generic agent
"trendsFilter": { "display": "ActionsBarChart" }   // rejected
// retry:
"trendsFilter": { "display": "ActionsBarGraph" }   // rejected
```

```json file=Wizard
"trendsFilter": { "display": "ActionsBar" }
// Valid trendsFilter.display values: ActionsLineGraph, ActionsBar,
// ActionsAreaGraph, ActionsPie, ActionsStackedBar, BoldNumber, ActionsTable.
// If an insight call is rejected, fix the payload against these examples
// rather than retrying variations.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Insight update called with short_id</summary>

**Problem.** The tool schema documents short_id as valid, so an agent fixing a rejected insight uses it, or invents insight_id. At runtime only the numeric id works.

**Impact.** Updates are rejected. One run failed a batch of 5 calls.

```bash file=Generic agent
call insight-update {"short_id": "AbC123xY", "name": "Signup funnel (wizard)"}
call insight-update {"insight_id": 42, "name": "Signup funnel (wizard)"}
```

```bash file=Wizard
# numeric id only, taken from the insight-create response
call insight-update {"id": 42, "name": "Signup funnel (wizard)"}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Created dashboards missing the wizard name tag</summary>

**Problem.** A generic agent gives saved artifacts natural names and changes the suffix casing freely. The tag is a wizard convention no doc asks for.

**Impact.** Silent: users cannot search for what the wizard created to find or clean it up.

```text file=Generic agent
dashboard-create { "name": "My Analytics Dashboard", "tags": [] }
insight-create   { "name": "Signups (Wizard)" }
insight-create   { "name": "Top errors this week" }
```

```text file=Wizard
dashboard-create { "name": "Analytics basics (wizard)",
  "description": "Key views for the events instrumented by the PostHog wizard.",
  "tags": ["wizard"] }
// every insight name ends with (wizard), exact casing
// MCP tutorial: every create AND rename keeps "(wizard MCP tutorial)"
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Agent edits the user's existing dashboards</summary>

**Problem.** With dashboard write scope, reusing or tidying an existing dashboard looks helpful and efficient. Nothing but the prompt wording stops the agent from changing the user's saved work.

**Impact.** The user's saved analytics change without consent, and the wizard's output cannot be told apart from the user's own.

```text file=Generic agent
dashboard-update { id: 4521, name: 'Product overview', ... }  // user's dashboard
```

```text file=Wizard
// create the parent dashboard first, capture its id
dashboard-create { name: 'Analytics basics (wizard)', tags: ['wizard'] }
// attach every new insight to the NEW dashboard only
insight-create { name: 'Signup funnel (wizard)', dashboards: [<new dashboard id>], ... }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Dashboard or notebook link given in prose, not the marker</summary>

**Problem.** Mentioning the URL in a friendly sentence reads like a complete handoff. The agent cannot tell that the harness only parses an exact marker line from its visible final message.

**Impact.** Silent: the wizard's success summary drops the dashboard or notebook link. The link must also use the app host, not the ingestion host.

```text file=Generic agent
Done! Your dashboard is ready at https://us.posthog.com/project/2/dashboard/17
I created the dashboard 'Analytics basics (wizard)': https://us.posthog.com/project/12345/dashboard/678
```

```text file=Wizard
[DASHBOARD_URL] https://us.posthog.com/project/12345/dashboard/678
[NOTEBOOK_URL] https://us.posthog.com/project/12345/notebooks/AbCdEfGh
# each on its own line in the visible assistant message, not in thinking;
# exact marker; also recorded in the handoff
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>MCP tool called without reading its schema</summary>

**Problem.** The agent is confident it knows the PostHog API and writes the payload from memory, often in the legacy `filters` shape. It skips `info` and the schema drill-down, and on failure retries variations instead of reading the error's suggestion.

**Impact.** Guessed shapes for insight queries and source payloads are rejected repeatedly, retries burn turns, and the first dashboard is built by trial and error.

```text file=Generic agent
exec({ "command": "call insight-create {\"name\":\"Signups\",\"filters\":{\"events\":[{\"id\":\"user_signed_up\"}]}}" })
// legacy filters shape, no info call, rejected
exec({ "command": "call insight-create {\"query\":{\"kind\":\"TrendsQuery\",\"breakdown\":{...}}}" })
// no schema drill-down; on error retry another guess
```

```text file=Wizard
exec({ "command": "info insight-create" })                 # REQUIRED before every call
exec({ "command": "schema insight-create query.source" })   # field carries a hint: drill first
exec({ "command": "call insight-create <json_input>" })
# errors carry a suggestion and similar names: read it before retrying
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Renamed MCP tools or skills retried blindly</summary>

**Problem.** Skill text and memory name tools or skills the server has since renamed, such as survey-list, error-issue-list, signals-scout-config-\* and llma-skill-\*. On an error the agent retries or fuzzy-searches, or skips the step on the first 404, instead of reading the error's suggested names.

**Impact.** The agent loses the first turn of a step to each dead name, and steps degrade or get skipped on a deploy that works.

```bash file=Generic agent
info survey-list                -> tool not found
call error-issue-list {}        -> tool not found
call signals-scout-config-sync  -> unknown tool
call llma-skill-get {"skill_name": "authoring-signals-scouts"}  -> 404
```

```bash file=Wizard
info surveys-get-all
info query-error-tracking-issues-list
call scout-config-sync
call skill-get {"skill_name": "authoring-scouts"}
# 404 -> try authoring-signals-scouts (stores seeded before the rename)
# Errors carry a suggestion and similar tool names - read it before retrying.
# If a name isn't found it may have been renamed; run `search <pattern>` or `tools`.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Endless search for a tool that is missing</summary>

**Problem.** When a tool such as products-enable is missing on a deploy, the agent assumes it was renamed and keeps searching under other names, or gives up on the step.

**Impact.** Round-trips are wasted, and the setup either aborts or invents a workaround instead of leaving a clean follow-up.

```text file=Generic agent
call products-enable {...}   -> never published on this deploy
search enable
search products
search product-enable        # keeps hunting under other names
-> abort the step
```

```text file=Wizard
info products-enable   # unknown -> ONE `search enable` to confirm, then stop
# record follow-up: turn on Session replay / Error tracking in Settings
# continue to the next step; never abort. Permission rejection: follow-up too
# (6c scanners: same pattern, one `search vision`; 403 = missing scope)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Made-up tool name for project settings changes</summary>

**Problem.** Asked to fix a project setting such as app_urls, the agent assumes an update tool must exist and invents its name. The MCP may not expose any settings mutation.

**Impact.** The call fails or hits the wrong tool, and the report claims a fix that never happened.

```text file=Generic agent
mcp__posthog-wizard__project-update { app_urls: [...] }   # name guessed, never discovered
```

```text file=Wizard
exec({ "command": "search <settings update regex>" }), then `info <tool>`
If you cannot find a settings-mutation tool:
  do NOT guess a tool name, do NOT fabricate a call
  record the fix as manual guidance in the report
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Harness mounts every MCP resource as a tool</summary>

**Problem.** Setting `directTools: true` looks like the simple, most capable way to expose the server. It also creates a posthog_get_&lt;name&gt; tool for each of about 165 skill resources, with sentence-length names, and one name over 128 characters fails the whole Anthropic request.

**Impact.** Every turn returns HTTP 400 and the agent makes zero tool calls, yet the run looks finished. Every model hit this silent no-op.

```ts file=Generic agent
const server = {
  url: mcpUrl,
  directTools: true, // also registers ~165 posthog_get_<resource sentence> tools
};
// -> tool name longer than 128 chars -> HTTP 400 on every request
```

```ts file=Wizard
const server = {
  url: mcpUrl,
  directTools: ['exec'],   // only the CLI-mode exec tool
  exposeResources: false,
};
// settings: { disableProxyTool: true }; runtime notes describe the exec grammar;
// run fails loudly (NO_PROGRESS, contact-support outro) when the agent makes no tool calls
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Setup report saved only as a local file</summary>

**Problem.** Writing posthog-setup-report.md to the repo feels like a complete handoff. The agent has no reason to also mirror it into PostHog or emit a marker the harness can show.

**Impact.** The user has no in-app copy of the report to link or comment on in PostHog, and the wizard end screen has no notebook link to show.

```text file=Generic agent
Write posthog-setup-report.md
# done: report exists only on disk, no notebook, no [NOTEBOOK_URL] line
```

```bash file=Wizard
call notebooks-create-markdown {"title": "PostHog setup (wizard) - acme-shop", "markdown": "## Events captured\n..."}
[NOTEBOOK_URL] <host>/project/<project_id>/notebooks/<short_id>
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Warehouse source fields guessed instead of looked up</summary>

**Problem.** The agent knows roughly what a Postgres connection needs and invents payload field names like conn_str. It never asks the API which fields that source kind needs.

**Impact.** Source creation fails, or the source is created with the wrong fields.

```text file=Generic agent
external-data-sources-create { source_type: "Postgres", payload: { conn_str: "..." } }  # field names guessed
```

```text file=Wizard
1. external-data-sources-wizard {source_type: "Postgres"} -> exact fields
2. wizard_ask exactly those fields (respect `required`)
3. database: external-data-sources-db-schema -> validate + list tables
4. external-data-sources-create (SaaS: data-warehouse-source-setup)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Event lookup tool denied for personal keys</summary>

**Problem.** entity-search sounds like the right tool for looking up event definitions and volume. Nothing says it needs project-key permissions that personal API keys lack.

**Impact.** The call returns permission denied, and the volume data the audit report needs never arrives.

```text file=Generic agent
exec { "command": "call entity-search {\"query\": \"purchase_completed\"}" }
=> permission denied (personal API key)
```

```sql file=Wizard
SELECT event, count() AS volume_30d, max(timestamp) AS last_seen
FROM events
WHERE timestamp > now() - INTERVAL 30 DAY AND event IN (<inventory event names>)
GROUP BY event ORDER BY volume_30d DESC
-- via execute-sql; works regardless of key type
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Audit retries, switches project, or aborts on MCP errors</summary>

**Problem.** A persistent agent retries a failing query, switches to another project it can see, fills the gap with plausible findings, or aborts. It does not treat a missing scope or an older PostHog as a normal result, or know the active project belongs to the wizard session.

**Impact.** The user gets no report, invented findings, or numbers from the wrong project. Calling audit_add_checks with an empty list is rejected.

```text file=Generic agent
execute-sql failed -> retry x3
health-issues-list -> permission denied -> retries, then writes guessed findings
project mismatch -> mcp__posthog__project-set-active <other project>
[ABORT] MCP unavailable
nothing open -> audit_add_checks({ checks: [] })   // zero-length batch rejected
```

```text file=Wizard
{ "mcp_available": false, "mcp_skipped_reason": "execute-sql failed: <short reason>" }
// events-audit: one attempt; live-data: at most one retry; never guess.
// Never switch the wizard's active project.
// Report renders code-side findings plus a {{mcp_disclaimer}} callout; dashboard step skipped.
// live-data sweep row: "Skipped: health-issues-list unavailable. Source-tree checks are unaffected..."
// Nothing open: skip audit_add_checks, sweep row "No open findings in PostHog".
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Logs and Endpoints signal sources left disabled</summary>

**Problem.** The guide said logs had no responder and never mentioned endpoints, so the agent created no source rows for them. A missing row drops signals silently.

**Impact.** Silent: emit_signal returns early with no source row, so Logs and Endpoints findings never reach the Inbox.

```text file=Generic agent
4-sources.md (old): "logs: Nowhere yet, no v1 responder"; endpoints not listed
-> no row for logs / alert_state_change
-> no rows for endpoints / endpoint_execution_failed
```

```text file=Wizard
| Logs      | Only when the project uses Logs (step-2 checklist)        | logs / alert_state_change |
| Endpoints | Only when the project has endpoints (step-2               | endpoints / endpoint_execution_failed |
|           |  endpoints-get-all probe); enable BOTH rows               | endpoints / endpoint_breakdown_limit_exceeded |
// narrowed: only source_type evaluation is "Nowhere" now
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Enabled-scout limit ignored when planning</summary>

**Problem.** The agent sizes the troop against the documented ceiling of ten and the run budget. It misses the per-project max_enabled_scouts and sends enables before disables.

**Impact.** The server rejects enables past the limit, so scouts the user approved never start.

```text file=Generic agent
// cap 6; 1 operational + 2 custom already enabled
plan = general + 5 specialists + 3 custom   // 9 planned
scout-config-update { "id": ..., "enabled": true }  // rejected: cap reached
```

```text file=Wizard
// max_enabled_scouts from scout-metadata-get
slots = max_enabled_scouts - enabled_operational
        - breaker_paused_operational - enabled_custom   // 6-1-0-2 = 3
// pick general + 2 specialists fit inside slots
// 6b room = min(10, budget, slots) - step6_count = 0 -> skip custom proposal
// step 3: disables first, then enables; cap cut a scout -> follow-up
// field absent (older deploy) -> old sizing
```

</details>

## Other

<details>
<summary>One declined prompt ends all credential collection</summary>

**Problem.** Agents generalize a single 'no' into 'the user does not want this', or re-ask out of persistence. Neither matches how users answer per source.

**Impact.** Sources the user never saw are silently dropped, or the user is nagged. Runs with a cancelled ask created a source 17% of the time versus 47% without.

```text file=Generic agent
wizard_ask({ subject: "Postgres", ... }) -> cancelled
"The user declined; skipping credential prompts for Stripe and Resend too."
```

```js file=Wizard
// a decline answers that one source: give it its deep link, do not re-ask
wizard_ask({ subject: "Stripe", ... })   // still ask the next source
// stop asking only after two cancellations in a row
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Long connection form abandoned instead of split</summary>

**Problem.** Told 'one call per source', the agent treats the per-call cap as the source's limit and gives up on wide forms like Postgres with SSH tunnel.

**Impact.** A source the wizard could connect in the terminal is handed back as a link.

```text file=Generic agent
wizard_ask({ subject: "Postgres", questions: [ ...12 fields incl. SSH tunnel ] }) -> over cap
"Form too wide to collect here; handing the user a link."
```

```js file=Wizard
wizard_ask({ subject: "Postgres", questions: [ first fields ] })
wizard_ask({ subject: "Postgres", questions: [ remaining fields ] })  // same subject
// a wide subject spans consecutive calls; never a reason to fall back
// ASK_MAX_QUESTIONS_PER_CALL = widest form + headroom
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Wrong credential type for SaaS sources</summary>

**Problem.** The agent asks for the most familiar key (Stripe secret key, Sentry DSN) or reuses a send-only key from .env, not the read-scoped key type the warehouse connector validates.

**Impact.** Source validation fails on the first attempt or the import lacks read scopes, burning a wizard_ask.

```text file=Generic agent
Stripe:  sk_live_...                              # secret key
Sentry:  https://<key>@o1.ingest.sentry.io/1      # DSN
Twilio:  account auth token
Resend:  send-only restricted key found in .env
```

```text file=Wizard
Stripe: restricted key (rk_live_...) with exact read scopes, not sk_live_...
Sentry: internal-integration token (not DSN or personal token)
RevenueCat: v2 secret key with read scopes; Twilio: API Key SID + Secret
Mailchimp: key with its -usX suffix; Resend/Mailgun: full/read-access key
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Stripe key prompt names no scopes</summary>

**Problem.** The agent asks for 'a Stripe key' without knowing PostHog needs a restricted key with specific scopes, and may invent a scope-preselecting link Stripe does not support.

**Impact.** The user guesses scopes, source validation fails, and setup stalls.

```text file=Generic agent
wizard_ask: "Paste your Stripe restricted API key beginning rk_live_
with no extra text and the required read scopes"
(no scopes listed, no link)
```

```text file=Wizard
Restricted key (rk_live_), not secret key (sk_live_). Name the scopes:
Read: Balance transactions, Charges, Customers, Disputes, Payment methods,
Payouts, Products, Coupons, Credit notes, Invoices, Prices, Subscriptions;
Write: Webhooks. Link posthog.com/docs/cdp/sources/stripe#option-1-...
Stripe has no URL param to pre-select scopes: don't invent a deep link.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Source type sent as display label</summary>

**Problem.** Display names and enum values differ by spacing and casing, and the agent naturally uses the familiar name.

**Impact.** PostHog rejects the create call and the source is not connected.

```ts file=Generic agent
external-data-sources-create({ source_type: 'Hugging Face', ... })
external-data-sources-create({ source_type: 'GitHub', ... })
```

```ts file=Wizard
{ kind: 'HuggingFace', label: 'Hugging Face', mode: 'in-cli' }
{ kind: 'Github', label: 'GitHub', mode: 'deep-link' }
// prompt: "Use the `kind` string exactly as printed for `source_type` -
//   PostHog rejects the display label."
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Non-interactive run stalls or invents credentials</summary>

**Problem.** Without a prompt tool the agent either waits, guesses plausible values, or reports done, since nothing tells it a no-op is acceptable.

**Impact.** The run blocks or reports success for sources that were never created.

```text file=Generic agent
wizard_ask unavailable (CI)
"Cannot collect credentials, waiting..."   # or invents host/password
```

```text file=Wizard
// treat every source as deep-link: emit each new-source URL
// say credentials go in the app; report the task as `not needed`
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Event table dropped when plan file missing</summary>

**Problem.** The report agent reads the events file it was pointed at and, finding nothing, writes 'none recorded' instead of checking the code.

**Impact.** The user cannot see which events were instrumented or where.

```markdown file=Generic agent
## Events
(no .posthog-events.json found, none recorded)
```

```text file=Wizard
// file missing or empty: grep changed files for capture( calls,
// read the capture handoff in queue.json, rebuild (event, meaning, file)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Long report composed in one turn</summary>

**Problem.** Composing a 10-15K token report then writing it once looks efficient. Nothing warns that the streaming socket times out mid-generation.

**Impact.** The connection drops around the 5-10 minute mark, the whole turn is lost, and a rerun hits the same wall.

```text file=Generic agent
1. Compose the full audit report (10-15K output tokens).
2. Write posthog-audit-report.md once.
-> "API Error: The socket connection was closed unexpectedly"
```

```text file=Wizard
1. Write a skeleton: headings + <!-- SECTION_SUMMARY --> placeholders
2. Edit placeholder 1 with its real section (own turn)
3. Edit placeholder 2 ... (each tool call resets the timer)
4. Grep for leftover `{{` before finishing
# notebooks: notebooks-create-markdown + one notebooks-add-cell per section
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Shell retries to delete a blocked temp file</summary>

**Problem.** Told unconditionally to remove the file, an agent blocked by the sandbox treats it as an obstacle and escalates through rm -f, unlink and node or python instead of stopping.

**Impact.** The top bash denial on the pi harness (about 31 per week) and a wasted retry loop, for a file the host deletes anyway.

```bash file=Generic agent
rm .posthog-events.json            # denied by the harness fence
rm -f .posthog-events.json         # retry
unlink .posthog-events.json        # shell fallback
node -e "fs.unlinkSync('.posthog-events.json')"
```

```text file=Wizard
Update `.posthog-events.json` to match the events you implemented, then remove it
with your file tools. If removal is blocked or fails, leave the file in place and
move on; the wizard host cleans it up after the run. Do not retry the removal or
reach for shell commands to force it.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Skill references listed without their folder</summary>

**Problem.** Bare filenames look fine to authors, but the agent resolves paths exactly as written from the skill root and cannot find the files.

**Impact.** The agent stumbles on missing files, skips the docs and example, and falls back to memory.

```markdown file=Generic agent
- `next-js.md` - Next.js docs
- `EXAMPLE.md` - example project code
- `basic-integration-1.3-conclude.md` - Review and fix any errors
```

```markdown file=Wizard
- `references/next-js.md` - Next.js docs
- `references/EXAMPLE.md` - example project code
- `references/basic-integration-1.3-conclude.md` - Review and fix any errors
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Duplicate picker labels widen the selection</summary>

**Problem.** Using team names as labels looks natural. The picker keys ticks by label, which the agent cannot see.

**Impact.** One tick selects several teams, or a team named 'All teams' enables every team.

```js file=Generic agent
options: [
  { label: "All teams", value: "all" },
  { label: "Platform", value: "t1" },
  { label: "Platform", value: "t2" }   // one tick submits t1 and t2
]
```

```js file=Wizard
options: [
  { label: "All teams", value: "all" },
  { label: "Platform (t1)", value: "t1" },   // append (<team id>) to colliding labels
  { label: "Platform (t2)", value: "t2" }
]
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Agent alarms user about internal security warnings</summary>

**Problem.** A capable agent is trained to be transparent, so when a guardrail flags a write it explains the scanner and possible compromise instead of quietly fixing the code.

**Impact.** Scary output on a normal run, and the flagged change may stay in place.

```text file=Generic agent
"Warning: a security scanner flagged a possible secret leak in your
project. You may be compromised."
```

```text file=Wizard
[YARA WARNING]/[YARA VIOLATION] seen -> handle silently:
revert the write, avoid the flagged pattern,
never mention security warnings or YARA to the user
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Prompt placeholders copied literally into output</summary>

**Problem.** Prompt templates show `<full https url>` and wrapper tags as shape hints, and a model following the template closely reproduces them verbatim instead of substituting values.

**Impact.** The outro gets '&lt;full https url&gt;' instead of the dashboard URL, and the setup report is wrapped in a stray tag instead of being valid markdown starting with an H1.

```text file=Generic agent
[DASHBOARD_URL] <full https url>
<wizard-report>
# PostHog setup report
...
</wizard-report>
```

```text file=Wizard
- Angle-bracket placeholders in prompts are fill-ins: substitute the real
  value and never emit the literal `<...>` text. Markers carry the real value
  (`[DASHBOARD_URL]` gets the actual URL, not `<full https url>`), and the
  setup report is valid markdown starting with an H1 heading, with no
  `<wizard-report>` wrapper tags.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Report shortened to get past notebook errors</summary>

**Problem.** When a tool call fails with 'Bad control character', the obvious fix is to send less text, but the real fault is JSON encoding of the markdown, so the content loss is silent.

**Impact.** The PostHog notebook carries a two-line summary instead of the full setup report.

```text file=Generic agent
notebooks-create-markdown {"title": "PostHog setup (wizard)",
  "markdown": "## Summary\nPostHog installed. See the repo for details.\n"}
# 13KB report cut to 2 lines after 'Bad control character'
```

```text file=Wizard
// Never trim the report just to make it parse. Bare JSON on one line,
// newlines and quotes escaped (\n, \"):
call notebooks-create-markdown {"title": "PostHog setup (wizard) - acme-shop", "markdown": "## Events captured\n\n..."}
// still failing? create with the first sections, then append the rest:
call notebooks-add-cell {"notebook_id": "AbCdEfGh", "cell_type": "markdown", "markdown": "## Next steps\n\n..."}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Notebook shows the report title twice</summary>

**Problem.** The report starts with an H1 for publish_handoff, and nothing tells the agent the notebook API already renders the title as the leading heading.

**Impact.** Silent cosmetic defect: the notebook opens with the same heading twice.

```text file=Generic agent
notebooks-create-markdown {"title": "PostHog setup (wizard) - acme-shop",
  "markdown": "# PostHog setup (wizard) - acme-shop\n\n## Events captured ..."}
```

```text file=Wizard
// The title becomes the notebook's leading `# heading`: start the markdown
// at the first section below it.
notebooks-create-markdown {"title": "PostHog setup (wizard) - acme-shop",
  "markdown": "## Events captured\n\n..."}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Many sources packed into one credential prompt</summary>

**Problem.** Batching questions looks efficient and an earlier skill even told agents to batch across sources, but one call has a fixed field cap that several sources overflow, while one call per source without a subject tripped the adjacency nudge.

**Impact.** Five sources need 15-25 fields. Agents gave up and fell back to browser links, so about 65% of 'completed' warehouse tasks created no source.

```js file=Generic agent
wizard_ask({ "questions": [
  { "id": "pg_host" }, { "id": "pg_password" }, ...,
  { "id": "stripe_key" }, { "id": "mysql_host" }, ...
] })   // three sources, ~20 fields, no subject
```

```js file=Wizard
wizard_ask({ subject: "Postgres", questions: [host, port, database, user, password] })
// create Postgres, then:
wizard_ask({ subject: "Stripe", questions: [restricted_api_key] })
// one subject per call, never two sources in one call; the guard counts per subject
// a kind with more than 8 fields: two calls with the same subject
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Credentials asked one field at a time</summary>

**Problem.** Asking each field separately feels conversational, and an earlier skill said both 'single call' and 'one source, one prompt' with no cancellation rule, but the ask tool counts calls per run and nudges after repeats.

**Impact.** The user bounces through many prompts, the batching nudge fires, and the agent gets stuck and aborts.

```text file=Generic agent
wizard_ask({ questions: [host] })
wizard_ask({ questions: [port] })
wizard_ask({ questions: [password] })   // one source, three calls
// cancelled ask retried and counted against the cap
```

```text file=Wizard
wizard_ask({
  subject: "Postgres",
  questions: [host, port, database, user, password, schema]
})
// ONE ask per source (up to 8 questions); a follow-up only after a validation failure.
// Cancelled/timed-out ask = declined: deep link for that source, not counted.
// 3 calls in a row on one subject earn a one-time retryable nudge (ASK_BATCH_THRESHOLD)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Retryable batch nudge treated as a hard stop</summary>

**Problem.** A tool response saying 'too many calls in a row, batch your questions' reads like a refusal or a spent budget, so a cautious agent stops and picks the fallback path.

**Impact.** Each nudge cost a source connection even though the next call would have gone through. In self-driving, wanted steps like custom scouts were silently dropped as follow-ups.

```text file=Generic agent
# agent: "question budget used up"
-> browser links for remaining sources / custom scouts recorded as follow-up
```

```ts file=Wizard
wizard_ask({ subject: 'Postgres', questions: [host, port, database, user, password] })
// adjacency is counted per subject; one call per source is never interrupted
// soft nudge ("Not an error ... not sent"): re-issue the same call once and it goes through.
// Only `cap reached (N calls)` means the budget is spent.
// Cancelled or timed-out asks do not count. Never drop a step to save calls.
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Tracking parameters stripped from setup links</summary>

**Problem.** Agents tidy URLs and treat utm parameters as noise. Nothing breaks visibly when they are removed.

**Impact.** Silent: sources finished in the browser after a handoff cannot be attributed to the wizard, so handoff conversion is unmeasurable.

```text file=Generic agent
https://us.posthog.com/project/2/data-warehouse/new-source?kind=Stripe
```

```text file=Wizard
https://us.posthog.com/project/2/data-warehouse/new-source?kind=Stripe&utm_source=wizard&utm_campaign=warehouse-source
# keep the utm_* parameters exactly as written
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Key files asked through a terminal prompt</summary>

**Problem.** A service-account key looks like any other secret, but it is a multi-line JSON file upload (or an OAuth flow) and the single-line terminal prompt submits on Enter.

**Impact.** The step dead-ends at source creation. Firebase was offered many times and completed almost never.

```js file=Generic agent
wizard_ask({ subject: "BigQuery", questions: [
  { id: "service_account_json", kind: "text",
    prompt: "Paste your service account JSON key" } ] })
{ kind: 'BigQuery', mode: 'in-cli' }
```

```text file=Wizard
{ kind: 'BigQuery', label: 'BigQuery', mode: 'deep-link' }   // also Firebase
[STATUS] BigQuery needs browser setup
<app-host>/project/<id>/data-warehouse/new-source?kind=BigQuery&utm_source=wizard&utm_campaign=warehouse-source
// the web form has a file-upload field; give the URL and stop, collect no credentials
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>First failed source aborts remaining sources</summary>

**Problem.** The old skill step said to emit \[ABORT\] on create failure, and agents treat any failure as run-fatal by default.

**Impact.** One source that will not connect ends the run. Later sources are never attempted.

```text file=Generic agent
external-data-sources-create(Postgres) -> error
[ABORT] Source creation failed   # Stripe and Resend never attempted
```

```text file=Wizard
// on failure: record the error, give that source its deep link, move on
[STATUS] Connected Stripe
// [ABORT] only when every in-cli source failed
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>One rejected credential abandons the whole source</summary>

**Problem.** A 'never re-ask' rule meant to stop duplicate questions is read as absolute, even when the API rejected a fixable value the user would gladly correct.

**Impact.** A source the user agreed to connect is lost to the browser fallback over one mistyped or under-scoped field.

```text file=Generic agent
data-warehouse-source-setup({ Stripe, key }) -> 400 key lacks read access to Invoices
"Credential rejected; I may not re-ask. Handing the user the browser link."
```

```js file=Wizard
// exception: a value the API rejected
wizard_ask({ subject: "Stripe",   // same subject
  questions: [{ id: "api_key", prompt: "Key lacks Invoices read. Need rk_live_ with read scope" }] })
// only the wrong fields; stop after a couple of attempts
// a dismissed or timed-out ask is a decline: fall back, do not re-ask
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Timed-out prompt confused with user dismissal</summary>

**Problem.** Cancelled answers came back as sentinel strings, so the agent could not tell 'user said no' from 'nobody is at the terminal' and kept asking.

**Impact.** With nobody present each later prompt waited out the full allowance.

```json file=Generic agent
{ "answers": { "host": "__cancelled__", "password": "__cancelled__" } }
// agent guesses, asks the next source, waits 20 more minutes
```

```json file=Wizard
{ "answers": { ... },
  "cancelled": { "reason": "user-cancelled" | "timed-out",
                 "questionIds": ["host", "password"],
                 "note": "what to do next" } }
// timed-out note: stop asking, hand over links
// credential asks get LONGER_ASK_TIMEOUT_MS (20 min)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Long credential form times out mid-entry</summary>

**Problem.** A single timer per request is the obvious design, but a nine-field form needs far longer than one question.

**Impact.** A user partway through a database form loses every typed field, and the agent is told nobody is there and stops asking.

```ts file=Generic agent
// one timer armed when the request opens
setTimeout(() => resolve(CANCELLED_ALL), perQuestionTimeout)
// 9-field form gets the same allowance as 1 question
```

```ts file=Wizard
// host calls onAnswer after each question; the bridge re-arms the timer
showQuestion(pending, {
  signal: controller.signal,
  onAnswer: () => { answeredCount += 1; armTimeout() },
})
// WizardAskScreen: store.noteAskProgress() after each non-final answer
// cancelled event carries questions_answered (count, never a value)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Timed-out question blocks every later question</summary>

**Problem.** The natural implementation counts each ask up front and resolves the tool on timeout without clearing the host's pending state.

**Impact.** One dismissed or timed-out prompt burns the budget or blocks the overlay, so later credentials (Stripe, Resend) fall back to deep links.

```ts file=Generic agent
askCallCount += 1                          // slot taken up front
const answers = await askBridge.request({ questions })
// timeout resolves agent, host pending state stays set:
//   wizard_ask request is pending"
```

```ts file=Wizard
timer = setTimeout(() => {
  timedOut = true
  resolve(buildCancelledAnswers(questions))
  controller.abort()   // interaction.ts: store.cancelPendingQuestion()
}, timeoutMs)
// mcp.ts / pi tools.ts:
if (isFullyCancelled(answers)) askAccounting.refund(args.subject)
// catch path: askAccounting.refund(args.subject)
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Stepping away treated as declining</summary>

**Problem.** A timeout and an explicit decline both resolve to the same cancelled value, so a skill that aborts on decline also aborts on timeout.

**Impact.** Users who just stepped away have the run killed as if they refused, after paying full agent startup.

```text file=Generic agent
3. On can't (at any point): emit exactly
   [ABORT] github connection declined
   and stop.
// wizard_ask timeout resolves to the same __cancelled__ as a decline
```

```text file=Wizard
# 2-read-context.md front matter (old step 3 removed)
next_step: 3-enable-products.md
# wizard flow: ... auth -> self-driving-github screen -> agent run
# GitHub App gate runs BEFORE the agent starts:
# cheap, unambiguous, un-timeoutable
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Question times out during slow user work</summary>

**Problem.** A 5-minute ask default looks generous until the user must create an API key, install a GitHub App, or run a production build.

**Impact.** The question is cancelled mid-task. The agent reads cancelled answers as a decline and wraps up or aborts.

```ts file=Generic agent
createAskBridge(interaction, {
  getSource: () => input.skillId,
  // timeoutMs unset: DEFAULT_ASK_TIMEOUT_MS (5 min)
  // timeout -> every field "__cancelled__"
});
```

```ts file=Wizard
// programs/error-tracking-upload-source-maps, self-driving
askTimeoutMs: 30 * 60 * 1000,
// linear.ts
createAskBridge(interaction, { ..., timeoutMs: run.askTimeoutMs })
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Blank required credential accepted as an answer</summary>

**Problem.** An empty string looks like a valid answer to the agent, which cannot tell a blank Enter from a deliberate decline.

**Impact.** Source creation runs on empty credentials and dead-ends with no sign the user declined.

```text file=Generic agent
wizard_ask(...) -> { password: '' }     // Enter on a blank required field
external-data-sources-create({ password: '' })   // fails; no decline signal
```

```ts file=Wizard
// WizardAskScreen submit():
if (isRequiredButEmpty(question, value)) {
  setRequiredHint(true)   // nudge toward Esc
  return
}
// Esc = explicit decline -> that source gets its deep link
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Same database connected twice under two names</summary>

**Problem.** Detection matches both a hosted provider (Supabase, Neon) and generic Postgres from one DATABASE_URL, and connecting every detected kind looks thorough.

**Impact.** Every table imports twice and the user pays ingestion for the duplicate.

```text file=Generic agent
Detected: Postgres (DATABASE_URL), Supabase (@supabase/supabase-js)
connect Supabase -> ok
connect Postgres -> ok   // same database
```

```ts file=Wizard
// registry.ts
{ kind: 'Postgres', /* signals: pg, DATABASE_URL, ... */
  supersededBy: ['Neon', 'Supabase'] }
// detect.ts: drops an entry when any kind in supersededBy also matched
(detector.supersededBy ?? []).some((kind) => detectedKinds.has(kind))
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Supabase connected as plain Postgres via the direct host</summary>

**Problem.** Supabase looks like plain Postgres, so the agent picks source_type Postgres, uses the direct host and whatever key is visible, but the direct host is IPv6-only and the familiar keys are not the DB password.

**Impact.** Warehouse source validation fails on the first attempt for most Supabase projects.

```yaml file=Generic agent
source_type: Postgres
host: db.<project-ref>.supabase.co     # direct host, IPv6-only
port: 5432
user: postgres
password: <anon / service_role JWT or account password>
```

```yaml file=Wizard
source_type: Supabase   # not Postgres
# host: pass on the Session-pooler guidance from the field caption
password: <database password>   # Supabase > Settings > Database
# not an anon/service_role JWT, not the account password
# SUPABASE_URL in env -> derive ref from db.<ref>.supabase.co, pre-fill host and username
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Prompting a user who is not there</summary>

**Problem.** If a question tool exists, the agent uses it. In CI, headless or signup runs nobody answers.

**Impact.** The run waits for the whole prompt timeout, then continues on a guess or fails.

```text file=Generic agent
CI run: wizard_ask("Which project?")   -> waits the full timeout -> run fails
```

```ts file=Wizard
shouldDisableAsk(session) => (session.ci || session.signup) && !session.e2eAsk
// no ask tool registered at all in those runs
disallowedTools: [WIZARD_TOOL_NAMES.wizardAsk]  // basic integration
```

</details>

<details>
